# 11 Minds Population — Product Blueprint

> **Purpose of this document.** This is a complete, self-contained reference to the *11 Minds Population* web application. It is written so that any reader — human or LLM — who has never seen the codebase can understand **what the product is, who it is for, how every part works, how the pieces fit together, how it is built and deployed, and what its known limitations are.** It covers product concept, use cases, architecture, the domain model, the agent-intelligence system, the simulation engine, the knowledge graph, ingestion, reporting, the full API/event surface, the frontend, the tech stack, deployment, and a candid list of gotchas.

Repository: `11-minds-army` (the product is branded **"11 Minds Population"**; "11 Minds Army" survives as the internal FastAPI app title). Backend = FastAPI (Python). Frontend = Next.js 14 (TypeScript). Deployed on Railway.

## 1. What it is (in one minute)

**11 Minds Population is a multi-agent simulation platform — a synthetic focus group, war-game, and prediction engine in one.**

You pose a question (e.g. *"Would Gen Z adopt a subscription model for home-cooked meal kits?"* or *"How will retail investors react to a Fed rate cut?"*). You feed it source material — pasted text, uploaded documents, a YouTube video, or an AI-generated research paper. The system then:

1. **Builds a knowledge graph** from your sources (entities + relations extracted by Claude).
2. **Spawns a "population"** of diverse AI personas — anywhere from a handful up to **1,000 agents** — each with a distinct background, role, debate style, and a **112-dimensional psychological profile** ("dials"). Two **modes** trade speed for depth: **Fast** samples a pre-built agent bank instantly (no LLM at spawn); **Pro** curates each persona with Claude Sonnet.
3. **Runs a live, Reddit-style debate** where these agents comment, react, challenge, and reply, grounded in the knowledge graph. The debate is driven by an **activity intensity** (every agent is guaranteed at least one post; higher levels add a reaction, then a debate, then a reply — per agent). Their discussion feeds *back* into the graph.
4. **Distills the debate** into (a) a structured executive **report** with a direct answer, confidence level, and KPI grid; and (b) a synthetic **market-research dashboard** that scores the population on adoption readiness, purchase intent, willingness-to-pay, virality, retention, churn risk, and more.

Everything streams live to the browser over a WebSocket: agents pop in as they're created, posts appear as they're written, and the knowledge graph grows node-by-node in real time.

---

## 2. Who it's for & use cases

The product targets **product managers, strategists, market analysts, investors, researchers, and founders** who want fast, structured, multi-perspective reasoning about a decision *before* committing real time or money to a real study.

The app is organized around **four canonical use-case categories** (surfaced as cards on the landing page and as the LLM-search taxonomy):

| Category | Internal key | Question it answers | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Product Testing & User Adoption** | `product_testing` | *Will real users adopt this?* | "Will busy parents pay for a meal-kit subscription?" |
| **Market & Stock Signals** | `market_signals` | *How will a market/asset react?* | "How will $NVDA react to the next earnings call?" |
| **Behavioural Prediction** | `behavioural_prediction` | *How will a population behave?* | "How will commuters respond to congestion pricing?" |
| **Strategy Stress-Testing** | `strategy_stress_test` | *Where does this strategy break?* | "What are the failure modes of our go-to-market plan?" (also the default fallback category) |

The unifying value proposition: **diverse minds, distinct reasoning, one simulation** — get the spread of opinion, the dissent, and the contradictions of a real panel, in minutes, grounded in your own material.

---

## 3. The mental model

Think of the system as a pipeline of five transformations:

```
   QUESTION ──▶ SOURCES ──▶ KNOWLEDGE GRAPH ──▶ POPULATION ──▶ DEBATE ──▶ REPORT + DASHBOARD
   (session)   (ingest)     (entities/relations) (112-dial      (rounds of  (structured
                                                  agents)        posts)      briefing + KPIs)
```

Two ideas make it distinctive:

- **Agents are psychological, not just role-based.** Each agent carries a 112-value "psychological DNA" across 9 dimensions (emotion, motivation, habit, trust, friction, identity, commercial intent, product experience, and composite scores). These dials shape *how* an agent argues (tone, hedging, aggression, deference) — separately from *what* it argues (driven by its role/background).
- **The debate is grounded.** Agents don't hallucinate in a vacuum — they reason over a per-session knowledge graph built from your sources, and their own posts are continuously re-ingested into that graph, so later rounds build on earlier insight.

---

## 4. End-to-end user journey

The session UI is a workspace of numbered steps — **1 Sources → 2 Twins → 3 Debate → 4 Lab → 5 Report**, with **What they know** (the graph) as a side view — in a header that shows the session title, a colour-coded status dot, a tick on every step that is done, a **Next: …** button naming the step that comes next, and the run controls. (Reader-facing names since 2026-09-30; the code, the API and this document keep the internal names Ingest / Agents / Thread / KG.) The typical flow:

1. **Create a session.** From the landing page, give it a *title* and a *query/hypothesis*. Status: `created`.
2. **Ingest sources** (Ingest tab). Add one or more of: pasted **text**, an uploaded **document** (PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, CSV, images via Vision, etc.), a **YouTube** URL (transcript + thumbnail/frame analysis + comments), or an **AI-generated research paper** (LLM Search). Each ingest extracts entities/relations into the knowledge graph; status flips `ingesting` → `ready`. Live `kg_updated` events stream nodes into the Graph tab.
3. **Build the population** (Agents tab). The Agents tab *is* the **Population Studio** (§7.8) until a population exists: detect what the inputs establish, gather statistics, answer what it cannot infer, review the plan of segments, **Approve & build**. A saved **lineup** (preset) can be loaded instead, and a **Build your own agent** button (on the Studio's empty state and on the roster) opens the Agent Builder (§7.10), where the analyst writes one twin by hand and saves it into a lineup. Once agents exist the tab shows the roster with the debate's run settings (Fast / Pro mode, activity intensity) and a **Rebuild in Studio** button — shown before a run and after one completes (the Studio keeps the plan, so segments and dials can be changed and the roster rebuilt at any time); disabled while a debate is running, since rebuilding replaces the agents that are debating. Rebuilding does not delete the previous run's posts. *(The old quick-spawn form — audience profile text, stance sliders, humanity sliders, agent-count slider — was removed on 2026-09-16; `POST /spawn-agents` remains as an API.)*
4. **Run the simulation** (Thread tab). Start the debate. Posts stream in live — threaded, with replies and "debate" rebuttals flagged. A sidebar shows a one-line **verdict** per agent (generated in batches by Claude once every agent has posted, refreshed on completion, persisted on the agent, with a **Refresh/Retry** button and a first-post excerpt as fallback). You can **Pause / Resume / Stop** from the header.
5. **Watch the graph** (Graph tab, any time). An SVG knowledge graph grows in real time with a live activity feed; click any node to inspect its relations and source mentions.
6. **Generate the report** (Report tab). One click produces a structured executive briefing (Direct Answer + Confidence, Question, Source Materials, Discussion, Key Metrics KPI grid, Outcome). You can then **chat** with the report or **talk to individual agents**, **regenerate**, or **Save as PDF**.

A separate full-page route lets you **chat 1:1 with any single agent** (`/session/[id]/agents/[agentId]`).

**The simple view (2026-10-02).** The same sessions, wrapped for a reader who does not want the workspace: a **Simple / Pro** switch on the landing page (remembered per browser) swaps the landing for `/`'s simple home — one question, one *Add anything* box (files, web pages, YouTube links, text), one **Start** — and every session also opens at **`/lite/[id]`** (a **Simple view** button in the session header beside *Next:*, and a **Simple view** pill bottom-left on every other pro page — `components/lite/SimpleViewPill`, mounted in the root layout — both return to the same session's simple screen, the report view when the pro Report tab is open, or to the simple home; the simple header's *Full portal* link goes the other way). There the flow is one screen at a time: **Who should we ask?** (an optional description of the people and an optional survey file, then **Run simulation**) → **Getting everyone ready** (three plain steps) → **the conversation** (the pro thread, unchanged, with *Talk* beside every person) → after it ends, three buttons: **Talk to a person** (a floating chat box; hover a name for who they are), **Make the report** (the pro report component) and **Lab tools** (a placeholder for now). Research, statistics gathering, the clarifying questions, the plan review and the debate settings are all decided for the reader (research on; questions skipped; 40 twins, Fast, intensity 2; the plan approved as proposed and the debate started by the backend the moment the roster is written). Nothing is analysed differently: every element carries a hover pill, **See in detail**, that opens the pro page where it is analysed in full.

---

## 5. System architecture

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              BROWSER (Next.js 14)                              │
│   Landing · Session workspace (5 tabs) · Agent chat page                       │
│   ── REST calls (api.ts → /api/v1/*) ──┐      ┌── WebSocket (/ws/{sessionId})  │
└────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┼───────────────────────────────┘
                                          │      │  (live events)
                                          ▼      ▲
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                          BACKEND (FastAPI, single process)                     │
│                                                                                │
│  API routers (/api/v1):  sessions · ingestion · simulation · agents ·          │
│                          reports · presets        + /health  + /ws/{id}        │
│                                                                                │
│  Services:                                                                     │
│    • agents/      agent_factory (spawn population), agent_runner (write posts), │
│                   profiles, dial_analytics (population dashboard),              │
│                   dynamic_dials (the dials this question needs),                │
│                   validation (does a twin behave like its own record?)          │
│    • simulation/  orchestrator (round loop), thread_manager, report_generator,  │
│                   citations (twin ↔ claim traceability)                         │
│    • ingestion/   text_processor, document_parser, youtube_extractor,           │
│                   llm_search                                                    │
│    • knowledge_graph/  lightrag_service (extract/query), graph_updater          │
│                                                                                │
│  Core:  config (pydantic-settings) · database (async SQLAlchemy) ·              │
│         redis_client (IN-PROCESS pub/sub, NOT Redis)                            │
└───────────┬───────────────────────────┬───────────────────────┬───────────────┘
            │                           │                       │
            ▼                           ▼                       ▼
   ┌─────────────────┐        ┌────────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────┐
   │  Relational DB   │        │  Knowledge-graph    │   │   Anthropic Claude    │
   │  SQLite (local)  │        │  JSON on filesystem │   │   API (all LLM work)  │
   │  or Postgres     │        │  lightrag_data/     │   │   orchestration/agents│
   │  (DATABASE_URL)  │        │  {session}/kg.json  │   │   /fast model tiers   │
   └─────────────────┘        └────────────────────┘   └──────────────────────┘
```

**Key architectural facts:**

- **Two transport channels frontend↔backend:** request/response **REST** under `/api/v1`, plus a **per-session WebSocket** (`/ws/{session_id}`) for live events. The frontend also polls the session every 8s as a resilience fallback.
- **Pub/sub is in-process, not Redis.** The module is named `redis_client.py` for historical reasons but is a pure asyncio-`Queue` fan-out (`backend/app/core/redis_client.py`). Every `publish(...)` enqueues a JSON event to all WebSocket subscribers *on the same process*. **Consequence: the app is single-replica by design** — multiple workers would not share events.
- **All "intelligence" is Claude.** Persona generation, post writing, KG entity extraction, KG querying, report writing, query classification, and image/video Vision analysis are all Anthropic API calls. A valid `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is mandatory; without it the product silently produces empty graphs and no posts (see §16).
- **One persistence store: Postgres on the "11 Minds Population" Supabase project** (ref `pkgfqfdnmikolrbampig`, eu-west-1) via `DATABASE_URL` (session-pooler string), for sessions/agents/posts/reports/presets **and the knowledge graphs** (`kg_graphs` table, one row per session — no more per-session `kg.json` files on the ephemeral disk). Locally, with `DATABASE_URL` unset, the same models run on SQLite (`backend/eleven_minds.db`). Schema is versioned with **Alembic** (`backend/alembic/`), run automatically at startup on Postgres.
- **User authentication is Supabase Auth** on that same project (email + password, sign-up, magic link). The frontend holds the session with `supabase-js`; every REST call carries `Authorization: Bearer <access token>` and the WebSocket passes it as `?token=`. The backend verifies tokens locally against the project's JWKS (ES256) and scopes every session, agent, post, report, preset and graph to its owner (`user_id`). Auth is ON whenever `APP_SUPABASE_URL` is set; unset = dev mode with no login.
- **Background work** (ingestion, agent spawning, the simulation loop) runs in FastAPI `BackgroundTasks` / fired coroutines in the same process, emitting incremental pub/sub events so the UI sees live progress.

---

## 6. Domain model & core concepts

The relational schema has **five tables**. There are **no DB-level foreign keys or ORM relationships** — tables are linked by convention via string `session_id` / `agent_id` / `parent_id` columns (several indexed). All primary keys are `String(36)` UUIDs; all rows have a `created_at`. Schema is created at startup via `Base.metadata.create_all` (no Alembic migrations). Models live in `backend/app/models/`.

### 6.1 `AnalysisSession` (`analysis_sessions`)
Owner column: **`user_id`** (UUID → `auth.users.id`, nullable, indexed). Rows created in dev mode have no owner; with auth on, ownerless rows are invisible to everyone.
The top-level unit of work. Columns: `id`, `title`, `query` (the question/hypothesis), `status` (enum), `agent_count`, `dynamic_dials` (the question's own dials, §7.2a), `created_at`, `updated_at`.

**`SessionStatus` enum** — the lifecycle state machine:

```
created ──(ingest/*)──▶ ingesting ──(done)──▶ ready ──(simulate/start)──▶ simulating
   │                                            ▲   │                          │
   │ spawn-agents / apply-preset force ─────────┘   │ pause                    │ max_rounds done
   │ (also reset agent_count=0)                     ▼                          ▼
   └──────────────────────────────────────────▶ paused ──(resume)─▶ …    complete
                                                                       (error: defined but never set)
```
Values: `created`, `ingesting`, `ready`, `simulating`, `paused`, `complete`, `error`.

### 6.2 `SpawnedAgent` (`spawned_agents`)
One AI persona within a session. Columns: `id`, `session_id` (indexed), `name`, `age` (default 30), `role`, `background` (2–3 sentence bio), `stance` (enum), `correlation` (one-sentence relation to the topic), `personality` (JSON **list** of trait tags), `debate_style`, `energy` (Float, 0.3–1.0 — drives how often it posts/debates), `avatar_color` (hex), **`dials` (JSON dict — the 112-value psychological profile)**, **`humanity`** (Integer 0–100, default 0 — emotional-vs-analytical register set at spawn; see §7.7), **`verdict`** (Text, nullable — the persisted one-line Claude verdict shown in the Agent Opinions sidebar; `NULL` until generated; added via the non-destructive startup migration in `database._ensure_columns`), **`segment`** (String, nullable — the Population Studio plan segment the agent was built from, §7.8) and **`demographics`** (JSON, nullable — `{gender, region, income_band, education, occupation, geo_behavior}`; Studio spawns fix the first five from the segment, Pro spawns carry `{region, geo_behavior}`, Fast spawns leave it `NULL`. `geo_behavior` is a spawn-written 2–3 sentence paragraph analysing the session query from the agent's place — injected verbatim into the persona system prompt, §7.4), **`character`** (JSON, nullable — the hand-authored character from the Agent Builder, §7.10: any of `decision_rules`, `behaviour`, `vocabulary`, `information_diet`, `failure_modes` as free text, injected verbatim into the persona prompt by `agent_runner._character_block`; Alembic `0010_character`, SQLite `_ensure_columns`), `created_at`.

**`AgentStance` enum:** `direct` (domain experts), `indirect` (adjacent-field perspectives), `neutral` (skeptics/press/public).

> The 112 dials live **inline as a JSON column** on the agent — not a separate table. Population-level dial aggregation is computed on the fly (not persisted).

### 6.3 `SimulationPost` (`simulation_posts`)
One contribution to the debate thread. Columns: `id`, `session_id` (indexed), `agent_id` (indexed), `type` (enum), `content` (Text, nullable), `parent_id` (nullable, indexed — self-referential for threading), `likes` (default 0), `round_num`, `created_at`.

**`PostType` enum:** `comment` (top-level), `reply`, `debate` (pointed rebuttal), `like`. Posts are ordered by `created_at`. Threading is purely via `parent_id`.

### 6.4 `ReportQuery` (`report_queries`)
A persisted Q&A log — one row per report question asked against a session. Columns: `id`, `session_id` (indexed), `question`, `answer`, `sources` (nullable), `created_at`.

### 6.5 `AgentPreset` (`agent_presets`)
Also carries **`user_id`** (owner); lists and loads are per user.

### 6.5a `Archetype` (`archetypes`)
A hand-authored twin promoted to a **mould the Population Studio casts personas from** (§7.11, brief L3-02). Columns: `id`, `user_id` (owner; RLS owner policy), `name`, `role`, `profile` (jsonb — the full authored profile as the Agent Builder saved it: background, character, dials, humanity, debate style, personality, stance, demographics), `created_at`. Created when an Agent Builder save has *use as an archetype* ticked (`as_archetype` on `POST /presets/custom` / `POST /presets/{id}/agents`); listed and deleted via `/archetypes`. Alembic `0011_archetypes`. Deleting an archetype does not touch the lineup it came from or the personas already cast from it.

### 6.6 `KnowledgeGraph` (`kg_graphs`) and `profiles`
`kg_graphs`: `session_id` (PK → session, cascade), `entities` (jsonb list), `relations` (jsonb list of `[head, verb, tail]`), `chunks` (jsonb, last 200), `updated_at`. **`ontology`** (jsonb, nullable — the typed ontology built over the entities/relations, §9.5; Alembic `0007_ontology`). `profiles`: `id` (→ `auth.users`), `email`, `display_name`, **`role`** (`admin` | `member`), filled by a trigger on sign-up — **the first account to register becomes `admin`** (the trigger takes a table lock so two simultaneous sign-ups cannot both win); every later account is a `member`. Admins can promote/demote from the Admin page; the last admin cannot be demoted. Every public table has **row-level security** with owner policies (the backend connects as `postgres` and bypasses RLS; the policies protect any direct PostgREST use).
A reusable, **session-independent** named snapshot of a population — the UI calls it a **lineup**. Columns: `id`, `name`, `agent_count`, `agents` (JSON list of full agent-profile dicts — every `SpawnedAgent` field except `id`/`session_id`/`created_at`, including `character`; a hand-authored profile also carries `authored: true`), `created_at`. A lineup is either saved from a session's roster or assembled agent by agent in the Agent Builder (§7.10); hand-authored agents are never stored loose.

### 6.7 `Probe` (`probes`) and `ProbeAnswer` (`probe_answers`)
The Behaviour Lab's storage (§11a). `probes`: `id`, `session_id` (→ session, cascade), `instrument`, `schema_id` (e.g. `purchase_intent.v1`), `spec` (jsonb — stimulus, price, currency, context policy, agent filter, seed), `experiment_id` + `variant_key` (set when the probe is one arm of an A/B; null otherwise), `seed`, `model`, `prompt_hash`, `status` (`queued|running|complete|failed|stopped`), `agent_count`, `answer_count`, `failed_count`, `aggregates` (jsonb — everything the UI draws), `error`, `created_at`, `completed_at`. `probe_answers`: `id`, `probe_id` (cascade), `session_id`, `agent_id`, `answer` (jsonb, the instrument's typed schema), `reasoning` (denormalised for cheap quoting), `latency_ms`, `created_at`. Both carry RLS owner policies via the session. Alembic `0004_measurement`.

### 6.8 `Experiment` (`experiments`)
An A/B/n container (§11a.7). Columns: `id`, `session_id` (→ session, cascade), `name`, `design` (`within|between`), `instrument`, `variants` (jsonb list `[{key, label, spec}]`, first is the control), `spec` (jsonb — shared across arms: agent filter, context policy), `seed`, `model`, `status` (`queued|running|complete|failed|stopped`), `agent_count`, `results` (jsonb — arms, per-metric lift with intervals, flips, segment map, verdict, arm probe ids), `error`, `created_at`, `completed_at`. The arms themselves are ordinary `probes` rows carrying `experiment_id` + `variant_key`, so deleting a session removes both. RLS via the session owner. Alembic `0005_experiments`.

**Relationship summary (all by convention):** `Session 1→N Agents`, `Session 1→N Posts`, `Agent 1→N Posts`, `Post 1→N Posts` (replies via `parent_id`), `Session 1→N ReportQueries`. `AgentPreset` is standalone. **No cascade deletes** — deleting a session orphans its agents/posts/reports.

---

### 6.10 `KnowledgeUnit`, `ScopePolicy`, `Retrieval` (`knowledge_units`, `scope_policies`, `retrievals`)
Scoped retrieval's storage (§9.6; Alembic `0008_scoping`, RLS via the session owner). `knowledge_units`: one row per unit of knowledge — `session_id`, `snapshot_id` (the tagging run), `text`, `source_ref` (the `[SOURCE …]` header or `debate: <name>`), `provenance_class` (the brief's seven classes), `trust_tier`, `facets` (jsonb, GIN-indexed: `{geography[], role[], channel[], condition[], stage[], segment[], register, time?, project}`). `scope_policies`: `session_id`, `layer` (`project | session | run`), `version`, `rules` (jsonb list), `note` — the highest version per layer is active. `retrievals`: `session_id`, `agent_id`, `purpose` (`post | probe | chat`), `snapshot_id`, `policy_version`, `unit_ids`, `routes` — what each twin was actually shown. `spawned_agents` gains **`exposure`** (jsonb, nullable): an override of the twin's derived exposure profile.

### 6.11 `LabBrief` (`lab_briefs`)
The Lab\x27s standing brief for a session (§11a.3, Forms; Alembic `0018_lab_briefs`, RLS via the session owner): one row per session (`session_id` unique) — `status` (building | ready | failed), `fingerprint` (a hash of `inputs`: what was on file when it was written — agent / post / probe / experiment / graph-entity counts, the latest research run, build and report ids), `brief` (JSON: `summary`, `population`, `what_we_know[]`, `tensions[]`, `challenges[{title, why, measure}]`, `already_measured[]`, `vocabulary[]`, `gaps[]`, `sources[]`, `question`), `model`, `error`, `built_at`. `GET /sessions/{id}/lab/brief` adds `stale` (the fingerprint no longer matches the session). Written by `services/measurement/lab_brief.py`; read by every Forms call.

### 6.9 `PopulationBuild` (`population_builds`)
One Population Studio run (§7.8). Columns: `id`, `session_id` (indexed; FK on Postgres, cascade), **`frame`** (jsonb, nullable — the sampling frame, §7.9; Alembic `0009_frame`, which also adds **`spawned_agents.weight`**), `status` (`queued | detecting | gathering | clarifying | planning | awaiting_review | spawning | complete | stopped | error`), `mode` (`fast | pro` — which model writes the personas), `target_count`, `constraints` (JSON — the dials: stance mix, demographics, sentiment, audience profile, uploaded survey text, `skip_questions`; legacy `humanity`/`humanity_coverage` keys may be present but the Studio ignores them — each segment's register sets humanity, §7.8), `sources` (JSON — `{quant, quant_sources[], quant_query}`), `detected` (JSON — what the inputs establish about the population), `questions` (JSON list — clarifying questions with `suggested`, `default` and the analyst's `answer`), `plan` (JSON — `{segments[], rationale, assumptions[], evidence_coverage}`; each segment carries its `share_pct`, `count`, stance, demographics, sentiment, arguments, evidence, rationale and the analyst's `decision`: `proposed | accepted | edited | rejected`), `log` (JSON list of `{ts, stage, level, message, detail}`), `error`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. Statistics pages gathered for a build are ordinary `evidence` rows with `source_class="quant"`, `trust_tier="high"` and the extracted facts in `structured.facts`.

## 7. The agent-intelligence system (the 112 dials)

This is the product's crown jewel. Code: `backend/app/services/agents/{profiles,agent_factory,agent_runner,dial_analytics}.py`.

### 7.1 Personas are emergent, not catalogued
`profiles.py` contains **no hard-coded archetypes** — only the *shape* of an agent (`AgentProfile` dataclass) and a 15-color avatar palette (`AVATAR_COLORS`). All substantive persona content (name, role, background, correlation, personality, debate style, and all dial values) is **generated at runtime by the LLM**. The only construction-time randomness is `energy` (`random.uniform(0.3, 1.0)`) and avatar color (de-duplicated against the palette). The one fixed structural concept is the three-way **stance** taxonomy (direct/indirect/neutral).

### 7.2 The dials — `DIALS_SCHEMA` (exactly 112 across 9 groups)
`DIALS_SCHEMA` (`agent_factory.py`) is a JSON template (every leaf initialized to `0`) injected verbatim into the generation prompt, so the LLM returns the identical nested structure. **Verified: 9 groups, 112 leaf dials, each an integer 0–10** (0 = none/lowest, 10 = extreme/highest).

| Group | # | What it captures | Representative dials |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sentiment` | 23 | Current emotional state re: the topic | joy, sadness, anger, fear, trust, anticipation, pride, shame, guilt, envy, awe, nostalgia, hope, anxiety, confusion, curiosity, frustration, … |
| `motivation` | 16 | What drives engagement | desire, urgency, need_intensity, aspiration, mastery, autonomy, status, belonging, security, novelty, convenience, control, … |
| `habit` | 11 | Behavioral patterns | cue_strength, action_simplicity, reward_immediacy, repeat_frequency, ritual_potential, dependency_risk, switching_cost, habit_pull, … |
| `trust` | 11 | Trust/skepticism profile | credibility, transparency, social_proof, authority, consistency, privacy_comfort, safety, fairness, reliability, guarantee_strength, … |
| `friction` | 11 | Personal barriers (resistance) | cognitive_load, time_cost, money_pain, ambiguity, choice_overload, technical_difficulty, emotional_resistance, embarrassment_risk, social_risk, regret_risk, friction |
| `identity` | 10 | Alignment with self-concept | self_fit, tribe_fit, values_fit, aesthetic_fit, cultural_fit, life_stage_fit, status_lift, taste_fit, belonging_fit, identity_fit |
| `commercial` | 10 | Commercial relationship | purchase_intent, willingness_to_pay, perceived_value, premium_justification, repeat_intent, referral_intent, churn_risk, upgrade_intent, objection_intensity, price_pain |
| `product` | 10 | Product/service experience | ease, reward_clarity, shareability, delight, usefulness, memorability, clarity, confidence, satisfaction, emotional_fit |
| `composite` | 10 | Derived/aggregate scores | human_resonance, product_emotional_fit, retention_potential, share_potential, desire_trust, habit_potential, virality_potential, product_humanity, emotional_risk, adoption_readiness |

(23+16+11+11+11+10+10+10+10 = **112**.)

**How dial values are set:** not random and not algorithmic — they are **LLM-chosen per agent**, tuned relative to the query topic, kept consistent with the agent's background/stance/personality, with `composite` instructed to be logically derived from the other groups. There is **no code-side validation** of the 0–10 range or schema completeness — it is enforced only by prompt instruction (missing dials fall back to `{}`).

### 7.2a Dynamic dials — the dials this question needs (brief L3-04)

Code: `backend/app/services/agents/dynamic_dials.py`. The 112 are generic by design and cannot say *formulary pressure*, *transport friction*, *waiting-list fatigue* or *shift rigidity* — usually the forces a study turns on. So the model **chooses 6–12 extra dials for the session's question**, and every twin then carries an integer 0–10 on each, tuned by the same persona-writing call as the sentiment dials. They are generic in kind, not in content: a pricing question gets pricing dials, a policy question gets policy dials — nothing is hard-coded per audience.

- **Definition** — `{key (snake_case), label, why, low (what 0 is), high (what 10 is)}`. Keys are slugged and deduplicated and **may not shadow a fixed dial**; fewer than 3 usable dials means none (an invented dial is worse than no dial).
- **Chosen once per session**, stored on `analysis_sessions.dynamic_dials` (Alembic `0012_dynamic_dials`) and cached in-process. The **Studio picks them at plan time** from the question, the detected population and the segments, logs them (`Dynamic dials — N chosen for this question…`) and carries them on `plan.dynamic_dials`; the Pro quick-spawn path and the Agent Builder pick them on first use.
- **Values** — the persona prompt states each dial with both ends and asks for one value per persona (`"dynamic": {…}` in the returned JSON), which `attach` moves into `dials["dynamic"]` beside the 112. Unknown keys are dropped, values clamp to 0–10.
- **Cast personas (L3-02)** obey the mould here too: where an archetype carries a dynamic value the cast twin stays within ±`DIAL_DRIFT` of it; where the mould was authored for another question, the writer's value stands.
- **The Agent Builder** shows them as a **Dynamic** group directly under Sentiment — set by hand or left to the system, filled by *Build sentiment profile*, saved with the twin into its lineup.
- **They reach the persona** at debate, chat and Lab time as a prompt block (`Formulary pressure: 9/10 — blocked in practice`), so a twin's own constraints shape what it raises and resists.
- **Read-out**: the roster's dial viewer and the population dial dashboard (`GET /sessions/{id}/dials`) show Dynamic as a group under Sentiment, with the authored labels and `why` rather than title-cased keys.
- **In the Behaviour Lab** every dynamic dial is a split and a filter of its own. `probe.segments_for` buckets each one **low / mid / high** under a namespaced key (`dyn:<dial>`, so a dial called *region* can never collide with the demographic split), and because `_select_agents` reads the same map, the same dials appear under *Who answers*. Instruments and the A/B heat-map take their split list from `probe.split_keys(rows)` — the base splits plus whatever dials the answering agents carry — so a new dial needs no instrument change, and an experiment's sentence names the dial ("the effect is not reliable for high transport friction").
- **Limits**: the Fast **bank** spawn path has no LLM, so bank populations carry no dynamic dials.

### 7.3 Agent creation flow — two paths (Fast bank vs Pro LLM)

There are **two spawn paths**, chosen by `mode`:

- **Fast (default)** — `seed_bank.sample_bank(...)` samples a **pre-built, topic-agnostic agent bank** (no LLM call, instant). The bank is generated **procedurally** (no API key needed) into `backend/app/data/agent_bank.json` — ~500 diverse personas, each with the full 112-dial profile spread across the 0–10 range for visible diversity. Sampling honors the requested count (with replacement + light dial jitter above the bank size), the stance split, and humanity/coverage. Personas are generic; they become topic-aware only when they post.
- **Pro** — `agent_factory.generate_agents(... mode="pro")` curates every persona with the LLM on the **Sonnet** tier and a deeper prompt (explicitly spread intelligence/expertise/articulacy/emotion — some sharp authorities, some out of their depth). Generated in **batches of ~10 personas per call** (`_BATCH_SIZE`), bounded by a **`spawn_concurrency` semaphore** (default 8) so a 1000-agent spawn (100 batches) doesn't fire 100 concurrent calls and trip rate limits. Parameters: `session_id, query, count, profile_query="", direct_pct=33, indirect_pct=33, neutral_pct=34, doc_context="", humanity=0, humanity_coverage=0, mode="pro"`.

Either path returns `AgentProfile` objects; the spawn task then **bulk-inserts** the whole population in one transaction and streams it to the UI in **batched `agents_spawned_batch` events** (~40 agents/event) — replacing the old per-agent commit + 0.3 s sleep + per-agent event, which made 1000-agent spawns impossibly slow and overflowed the WS queue.

The legacy LLM flow (Pro) in detail:

Steps:
1. Pull knowledge-graph context for the query (`query_rag`, hybrid mode), truncated to 2500 chars, to ground the personas in the ingested material.
2. Compute exact per-stance counts from the percentages (each stance gets ≥1; neutral absorbs rounding drift so they sum to `count`). **This deterministic split is the diversity-enforcement mechanism** — the LLM is told exactly how many experts vs. adjacent vs. skeptics to produce.
3. Optionally append an **audience profile** instruction (`profile_query`) and **survey/profile data** (`doc_context`, up to `SURVEY_CHAR_LIMIT` = 40,000 chars, trimmed in the browser with a notice) to translate into dial values.
4. Prompt Claude (system = "expert behavioral psychologist and simulation designer") for a JSON array of exactly `count` agents, each with all fields + the full `DIALS_SCHEMA`. Both LLM spawn paths (this one and the Studio's `_plan_prompt`) share **`_GEO_INSTRUCTIONS`**: every persona gets a real, specific `region`; the model must **shift the dials to how the topic lands in that place** (price sensitivity vs local incomes, trust vs the local mood toward institutions, habit/friction vs local infrastructure and routines, identity vs local values — two similar personas in different places must diverge); and it writes a per-persona **`geo_behavior`** paragraph — the query analysed from that place: local attitudes, habits, reference points, and the culture's way of voicing agreement/disagreement. `region` + `geo_behavior` land in the agent's `demographics` and are persisted.
5. Build per-agent **slots** (stance + humanity flag), shuffled so the humanized subset and stances spread evenly, then chunk into batches of ≤10 and call Claude (**`model_orchestration`**, `max_tokens=12000`) for each batch concurrently via `asyncio.gather`.
6. Parse each batch with `_parse_agents_json` — a **salvage parser** that recovers every complete `{...}` object even if the output is truncated, so a partial response still yields agents instead of zero. Build `AgentProfile` objects with fresh UUIDs, de-duplicated colors, and randomized energy. If *all* batches fail, raise a clear error (surfaced as `spawn_error`) rather than a cryptic JSON exception.

### 7.4 Post generation — `agent_runner.generate_post(...)`
When an agent posts, its persona is assembled into a first-person system prompt and Claude writes the post text.

- **`_build_system_prompt`** constructs the identity block: *"You are {name}, a {age}-year-old {role}. Background… Your relationship to the topic… Your personality… Your debate style… Your stance type…"* + a **geographic-behavior block** (`_geo_block`) + the dial-derived behavioral guidance + a fixed coda (*stay in character, be specific, conversational like a Reddit post, 1–3 paragraphs, never reveal you're an AI*).
- **`_geo_block`** makes the agent's place behavioral in every prompt (posts, chat, probes): it injects the spawn-written `demographics.geo_behavior` paragraph under a *"HOW YOUR PLACE SHAPES YOU ({region})"* header, plus a directive to keep vocabulary, prices, currency, units, reference points and the local way of voicing agreement/disagreement true to that geography. An agent with a `region` but no paragraph (pre-existing rosters) gets a generic version of the same directive; an agent with neither is unchanged.
- **`_dials_to_behavioral_guidance`** is a **deterministic Python rule engine** (not an LLM call) that converts dials into natural-language style instructions via ~35+ threshold rules. Crucially, it reads **only 4 of the 9 groups** — `sentiment`, `motivation`, `friction`, `trust`. Examples: `anger≥8` → "sharp, clipped language"; `anxiety≥7` → "you hedge constantly: 'I think', 'maybe'"; `pride≥7` → "reference your own expertise"; `trust.credibility≥8` → "cite sources and statistics" vs `≤2` → "argue from personal experience". The design intent: **dials govern *how* you communicate; role/background govern *what* you say.**
- **The other 5 groups** (`habit, identity, commercial, product, composite`) do **not** affect debate behavior — they exist purely to feed the analytics dashboard (§7.5).
- Three post modes build different prompts: `comment` (new top-level), `reply` (engage with a quoted post), `debate` (pointed rebuttal). Each includes the query + KG context (~1500 chars) + a window of recent thread history.
- Model: **`model_agents`** (defaults to Haiku 4.5), `max_tokens=600`. Returns plain post text only.
- A sibling `chat_as_agent` powers the 1:1 agent chat (multi-turn, `max_tokens=800`) with **`task="chat"`** (2026-10-02): the same persona, dials, register, place, character and *what you actually know* blocks, then `_CHAT_DIRECTIVE` — this is a conversation with the person, nothing is off-topic (the debate question, their work, home, partner, money, health, opinions on anything), never deflect or redirect, never the assistant voice, the dials and register decide the tone of every answer whatever it is about, answer personal questions from a plausible consistent life, and the only bound is knowledge: beyond their education, trade or life they say they don't know in their own voice (a lay guess must be called a guess) — plus a per-band manner line (`_CHAT_BY_BAND`: reactive a line or two from the gut … expert a considered short paragraph, out of their depth off their field). The scoped knowledge block is framed as *what you have read, heard and picked up*, never as a graph or a source.

### 7.5 Population analytics — `dial_analytics.aggregate_dials(...)`
Pure functions (no I/O) that roll a population's dials into the **"population dial dashboard"**:
- **Per-dial & per-group distributions** (mean, min, max, stdev, n, raw values, and means split by stance).
- **Orientation awareness:** a `NEGATIVE_DIALS` set (all friction dials, churn_risk, objection_intensity, price_pain, emotional_risk, dependency_risk, switching_cost) is inverted (`10 - mean`) so "lower is better" reads correctly, banded into strong/moderate/weak/critical.
- **The scorecard** — the headline synthetic market-research metrics, in fixed order: `adoption_readiness, purchase_intent, willingness_to_pay, virality_potential, retention_potential, human_resonance, product_emotional_fit, churn_risk, emotional_risk`.
- **A stance × scorecard heatmap** showing how direct vs. indirect vs. neutral agents differ on each metric.

This is what reframes the simulation as a **synthetic market-research panel**. Exposed via `GET /sessions/{id}/dials`.

### 7.6 The model pipeline (Fast Haiku / Pro Sonnet)
| Role | Setting | Default | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|
| Orchestration | `MODEL_ORCHESTRATION` | `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` | Fast-mode is irrelevant here; report writing, YouTube Vision, synthetic-paper generation |
| Agents | `MODEL_AGENTS` | `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` | **Fast-mode** debate posts + agent chat |
| Fast | `MODEL_FAST` | `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` | KG entity extraction, KG query, query classification, per-agent opinion verdicts |
| **Pro orchestration** | `MODEL_PRO_ORCHESTRATION` | `claude-sonnet-4-6` | **Pro-mode** persona curation (`generate_agents`) |
| **Pro agents** | `MODEL_PRO_AGENTS` | `claude-sonnet-4-6` | **Pro-mode** debate posts |

The mode → model mapping lives on `Settings`: `agent_model(mode)`, `orchestration_model(mode)`, `sim_concurrency(mode)`. **Fast** routes posts to Haiku at high concurrency; **Pro** routes persona curation *and* posts to Sonnet at lower concurrency (richer, slower, pricier). `MODEL_FAST` (KG/classification) is unaffected by mode.

### 7.7 Humanity & Coverage (spawn-time controls)

Two population controls that the API's classic Fast/Pro spawn (`POST /spawn-agents`) still accepts tune how *human vs. expert* the population reasons — **the UI no longer exposes them** (the quick-spawn form was removed on 2026-09-16; the Studio's per-segment register is the only route in the product) — added to counter the tendency for every agent to sound like an analyst. **The Population Studio does not use these sliders**: there, each plan segment's **register** (`humanity_hint`) sets its agents' humanity band directly (§7.8) — the two controls almost did the same job, so the Studio path was merged onto the per-segment register (2026-09-15).

- **Humanity (0–100)** — a graduated **sentiment-vs-logic register**, not a binary switch. `agent_runner._humanity_band` maps the value to five distinct bands, each with its own hard behavioral directive so moving the dial visibly changes how an agent argues:

  | Band | Range | Register |
  |---|---|---|
  | **expert** | `[0, 20)` | Pure logic — cites data, measured, analytical. |
  | **tempered** | `[20, 50)` | Logic leads and stays in control, but feelings color tone, word choice and emphasis. Still cites if credible. |
  | **balanced** | `[50, 60)` | Weighs feeling and logic 50/50 and names both out loud. |
  | **defensive** | `[60, 70)` | Feelings decide the position *first*; logic is then marshaled to **defend that feeling at any cost** (motivated reasoning — never concedes). |
  | **reactive** | `[70, 100]` | Purely emotional — here to feel, judge and react; ignores logical arguments and reacts to how they *feel*; short, raw, no citations. |

- **Coverage (0–100)** — what percentage of the spawned population the Humanity setting applies to; the rest stay pure-expert (`humanity = 0`). UI defaults: Humanity `50`, Coverage `60`.

**How it works (spawn-time, persisted per agent):**
- Both spawn paths compute a humanized subset (`round(count × coverage/100)`) and set each agent's `humanity` field to the slider value (others `0`). **Fast** (`seed_bank._humanize`) **grades the dials by band** — credibility/authority stay moderate for *tempered* and drop progressively toward *reactive*, with 1–3 sentiment dials boosted hot accordingly. **Pro** (`agent_factory.generate_agents`) instructs the LLM to make the subset emotion-led (sentiment wide/intense, credibility/authority low) and to spread dials across the full 0–10 range, with sentiment as the primary driver of voice.
- The value persists on `SpawnedAgent.humanity` and flows back through the agents API and the spawn events.
- At post time, `agent_runner._build_system_prompt` reads `humanity`, resolves the **band**, and appends that band's directive (table above). The "cite sources/credentials" expert rule is allowed only below `50` (logic-led bands); `reactive` explicitly forces gut/personal argument; and the closing instruction tightens with the band (e.g. *reactive* → "1–2 short sentences, react from the gut"). The *balanced* directive explicitly allows committing to a side when gut and reason agree — an earlier wording ("you sit in the tension") made every balanced agent identically torn.
- `generate_post` additionally (all added 2026-09-15, after a prod run produced fifty near-identical mini-essays):
  - frames the KG context as **the agent's own knowledge** ("things you happen to know… never call it 'context', 'evidence' or 'the knowledge graph'") — agents were citing "the knowledge graph" verbatim in posts — with band-scaled usage (`_KNOWLEDGE_USE`: expert cites specifics, balanced uses at most one or two loose facts, reactive cites nothing);
  - draws a **random per-post length directive** per band (`_LENGTH_CHOICES`: e.g. balanced is 50/50 "one short paragraph" vs "2-3 sentences") and scales `max_tokens` by band (`_BAND_MAX_TOKENS`: expert 600 / tempered 500 / balanced 420 / defensive 350 / reactive 150; Pro raises expert+tempered to 850) so a thread reads with mixed lengths;
  - the `direct` stance gloss is now "a first-hand stake — you live this decision or work inside it; experienced, not necessarily an expert" (it used to call every direct agent "a domain expert", pushing ~⅔ of Studio populations into analyst voice).

**Surfacing it in the UI:** the Agents page shows the two sliders with a **live band chip** under the intensity slider (e.g. *"Defensive — defends their feelings with logic at any cost"*) and a preview (*"≈ N of M agents will be defensive — …"*); each agent card shows a **"% human" badge** (when humanized) and **dominant-emotion chips** (its top sentiment dials ≥6, e.g. *Curiosity 9 · Trust 8*).

> Humanity is **orthogonal to stance** — a `direct` expert can still be high-humanity (a passionate practitioner who argues from emotion). It is applied **at spawn**, so changing the sliders requires a re-spawn to take effect.

### 7.8 The Population Studio — evidence-grounded, human-in-the-loop population building

Code: `backend/app/services/population/{sources,builder}.py`, `agent_factory.generate_agents_from_plan`, `app/api/v1/population.py`, `frontend/src/app/session/[id]/population/page.tsx` + `components/population/*`. **It is the Agents tab's first screen** (embedded `components/population/PopulationStudio.tsx`; the `/session/{id}/population` route renders the same component full-page for deep links). The roster's **Rebuild in Studio** reopens it; *View agents* returns to the roster.

**Why it exists.** Fast personas are generic and Pro personas are invented from a prompt: neither shows the analyst *who* the population is or *why*, and neither reads base rates. The Studio builds the population as a **plan of segments** the analyst can see, question and veto before a single agent is written, and it grounds the plan in three kinds of input: the session's evidence brief and knowledge graph (§10a), the analyst's own uploads and audience profile, and **quantitative facts gathered from statistics publishers**.

**Stages (each one logged and streamed as it happens):**
1. **Detect** — reads what is on file (evidence brief groups, graph size, uploaded survey, audience profile, statistics already gathered) and has Claude state, with sources, what the inputs establish: topic, the decision the population faces, geography, the realistic population, the groups implied, demographic and mood signals, gaps, and a **confidence** figure. Every finding is a log line. The same call **proposes the dials from the research** (age range and skew, share of women, regions, settlement, income, education, for/against mood, temperature, institutional trust, price sensitivity, tech comfort, openness — with sentinels where the inputs are silent); `apply_detected_dials` folds them into the constraints **only where the analyst left a dial on its default**, records them in `constraints.derived_from_research` (dial → basis), and the panel shows a *from research* chip on each. A dial the analyst moved always wins; a research-set dial may be revised by a later detect.
2. **Gather** (optional, on by default) — statistics about the **audience**, never about the question itself. Four steps, every one logged:
   - **Plan targets** (`builder.plan_quant_targets`, prompt `TARGETS_SYSTEM`): one call reads, in order of precedence, the **analyst's configuration** (the dials as set, the dials already derived from research, the audience profile, the first 2,500 characters of the uploaded survey — "search for base rates about THOSE people, not the general public"), then what **detect** established (target population, geography, kind, segments hinted, demographic signals and, as priority 1, the **gaps**), then the facts already on file and the evidence brief, then the **ticked publishers** with what each covers and how it titles its pages (`sources.catalogue_for_prompt`). It returns the audience in one line, **2–5 fact targets** — each one measurable base rate with a `dimension` (`size, age, gender, region, household, income, occupation, education, attitude, trust, tech, habit, health, transport, housing, price, consumer, other`), a `why`, a `priority` and 1–2 publisher-phrased `queries` routed to publisher keys — and a `skipped` list naming the dimensions the configuration already settles (an analyst-set dial is never searched for; the log shows "Not searching income: …"). If the call fails, `fallback_targets` runs one mechanical `keyword_squeeze` target. The analyst's own `quant_query` is prepended as a verbatim one-round target.
   - **Which publishers.** The panel's ticks are sent as `sources.quant_sources` with `quant_auto: true` while the analyst has never ticked or unticked anything; the build then re-derives the set (`default_sources`) — **since 2026-10-02 that is every publisher in the catalogue, whatever the geography**, in the Studio and the simple view alike (Drishan: *all of these sources should be auto-enabled*); the planner still names the publishers each target is routed to and `route_sources` orders them by automation fit, so an unrelated publisher is only searched when it is the best route — saves them back onto the build's `sources`, logs "Publishers chosen for …", and the panel's ticks follow. A touched selection is used as-is.
   - **Search and triage** (`sources.gather_target`): each query runs against the publishers `route_sources` picks — the planner's named keys that are ticked, else every ticked publisher whose `covers` includes the target's dimension, else all of them, ordered by readability (`fit`) — with a `site:` operator on the publisher's `site` prefix where it has one (`gov.uk/government/statistics`, `ipsos.com/en-uk`, `edelman.com/uk/trust`) and a domain filter on the results. Before any page is read, a cheap **triage** call (`TRIAGE_SYSTEM`) picks the results that look like statistical releases, bulletins, survey reports or polling tables and skips press releases, policy papers, consultations, guidance and pages about another country; a `heuristic_rank` on URL and title patterns stands in when the call fails. **Triage judges against the fact target, not the question** (since 2026-10-06): the prompt puts the target first and labels the question *context — NOT what the page must be about*, and says a Census tenure table answers a tenure target for a question about food-waste caddies. *Before that, the Manchester caddy build skipped every tenure and household-composition table because "none contain data on attitudes toward food-waste caddy collection".*
   - **Read and judge** (`_read_and_extract`): the best pages are read (`fetch_page`, PDFs skimmed) and Claude extracts typed facts (`statistic, value, group, geography, year, quote`; up to 24 per page, **from the whole fetched page** — until 2026-10-06 it saw the first 9,000 chars, and statistics bulletins keep their tables lower down) plus distribution sentences, **against the target**: the schema also returns `answers_target` (the page gives the number asked for **at the breakdown and geography the target names** — a *by ward* target is not answered by MSOA, LSOA or constituency figures, a city target not by its region; since 2026-10-06, after a page of three MSOA populations was marked as answering "population of Manchester by ward" and stopped that target's second round) and `relevance` 0–100 (same group, same place, recent — judged against the target's group and place, not whether the page mentions the question's subject). A page is `on_topic` only when it has usable facts *and* relevance ≥ 30, and `Evidence.relevance` carries the judged score, so a page about the wrong country is kept greyed and never reaches the plan. Each page is a `quant` evidence row (`trust_tier=high`, `run_id` NULL — that column is a foreign key to `research_runs` on Postgres, so the build is recorded as `structured.build_id`; `structured.target` and `structured.answers_target` record what it was hunting), streams to the Ingest feed as a `research_item`, and on-topic pages are chunked into the graph under `[SOURCE quant | publisher | url | date]`.
   - **Re-fire** — when a round ends with no page answering the target, a **refine** call (`REFINE_SYSTEM`) is shown what was tried and what each page read turned out to be, and writes 0–2 new queries that take another route **to the same fact** (the target's reason is shown as context only; since 2026-10-06 the prompt forbids swapping a tenure or household target for a search about the question's attitudes, which is where the caddy build's second round went): the survey's or dataset's proper name, the publisher's own region vocabulary, a broader geography, or other ticked publishers covering the dimension. Up to `QUANT_MAX_ROUNDS` (2) rounds per target, and a target stops as soon as one page answers it (its second phrasing is not run); the log says "Target found", "Target partly covered" (related facts, not the figure) or "Target not found". `gather_targets` runs the targets in priority order sharing one page budget (`QUANT_MAX_PAGES`, 16; at least 3 pages per target, unused pages roll over; `QUANT_RESULTS_PER_SOURCE` 5, `QUANT_PAGES_PER_QUERY` 3), and stops between pages on Stop.
   - **Dials from the statistics** — detect runs before these facts exist, so after gathering the build re-reads the dials against the facts now on file (`DIALS_FROM_FACTS_SCHEMA`, the detect dial block) and folds them in through `apply_detected_dials` (analyst-moved dials still win; the log says "Dials set from the statistics: …").

   The **Sources panel's ad-hoc Search** uses the same machinery: a query that reads like a question (`looks_like_question`) is planned into up to 3 audience targets against the latest build's constraints and detected population and re-fires like the build; a terse keyword query runs verbatim, one round (`keyword_target`). The **catalogue** (`QUANT_SOURCES`, from the September 2026 source review) carries per publisher `domain`, optional `site`, `regions`, `kind`, `covers`, `phrasing` (how it titles pages), `fit` (1–5 readability for an automated reader) and an extraction `note`: UK — ONS, Nomis, gov.uk statistics (DWP HBAI, DfT National Travel Survey, English Housing Survey, Community Life Survey), Scotland's Census, NISRA, FCA Financial Lives, Ofcom, More in Common, Opinium, Focaldata, Savanta, Ipsos UK, YouGov (fit 2 — headline without crossbreaks), Edelman Trust Barometer, NHS England statistics, Sport England Active Lives, SMMT, British Retail Consortium, London Datastore, Understanding Society, data.gov.uk (a directory); US — Census Bureau, Pew, Gallup; Europe/global — Eurostat (UK series end 2020), OECD, World Bank, Our World in Data; **Statista is opt-in only** (fit 1: teasers hide the number behind a canvas and a bot wall). Default ticks for a UK geography are the review's "default set": ONS, Nomis, gov.uk, FCA, Ofcom, More in Common, Opinium (+ Scotland's Census / NISRA / London Datastore when the geography names them); US → Census, Pew, Gallup, OECD; EU → Eurostat, OECD, OWID, Pew; otherwise OECD, World Bank, OWID, Pew. The Sources panel groups the chips (United Kingdom / United States / Europe & global), orders them by fit with a readability dot, and dims fit ≤ 2. Facts can be ignored / re-included (the evidence exclude flag) and the plan only reads included ones.
3. **Clarify** — Claude asks 0–4 questions whose answers would *materially* change the population (each with suggested answers and the default it will assume). The build pauses in `clarifying`; the analyst answers any subset or skips; answers are logged as decisions and fed to the planner. `constraints.skip_questions` bypasses this.
4. **Plan** — one structured call composes 3–8 segments: name, share, stance, description, age range, gender split, regions, income band, education, occupations, mood, temperature, dominant emotions, the arguments the group actually makes, the evidence behind it (or none — stated), the **rationale**, and a **register** (`humanity_hint`: `expert | tempered | balanced | defensive | reactive`) — the planner is told to set it from who the group honestly is (ordinary consumers are rarely "expert") because it decides how analytical vs emotional the segment's agents sound. `normalise_segments` makes the kept shares sum to 100 and the counts sum to the target (largest remainder, ≥1 each) and clamps ages inside the age dial. The plan also carries `assumptions` and an honest `evidence_coverage` line. Status → `awaiting_review`.
5. **Review** — per segment the analyst can **Accept**, **Edit** (share, stance, ages, gender, regions, income, education, mood, temperature, **register**; editing a share rescales the other kept segments to fill the rest) or **Reject with a reason**; a rejection regenerates that slot with a single-segment call that is given the reason and the segments that stay (`replaced` names the old one). **Re-plan** re-composes around the accepted segments with the current dials. Unreviewed segments count as accepted at approval.
6. **Approve & build** — wipes the session's agents and calls `generate_agents_from_plan`: per segment, batches of ≤10 personas on the mode's orchestration model (Fast = Haiku, Pro = Sonnet), every batch told the segment spec as hard constraints (same stance, inside the age range, matching the gender split, living in the named places, the segment's mood and arguments), the population-wide dials, the evidence brief + quant facts, and who is already in the roster. **The segment's register IS the humanity dial**: `agent_factory.HINT_HUMANITY_BANDS` maps each hint to a band of the humanity scale (expert 0–15, tempered 25–45, balanced 50–58, defensive 60–68, reactive 72–90; unknown/missing → tempered), the persona writer varies the value per person inside the band, and `finalise_segment_dicts` clamps every persona into its segment's band (the old population-wide `humanity`/`humanity_coverage` constraints are ignored on this path). Cross-batch duplicates are repaired as in §7.3. Agents are bulk-inserted with `segment` + `demographics` and streamed with the usual `agents_spawned_batch` / `agents_ready` events; the log narrates progress per segment.

**The dials (`constraints`).** Population size and model; **Expert ↔ Reactive** (`voice: {auto, value}`, 0–100) — shapes **how the personas feel and speak, never who is in the population**: composition always aims at the most realistic, quantified mix for the demographic (the evidence, statistics and sampling frame), and `voice_instruction` tells the planner exactly that. Within 10 of the middle nothing is imposed and each group keeps its natural register. Either side, at build, `agent_factory.apply_voice` runs after the segment's register band: it shifts every persona's humanity by up to ±40, scales the **sentiment dials** (toward expert they flatten toward 3; toward reactive the hot ones run hotter and the cool ones stay cool) and counter-scales `trust.credibility` / `trust.authority`, and the persona prompt carries a matching directive for the background and debate style (measured and evidence-led, or reacting from their own job, money, family and neighbourhood) — with every job, place, income and segment kept exactly as planned. **Auto** (the default) lets the planner choose the value from the question and record why (`plan.voice {auto, value, reason}`, shown under the dials and logged); the analyst can set it and re-plan; **auto** (the default) lets the planner choose the value from the question — a guideline or policy question leans expert, a consumer or public-mood question leans reactive — and the plan records `plan.voice {auto, value, reason}`, shown under the dials and logged, so the analyst can set it by hand and re-plan; at build the value reaches the persona writer as a voice directive, `constraints.voice_used`, when it is 10 or more from 50);  **Demographics** — age range + skew, share of women, regions (free text), settlement type, income, education, notes; **Sentiment** — *follow the evidence* (default) or a for/against/mixed mood target, plus emotional temperature, trust in institutions, price sensitivity, comfort with technology and openness to change (0–10; 5 = not imposed); **Stance mix** — *let the segments decide* (default) or a direct/indirect/neutral target. **Humanity is not a Studio dial**: the panel shows a note pointing at the per-segment register instead, and legacy `humanity`/`humanity_coverage` keys in `constraints` are ignored. Only dials moved off their default are stated to the planner and the persona prompts (`_constraints_block`), so an untouched dial never over-constrains the model.

**Where the demographics go afterwards.** `agent_runner._demographics_line` puts gender, place, income, education and the segment into every persona prompt (posts, chat, probes), `agent_runner._geo_block` adds the *how your place shapes you* paragraph (§7.4), and `probe.segments_for` adds `segment`, `gender`, `region`, `income_band` and `education` as Lab split keys, so a survey can be cut by the slice it was built from. Presets carry `segment`/`demographics`/`humanity` through save and load.

**The population map** (`components/population/SamplingFrameGraph.tsx`, opened by the Studio's *Sampling frame* button; `services/population/facets.py`). A graph of **who is in the population**: the cell types are the plan's **facets** — the 5–15 things a reader needs to understand these people for *this* question (where they live, what they earn, how they commute, their condition status …), chosen and ranked per plan by `pick_facets` (`population_facets`, at plan time; attribute facets on any failure) and stored as `plan.facets`. Two kinds: **attribute** facets read what personas already carry (place, age band, income band, occupation, gender, education, segment, stance, register); **persona** facets are question-specific — the persona writer gives every persona one label from the facet's 2–6 `values_hint`, stored in `demographics.facets`. Every persona sits in exactly one cell of every facet, and **a cell's size is the number of personas in it, nothing else**; the map fills in live as personas stream in. Layout: **one panel per facet** in rank order (a two-column grid), each cell a bubble whose diameter scales with the square root of its persona count, showing the count inside and the label and share below, biggest first; a facet's long tail beyond eight cells folds into a dashed **"N others"** bubble that expands on click, so thirty places or fifty occupations stay readable. A *show top 5 / 8 / 10 / 15 / all* control, "N not placed" when personas carry nothing for a facet, the facet's reason on hover. (A single ring with every cell on it was tried first and could not hold high-cardinality facets.) It draws personas only — no target rings; the matching against published distributions lives on the *Sampling frame* card (§7.9), not here.

### 7.9 The sampling frame — matched to published distributions, with the analyst in the loop (brief L2-01 → L2-05)

Code: `backend/app/services/population/frame.py`, builder stages in `builder.py`, routes in `api/v1/population.py`, UI `components/population/FrameCard.tsx` (+ rings in `SamplingFrameGraph`, a weighted line in the Lab). Drishan's framing (2026-09-16): *the Studio sorts by the most important sample dimensions, researches the realistic data, and matches to it; where it can't find a distribution it flags it and the analyst uploads data or lets the model estimate, labelled.* That is what this is.

- **Pick the dimensions** (`pick_dimensions`, `population_frame_dims`, after detect): 3–5 dimensions ranked by how much they matter for *this* question, each with `attribute` (region, age, gender, income, education, occupation, household, ethnicity, employment, tenure, condition, attitude), `kind` (demographic / behavioural / attitudinal), a reason, whether a published distribution plausibly exists, and the proxy attribute when it does not. **One attribute per dimension** (since 2026-10-06): the picker is told a dimension is one thing a publisher could state — *age*, not *age and household lifecycle stage*; *deprivation*, not *deprivation and housing type* — and to make two dimensions when two attributes matter, because no publisher states a joint distribution and a compound label matched nothing. **The ranking is the mechanism, not a nicety**: the top three are matched exactly, the rest are weighted only (`MATCH_EXACTLY`), because a fifty-agent panel cannot honour five dimensions at once.
- **The frame drives the statistics search** (`search_targets`, `population_frame_search`): the dimensions are chosen right after detect, and before gather runs each one becomes a fact target phrased the way the publishers title their pages ("population estimates by single year of age", "highest level of qualification, Census 2021"), routed to the ticked publishers that cover it, **ahead of the planner's own gap targets** (up to 9 targets when a frame exists, 5 otherwise; a deterministic phrasing per attribute stands in when the planning call fails; attitudinal dimensions are not searched). Alongside them a **sizing trio — TAM / SAM / SOM** (Drishan's framing): everyone in the place, the slice with the condition or in scope, the slice the system reaches today (prescribing, activity, uptake). The existing gather machinery — Tavily advanced domain-scoped search when keyed, triage, read, relevance judge, re-fire — hunts them. After gather, `extract_sizing` (`population_frame_sizing`) reads the three figures from the material — the published statistics first, then the research-and-uploads passages that carry sizing words (`ResearchMaterial.for_sizing`: population, households, adults, million, owners, uptake, prevalence, market …) — with source and year, never estimating (a figure read from an upload names the document and the source it cites), and the frame card shows them as three tiles; this is the headcount denominator L7-02 will multiply through.
- **Derive the targets** (`derive_targets`, `population_frame_target`, after gather): one structured call per dimension over **everything on file, in two pools**. The first is the published statistics (typed facts + page excerpts from the quant evidence rows, `material_text`). The second is **the analyst's own research and uploads** (`ResearchMaterial`, built by `builder._research_material`): the on-topic web pages the Ingest research run read (`sources.load_research_rows`, `source_class="web"`), every document or pasted text uploaded in Ingest (those live only as knowledge-graph chunks tagged `[SOURCE personal | …]` / `[SOURCE youtube | …]`; synthetic, web, social and quant chunks are skipped — the first is a prior, the rest are on file elsewhere), and the Studio's own survey / profile text (`constraints.doc_context`). The pool is split into passages (paragraphs, table rows, bullet lines) and each dimension is shown only the passages that name its attribute (`_DIM_KEYWORDS` plus the words of the dimension's label and reason) *and* carry a number, up to 6,000 characters, so a market-facts table's rows reach the call intact. The prompt prefers a published statistic; when none states the distribution, one stated in an upload or a research page is accepted and labelled by its **provenance** — `official_statistic`, `client_data` (from your upload) or `research_web` (from a research page); the frame card shows a *from your upload* / *from a research page* chip and the log says which. It may answer **found** (categories + shares copied from the source, with source, year and the *actual* coverage — a nation-level figure used for a town says so), **proxy** (a related attribute's distribution stands in, e.g. income for price sensitivity) or **missing** ("nothing on file: no statistics gathered, and no research page, upload or survey mentions it"). It never estimates. **How it reads a dimension** (since 2026-10-06; `dimension_brief`, `target_from_answer`): the call is shown the label, attribute, kind, place, the reason it matters (marked context only) and the **published proxy the picker named** ("look for that attribute's distribution if the dimension itself is not stated"); the prompt's three rules of reading are that a dimension is read **through its attribute** (a compound label — *age and household lifecycle stage* — is matched by the attribute's table alone, with the note saying which half the material does not state), that **every category the source states is kept** (dropping the 65+ bands of an age table to "focus" is estimating), and that the named proxy is looked for. In code, a distribution the model **listed with a source while calling the dimension missing is read as found** (the note starts "read as found: N categories listed from …"), and shares that sum to 40–95% are rescaled to 100 with a note to check no band was dropped. *On the 2026-10-06 Manchester caddy build the matcher listed the Census 2021 age bands (summing to 89%, the 65+ bands omitted) and reported the dimension missing because its label asked for two tables; the bands were discarded and the row went to "No figure found".* The log's "Reading the frame from everything on file: N statistics pages; M research pages, K uploads (…)" line names the pools each build read. *Before 2026-09-30 the frame read the quant rows only, so a facts table uploaded in Ingest and every research page were invisible to it and every gap went straight to "estimate or upload again".*
- **The ladder for every gap** (`resolve_frame_gap`, `POST …/builds/{bid}/frame/{dim_key}`): a missing dimension pauses the build at *clarifying* beside the questions. The analyst chooses per gap: **Model estimate** (`estimate_target`, `population_frame_estimate` — allowed only for demographic/behavioural attributes with known population patterns, refuses attitudinal ones and names the proxy; the result is labelled `model_inference` with the model's reasoning and confidence). **The estimate is shown what is on file** — the published facts plus the research-and-uploads passages for that dimension — and told to anchor the estimate to *this* audience and say in its reasoning what it leaned on, without treating the material as the distribution (nothing in it stated one, or the target would be found); the log says "anchored to what is on file" or "nothing on file to anchor it", **Upload a table** (a CSV of `category, share`, labelled `client_data`), **Use a proxy** (another dimension's distribution), or **Skip** (not matched and not weighted; the note and the button say so — estimate or upload a distribution later to change it; nothing revisits a skipped dimension on its own). *Estimate all* (`POST …/frame/estimate-all`) runs the ladder's estimate for every gap. Skipping the questions, or moving to the plan with gaps open, marks them **skipped** — nothing is invented silently.
- **Match at plan time** (`build_report`, stage `planned`): after the plan (and every segment decision / re-plan) each dimension's cells show target share vs the share the segments imply (`planned_distribution` splits a segment over its places, its age range, its gender share, its income / education / occupations). A **level** (good ≤ 5 pts off, fair ≤ 12, poor) and the worst cell are logged; the Studio's *Sampling frame* card draws target-vs-planned bars per cell.
- **Weight at build time** (`weights_for`): after the personas are written, every agent gets a raking weight (`stats.rake_weights`, trimmed to 0.2–5) to every active target and it is stored on `spawned_agents.weight`; the report is recomputed with **achieved** shares, **thin cells** (fewer than 3 agents where the target expects at least one — "not safe to cut by"), the **effective sample size** and the split of dimensions matched exactly / weighted / unmatched. The log carries the one-line summary (`summary_line`).
- **Weights appear in aggregation** (L2-02): a probe whose agents carry weights other than 1 also reports `aggregates.weighted` — the instrument's primary metric weighted to the frame beside the one-agent-one-vote figure, with the effective n — and the Lab shows it under the results ("Weighted to the sampling frame: would buy 41% (one agent one vote: 46% · effective n 32 of 50)"). Survey has no primary metric yet, so no weighted line there.
- **It is said out loud.** The final report's prompt carries a *POPULATION FRAME* block (`report_generator`) with the summary line and each dimension's status and source, including any model-estimated one, to be stated under Source Materials. The sampling frame graph's rings come from the targets where they exist (labelled with the source, "model estimate" for estimated ones) and the plan's own split elsewhere.
- **The cells are the frame's own dimensions, not a fixed list.** The planner returns `frame_values` per segment (one category per frame dimension, from the published categories when there are any, else a short consistent label), the persona writer returns `frame` per persona (stored in `demographics.frame`; a persona that skips a dimension inherits its segment's cell in `finalise_segment_dicts`), and every reader — `agent_cell` for achieved shares and weights, `planned_distribution` for the plan, the sampling frame graph — takes the explicit value first and only falls back to the demographic attribute (region, age, gender, income, education, occupation) when there is none. So a question that calls for *work pattern* or *privacy stance* gets real cells with real persona counts, and a persona sits in exactly one cell of every type. The graph's legend shows cells and personas per type ("3 cells · 50 personas"), each type is clickable to focus, and a type with a published distribution says which source its rings come from; with no frame at all the graph falls back to the persona attributes.
- **Honesty rules built in:** marginals only (the joint is reconciled by raking, and the report says the match is per dimension); model estimates lower the effective confidence and are labelled everywhere; a distribution read from an upload or a research page is labelled as such and never passed off as a published statistic; an attitudinal dimension is never estimated, only proxied. **The ladder's order is the intended one:** published statistics on file → the analyst's research and uploads → a model estimate anchored to what is on file → ask the analyst.
- **Tests:** `backend/tests/frame_test.py` (categorisation incl. proxies, planned/achieved distributions, weights + ESS, report levels / modes / thin cells, the ladder, model-facing steps stubbed; the research pool — passage selection per dimension, synthetic chunks excluded, an upload alone filling a target with `client_data` provenance, the estimate shown the material, sizing read from an upload).
- **Not built / limits:** ethnicity / tenure / household distributions have no persona attribute to read yet (they can be targets but no agent lands in a cell until personas carry them); the Lab's per-question survey numbers are unweighted; L2-06 dyads. The knowledge graph keeps the last 200 chunks per session, so a very large upload's early passages can age out of the research pool; web pages read in research carry no typed facts, so the frame reads their text, not extracted figures.

**Stop semantics.** Stop means "enough gathering — show me the plan", never "throw it away": during detect / gather / clarify the running stage is cancelled and the plan is composed at once from what is on file (research evidence and statistics first, general knowledge for the gaps, listed as assumptions); during planning the plan is allowed to land; during the build, generation stops after the batches in flight and the personas already written are inserted and kept (`should_stop` in `generate_agents_from_plan`; the log says "stopped early: N of M"). Approve, Re-plan and segment decisions work on a `stopped` build that has a plan, so the spawn option never disappears.

**Speed.** `generate_agents_from_plan` writes the segments **concurrently** (bounded by `spawn_concurrency`); within a segment the first batch goes alone so the rest can build around it. A statistics fact without a number (Statista's paywalled teasers yield `<redacted>`) is dropped by `usable_facts` before it can reach the plan or the graph.

**Visibility.** The build row is re-emitted whole (`population_build`) on every state change and each log line streams as `population_log`; the page also polls every 6 s while a stage runs, and polls the research state every 8 s while the Ingest tab's research is running so a Stop there is reflected. A reload restores the dials the build was started with. The Studio never spawns without an explicit Approve. Layout: three independently scrolling columns on wide screens; below `xl` the page stacks build → sources → dials in one scrolling column with the action bar stuck to the bottom.

### 7.10 Build your own agent — the Agent Builder (brief L3-01 / L3-04, first step)

**Why it exists.** Every persona so far is written by the model (Studio / Pro) or sampled from the bank (Fast). The brief wants twins whose behavioural model is *authored*, not invented (L3-01, L3-04). The Agent Builder is the first step: an analyst writes one twin by hand, decides how much of its psychology to set and how much to leave to the system, and saves it into a **lineup**.

**Where.** A **Build your own agent** button on the Agents tab — on the Studio's empty state (next to *Detect & plan*) and on the roster (next to *Rebuild in Studio*) — opens `/session/{id}/agents/builder` (`components/simulation/AgentBuilder.tsx`).
