**The form (left).** *Who they are*: name, age, job / role, gender, lives in (a real place), household income, education, description (background), relationship to the topic, personality (comma-separated), debate style, stance (direct / indirect / neutral), an **Expert ↔ Reactive** slider (0–100, the humanity dial; left to the system until moved, *auto* releases it again) and *how their place shapes them* (`geo_behavior`, written to the persona as "you"). *Character* (all optional, the brief's L3-01 fields): **how they decide**, **how they behave**, **how they talk**, **where their information comes from**, **where they go wrong**. These are stored as `character` and reach the persona prompt word for word under *"WHO YOU ARE, IN YOUR OWN TERMS — these rules decide how you think and act; never contradict them"* (`agent_runner._character_block`, posts, chat and probes alike).

**The dials (right).** All 112 dials in their nine groups, each a slider. Every dial starts as **auto** (the system's to set); moving one **fixes** it by hand (shown bold, with an *auto* link to release it), a group can be released at once, and *all auto* clears everything. The header counts *N set by hand · M by the system*. **Build sentiment profile** sends the text plus only the fixed dials to `POST /sessions/{id}/agent-builder/profile`; `agent_builder.build_profile` asks the Pro orchestration model (structured tool-use, `analyze`) to set every dial relative to the session's query from what was written — the fixed dials are listed as constraints and come back unchanged (`merge_fixed_dials`), the Expert ↔ Reactive value is suggested unless the analyst fixed it, and a one-sentence **reading** explains the signature. System-set dials show at reduced opacity; moving one takes it over. Editing the text after a build marks the profile *built before your latest edits*.

**Saving.** An agent always belongs to a lineup: **New lineup** (named) → `POST /presets/custom`, or **Add to existing** (a saved lineup from the picker) → `POST /presets/{id}/agents`. Save requires a name, a job and a description; if any dial is still the system's (or the text moved since the last build) the save **builds the profile first**, then stores the full 112-dial profile. `agent_builder.normalise_authored_agent` validates and shapes the profile exactly like `save_preset` does (defaults: age 35, stance neutral, energy random, avatar colour de-duplicated against the lineup, occupation = role); an incomplete profile is a **400**, and a name already in the lineup is a **409** so a twin stays one person (L3-03). After a save the page offers *Build another* (form reset, target kept on that lineup) or *Back to agents*. Loading the lineup from the Studio's empty state brings the agents into the session as usual (`apply-preset`), `character` included.

**Not yet.** No editing of an agent already inside a lineup; the named HCP / patient fields of L3-04 are free text under *Character*, not typed fields. Tests: `agent_builder_test.py` (dial rules, draft validation, prompt block, and an HTTP round trip: profile → new lineup → add → 409 on a repeated name → load into a session).

### 7.11 Archetypes — the Studio casts segments from authored twins (brief L3-02)

**Why it exists.** Build your own agent and the Studio were two factories that did not talk: one human-written twin sat in a lineup while the Studio invented every persona's psychology from scratch. L3-02 makes the Studio **cast copies from a human-written mould**: *twin = archetype template + sampled attribute vector + retrieved local context*, and the model writes texture only.

**The mould.** Ticking **Use as an archetype the Studio can cast a segment from** on an Agent Builder save (on by default) also stores the agent as an `Archetype` (§6.5a) — the lineup save is unchanged, so authored agents still go into lineups, singly or many at a time. `GET /archetypes` lists them with a one-line summary (job · place · Expert↔Reactive · first character rule); `DELETE /archetypes/{id}` removes one.

**Matching at plan time.** After the planner proposes segments, `archetypes.assign_archetypes` matches each segment to the owner's archetypes by **job words only**: the head of the archetype's role ("GP partner in a coastal practice" → "GP partner") and its occupation, with place and setting words (coastal, community, town, deprived, NHS …) dropped, against the segment's name and typical occupations (score 2 per hit) plus its description (1 per hit); a synonym table makes *GP / doctor / physician* or *pharmacist / pharmacy* one job. A job word must hit the name or occupations — a description alone never casts a segment, since every patient segment's description mentions GPs. The best match wins, else the segment stays *model invents*. (The first prod run cast "older coastal residents" from a GP because of the word *coastal* in her role; hence the rule.) The result is stored on the segment as `archetype_id` / `archetype_name` and logged (*Cast from archetypes: 1 of 2 segments*, or a hint to author one). A replacement segment after a rejection is matched the same way. On the plan card every segment shows a **cast from …** / **model invents** chip and a picker to change it (`decide_segment` edit with `archetype_id`, `""` = model invents; the choice is marked `archetype_manual` and survives re-plans); the plan header counts *N of M cast from archetypes*.

**Casting at build time** (`agent_factory.generate_agents_from_plan`, the `spawn:cast` path, when `constraints.archetypes` carries the referenced moulds):
1. **The attribute vector is drawn before any model call.** `archetypes.sample_attributes` gives every persona slot its facts: an age inside the segment's range, a gender honouring the split exactly, a place rotating through the segment's regions, the segment's income and education, and **a cell on every sampling-frame dimension drawn from the published shares** (the segment's own cell 75% of the time when the planner gave one; the age follows the drawn age cell, and income / education / gender cells override the segment's value). Seeded per segment, so a rebuild draws the same facts.
2. **Local context is retrieved per place.** `archetypes.local_context` pulls the graph entities and relations that mention the persona's place and job (`get_kg_context_string` with focus terms — no LLM call) and hands the writer that slice.
3. **The model writes texture only.** `cast_prompt` states the mould — background for tone, personality, debate style, relationship to the topic, Expert↔Reactive, the character rules verbatim, and the 112 dial priors — then the fixed facts per slot, and asks for a name, a 2–3 sentence life story, `correlation`, the place paragraph, a job title adapted to the place and age, the archetype's traits with at most one swapped, and dials moved by at most ±2 where the facts justify it. It may not invent decision rules, information sources or a different temperament.
4. **The rules are enforced whatever comes back.** `enforce_archetype` overwrites the returned facts with the drawn ones, clamps every dial to the archetype's value ±`DIAL_DRIFT` (2), the humanity value to ±`HUMANITY_DRIFT` (10), copies the character verbatim and records `character.archetype = {id, name}`. Cast personas skip the segment's register band and the Expert↔Reactive voice pass (the mould's temperament is authored) and are de-duplicated by name only — siblings share a job and similar ages by design, so the role-at-same-age clone rule does not apply to them. A batch whose slot numbers restart at 1 is paired in order rather than lost. Segments without an archetype are written exactly as before, so a plan mixes cast and invented segments.

**What the analyst sees.** Roster cards carry a **cast from …** chip; the segment chip, demographics line and dials read as usual; the character reaches every prompt through `_character_block` (§7.10). The build log names which segments were cast and warns when a referenced archetype has since been deleted (that segment falls back to the model).

**Not yet.** Matching is by job only (no LLM judgement of fit); templates are whole agents, not a typed library per HCP role or patient cohort class (L3-01 proper); the retrieved context is graph entities, not scoped knowledge units (§9.6). Tests: `archetypes_test.py` (matching and synonyms, manual choice kept, sampling honours the segment and the frame, enforcement bounds the drift, prompt content, slot pairing, a stubbed build with one cast and one invented segment, and an HTTP round trip: author with `as_archetype` → plan auto-matches → pick / clear on a segment → delete).

### 7.12 Behavioural validation — the confidence score beside every twin (brief L3-05)

Code: `backend/app/services/agents/validation.py`; badge: `frontend/src/components/ConfidenceBadge.tsx`.

A twin that sounds right is not the same as a twin that *is* right. After a population is built the
**validation battery runs on its own** — nobody asks for it — and every scored twin carries a
**confidence score (0-100)** wherever it speaks: on its roster card, beside its posts in the debate,
beside its answers in the Lab, above its replies in one-on-one chat, and on the report's trace card.

**The four parts** (each 0-1, blended by weight: stability .30, refusal .25, knowledge .25, register .20;
a part that could not be scored drops out of the blend instead of scoring zero):

| Part | What it asks | How it is scored |
|---|---|---|
| **Stability** | the same question in different words, answered in a **separate call that cannot see the first** | mechanical — the gap between the two 0-10 answers. No judge. |
| **Refusal** | things this twin cannot know (another area's internal figures, an undecided price) | a judge marks *said so* / *hedged* / *asserted it anyway* |
| **Knowledge** | what someone in this role would certainly know | a judge marks the answer against the item's expected answer |
| **Register** | does it talk the way its own record says it talks | a judge scores 0-10 against the persona record |

**Shape of a run.** Items are written **once per segment** (one call; the segment's archetype is shown to
the writer when it has one), every twin then answers them in two separate contexts, and one judge call
scores that twin — **three calls per twin** on the Fast model, at `VALIDATION_CONCURRENCY` (8) in
parallel. A pass covers up to `MAX_SCORED_DEFAULT` (250) twins, picked **round-robin across segments** so
a small segment is never drowned out by a large one; the rest stay unscored rather than guessed at.
Scores are published per twin (`agent_validated`) as they land, so badges fill in live.

**Stored on the twin** (`spawned_agents.validation`, Alembic `0013_validation`): the score and band, the
four parts, the judge's evidence line for each item, **the items themselves and the twin's own answers**,
plus model, seed and prompt hash — so a score can be inspected and reproduced, not just believed.
`GET /agents/{id}/validation` returns the whole record; `GET /sessions/{id}/validation` gives the
population's reading (mean, band mix, weakest part, and a **test-retest agreement** across all twins,
which is `stats.agreement` used as designed — over many pairs, not one); `POST /sessions/{id}/validation`
re-runs it. The Studio logs the population line when the background pass finishes.

**What it is not.** It is fidelity to the twin's own record, not evidence that the population predicts
reality — the badge's own tooltip says so, because a client will otherwise read "confidence" as
confidence in the answer. Two parts lean on a judge that shares the writer's priors; stability is the
only part with nothing in the loop, which is why it carries the most weight. The external check is the
panel back-test (brief §06). **Known gap:** twins are not yet told they may say "I don't know" in the
debate itself (L3-06), so the refusal part is currently the weakest score in most populations — which is
the number that will prove L3-06 worked.

### 7.13 Dissent, refusal and consensus drift (brief L3-06)

**Why it exists.** A synthetic panel agrees too easily: one model writes every twin, they read the same room, and a result can come back 95% one way because the first few answers set the tone, not because the population thinks that. And a twin asked about something outside its life will invent an answer rather than admit it does not know. Both make a number look stronger than it is. Four mechanisms, none of which hides or rewrites a number.

1. **"What you actually know."** `agent_runner._limits_block` adds to every persona prompt (posts, chat, probes) what this twin can speak to — its own job, its own place, its information diet from the authored character (§7.10), what people around it say — and a bounded licence: anything outside that (another area's internal figures, a decision not yet taken, a speciality that is not theirs, an unpublished number) it does not know and must say so, or guess and call it a guess. The block insists in the same breath that on its own ground the twin has a view and gives it, so the panel does not become a room of shruggers.
2. **"Not mine to answer" in the Lab.** Every instrument's answer schema gains `can_answer` (yes/no) and `cannot_answer_why` (`probe.with_dont_know`), and every probe directive carries the rule for using them. A refusal is **a result, not a missing value**: `dont_know_block` reports the share, the coded reasons and who refused, and the refusing twins are **kept out of the denominator** of every other number (`answered`), so a fabricated answer never enters a share we quote. `answer_count` still counts everyone who came back. When nobody could answer, the aggregates say so in one sentence. Experiments exclude refusals from the paired rows, so a lift is measured on twins who answered both arms.
3. **The unanimity check** (`measurement/unanimity.py`, mechanical, no model). A population is built to differ — stances, segments, places, registers chosen because they should behave differently. When the headline share is lopsided (≥ 85% either way) **and** the widest gap between the structural splits the instrument already computed (stance, segment, region, age band, register, income, education; buckets of ≥ 3) is ≤ 8 points, the result is flagged as agreement the population should not have produced, with a one-line reason naming the flattest split. `aggregates.unanimity` carries the verdict either way; the Lab shows the flag **above** the tool's numbers, beside the refusal share, because both change how the numbers below should be read.
4. **Independent voices in the debate.** A fixed share of twins (`independent_voice_share`, default 0.3) never see the thread in any phase: they post from their own head, so the room always contains voices that cannot have drifted toward the majority. Chosen by a hash of the agent id (`orchestrator._independent_ids`), so the same twins stay independent across phases and re-runs. Everyone else, once they have read the room, gets `_DISSENT_RULE`: agreeing adds nothing on its own, say the one thing only you can add or do not repeat the point; if the prevailing view is wrong say so even alone; only a specific fact or argument moves you, and name it.

**What the analyst sees.** In the Lab, a yellow *This population agreed more than it should have* card with the reason when the check fires, a *N% said this was not theirs to answer* block with the reasons and the names, and a plain sentence when nobody could answer. Refusals feed the validation battery's refusal part (§7.12), which previously scored low because no twin could decline.

**Not yet.** The report (§11) does not yet carry the unanimity verdict or the refusal share; the survey instrument reports refusals per form, not per question; the independent share is a setting, not a Studio dial. Tests: `dissent_test.py` (19 — the limits block, the refusal fields and denominator, the unanimity thresholds either way, deterministic independent voices, the dissent rule only when a thread is shown).

---

### 7.14 Population kits — a published segmentation, built straight into agents (2026-10-08)

**Why it exists.** To test the twins against a real poll, the population has to be the poll's population: the same segments, in the same mix, made of people whose facts and convictions come from published research, not from the planner's guesswork. A **kit** is that research, packaged: it replaces detect → gather → frame → plan. Two ship (`backend/app/data/kits/<id>/kit.json`, both from More in Common):
- **`britains_choice_2020`** — the *British Seven* of *Britain's Choice* (Oct 2020; YouGov, n=10,385): Progressive Activists, Civic Pragmatists, Disengaged Battlers, Established Liberals, Loyal Nationals, Disengaged Traditionalists, Backbone Conservatives. These are the segments More in Common's **June 2023 poll for 11 London** is broken down by, so this is the kit that poll is scored with (§11a.8). Share presets: *published_2020* (13/13/12/12/17/18/15) and **`june2023_sample`** (default) — that poll's own weighted segment composition (8.2/11.1/7.5/16.9/22.3/14.2/19.7), a fact about who was sampled, not about what they answered.
- **`shattered_britain_2025`** — the current *Seven Segments* (Shattered Britain, July 2025; moreincommon.org.uk/seven-segments): Progressive Activists 12, Incrementalist Left 21, Established Liberals 9, Sceptical Scrollers 10, Rooted Patriots 20, Traditional Conservatives 8, Dissenting Disruptors 20; plus segment findings from More in Common/Wellcome's 2026 mental-health report.

**What a kit holds, per segment.** `distributions` (age band, gender, region, ethnicity, education, income, settlement, class, religion, EU-referendum vote — each with its page) · `beliefs` (27–29 items, each a first-person *agree* / *disagree* pair with the segment's published `agree_pct`, the UK figure and its source) · `character` (how they decide, behave, talk, where their information comes from, where they go wrong — summarised from the report's profiles and the website) · `findings`, `voice` (short focus-group quotes), `humanity_hint`, temperature, emotions. Population-wide: the GB `frame` (region, age, gender, ethnicity targets) and `assumptions` (what the sources do not say, listed). **Every number is cited**: the kits are generated by `services/kits/authoring/build_*.py` from evidence files in `sources/` (each figure with its report page; chart values read from rendered page images because the PDFs' text layers list chart numbers out of order), and a build fails rather than fill a missing number. Nothing in a kit may come from a benchmark it will be scored against.

**How a kit becomes agents** (`services/kits/registry.py`, `cast.py`, `apply.py`):
1. `plan_from_kit` writes the plan with every segment accepted and draws one **card** per agent (`draw_cards`, seeded): each attribute is a **stratified quota** (largest remainder), so a segment of 45 lands on its published age, gender, region… mix as closely as whole people allow; and each person gets **their own beliefs** — every item lands on its published share, linked through one latent *how typical of the segment* score (+ noise) so convictions cohere within a person without making anyone a caricature. Region and ethnicity use the poll banner's own labels so the results cut the same way.
2. `start_kit_build` (`POST /sessions/{id}/population/kit`) creates the build already at `awaiting_review` with the kit's frame; the analyst reviews and approves as usual (approve re-draws the cards of any segment whose count changed — `ensure_cards`).
3. At spawn, `agent_factory` routes kit segments to `_kit_segment` (`spawn:kit`): the prompt (`kit_cast.cast_prompt`) states the segment (summary, psychology, priorities, information diet, research findings, pen portrait, quotes) and each slot's fixed facts and beliefs; the model writes **texture only** (name, town, job, life, dials), and `kit_cast.enforce` overwrites the facts, pins the region/age/gender/ethnicity/EU-vote cells in `demographics.frame`, clamps humanity to the segment band, and copies the segment's character plus the person's `character.beliefs`. `_character_block` renders those beliefs as **WHAT YOU BELIEVE** in every prompt the twin answers.

**The AI-bias guard.** More in Common gave its segmentation quiz to the big chat models (Feb 2026): ChatGPT and Gemini came out Progressive Activists, Claude, Grok and DeepSeek Incrementalist Left — the socially desirable answer. Hence the per-person beliefs (an Alex in Middlesbrough holds *the government should put British people first* in his own prompt), and the Forms directive now tells every respondent to use *Don't know* when that is the honest answer and to give their own answer, not the kindest or most balanced one (§11a.3).

**UI.** The Studio's empty state offers **Or start from a published segmentation** (`population/KitPicker`): each kit, its segments and a *segment shares* select; *Plan N agents* opens the plan review.

## 8. The simulation engine

Code: `backend/app/services/simulation/orchestrator.py` + `thread_manager.py`. Entry point: `run_simulation(session_id, intensity, mode)`.

### 8.1 The phase engine (intensity ladder)

The simulation is no longer an open-ended, energy-weighted round loop where some agents might never post. It is a **deterministic, coverage-guaranteed phase engine**: every agent is guaranteed to act at least once, and `intensity` adds one bundled action *per agent* per level. `_build_phases(intensity)` expands the ladder:

| Intensity | Phases (each phase = one action by **every** agent) |
|---|---|
| 1 | `comment` |
| 2 | `comment`, `like` |
| 3 | `comment`, `like`, `debate` |
| 4 | `comment`, `like`, `debate`, `reply` |
| ≥5 | …then alternating `debate`, `reply` ("and more like that") |

The run:
1. Load the session `query` and **all** agents; publish `simulation_started {agent_count}`; warm the KG cache. Start a **status watcher** (see below).
2. For each phase (in order): snapshot the thread + KG context **once** (shared by all agents in the phase), then run **every agent's action concurrently**, bounded by a `sim_concurrency(mode)` semaphore (**Fast ≈ 48, Pro ≈ 12** parallel LLM calls). This guarantees one comment per agent at intensity 1 (e.g. 1000 comments for 1000 agents), then a like each, then a debate each, etc.
3. After the phases: only if status is still `simulating`, set `complete` and publish `simulation_complete {message}`.

**Status watcher (`_StatusWatcher`):** a single background coroutine polls session status once a second and exposes an in-memory `stopped` flag. The thousands of per-agent tasks check that flag instead of each hitting the DB — at 1000-agent scale the old per-task status reads (2 DB reads/agent/round) would have been thousands of round-trips. Pause/Stop still take effect within ~1 s.

### 8.2 A single agent's turn — `_agent_action`
- **`like`** (no LLM): increment a random recent post by a *different* agent via `add_like`, publish `like_added`. (Likes never create a post row.)
- **`comment` / `debate` / `reply`** (one LLM call on the mode's agent model): for debate/reply, pick a random recent post by a *different* agent as the parent (falling back to a top-level `comment` if the thread is still empty); generate text via `generate_post(... mode=mode)`; persist via `create_post`; publish `post_created` with the full embedded agent profile.
- **KG enrichment is adaptive:** the per-post KG-update probability is `min(1.0, kg_sim_max_updates / agent_count)` — so any run of **≤ `kg_sim_max_updates` agents (default 120) feeds EVERY post back into the graph** (small/Pro runs capture the whole debate), and only larger populations throttle (e.g. 1000 agents → ~12%) so per-post extraction never saturates the API at scale. Updates run through a `kg_sim_concurrency` semaphore (default 6) as fire-and-forget tasks. *(Earlier this was a flat 15%, which made the KG look empty on small Pro runs — 15% of a handful of posts rounds to zero.)*

### 8.3 Threading & ordering — `thread_manager.py`
- `build_thread_context(posts, agents)` renders the transcript as `[name | role]: content` lines, indenting replies with `> `, skipping likes.
- `create_post(...)` inserts a `SimulationPost`; `add_like(post_id)` increments `likes`; `get_posts(session_id)` returns all posts ordered by `created_at` (not by round — intra-round order reflects whichever concurrent commit landed first).

### 8.4 Pub/sub events emitted during simulation
`simulation_started`, `post_created` (post + full agent), `like_added`, `simulation_complete`. (`kg_updated` is emitted separately by `update_graph_from_post`.)

---

## 9. The knowledge graph

Code: `backend/app/services/knowledge_graph/{lightrag_service,graph_updater}.py`. Despite the `lightrag-hku` dependency, the active implementation is a **lightweight, Claude-powered, per-session JSON graph** (not the heavyweight LightRAG engine).

### 9.1 Storage
One JSON file per session: `LIGHTRAG_DATA_DIR/{session_id}/kg.json`, shape `{ "entities": [...], "relations": [[head, verb, tail], ...], "chunks": [...] }`. Loaded into an in-process cache; disk is the source of truth on cache miss.

### 9.2 Extraction — `insert_chunks`
For each batch, it combines up to 5 chunks (truncated to ~2500 chars) and asks **`model_fast`** (Haiku) to extract entities (1–4 words, 5–12 of them) and relations (3–8 triples). New entities/relations are **deduplicated by exact match** and appended; chunks are retained (last 200). Returns the newly added items so the caller can publish a `kg_updated` event. *(Extraction failures are now logged rather than silently swallowed — see §16.)*

### 9.3 Query & context
- `query_rag(query, mode="hybrid")` — builds a prompt from up to 50 entities, 30 relations, and the last 8 chunks, and asks `model_fast` to answer. Used by **agent spawning** (to ground personas) and the **report generator** (live, keyed on the report question).
- `get_kg_context_string(...)` — a **non-LLM** string of entities + relations, used by the **simulation loop** every round (fast, cheap). *(Deliberate latency trade-off: the loop uses the cached string; the report uses a live query.)*
- `get_kg_data` / `get_entity_details` (case-insensitive) power the Graph tab and node-click inspector.

### 9.4 Feeding the debate back in — `update_graph_from_post`
Each agent post is formatted as `[name | role]: content`, inserted as a single chunk (entities/relations extracted), and a `kg_updated` event is published. This is why the graph grows during the debate and why later rounds can reason over earlier insight.


### 9.5 The ontology — a typed layer over the free-text graph (brief L1-03)

Code: `backend/app/services/knowledge_graph/ontology.py`, routes in `api/v1/sessions.py`, UI `components/knowledge-graph/OntologyView.tsx`. The per-session graph is free text — entity strings and `[head, verb, tail]` triples — which grounds a debate but cannot be reasoned over: "GP", "general practitioner" and "Dr Nandakumar" are three unrelated strings, and "Blackpool" does not know it sits inside the North West. The ontology types that graph **without changing it**.

- **Schema (`SCHEMA`).** Nine classes, each with a colour and a description used verbatim in the classification prompt: `geography` (with containment levels `nation → region → icb → local_authority → lsoa`, plus `place`), `hcp_role` (role or specialty; a named clinician is classified by role), `condition`, `journey_stage` (a stage or a named barrier at one), `intervention`, `channel`, `attitude_segment`, `organisation`, `other`. Twelve predicates with declared domain/range: `within`, `located_in`, `treats`, `has_stage`, `precedes`, `addresses`, `delivered_by`, `reaches`, `holds_view_on`, `affects`, `part_of`, `related_to` (fallback).
- **Build (`build`)** — on demand, not on every ingest. Picks the session's entities (all of them up to 240; beyond that `rank_entities` keeps the best-connected and most-mentioned), then two structured `llm.analyze` passes on `model_fast`: **classify** (one call per 80 entities → class, geography level, and for a place its containing area and that area's level; names must come back exactly as given, anything else is dropped, unknown classes fall back to `other`) and **type** (one call per 100 relations → a predicate per index, given both endpoint classes; unknown → `related_to`). `assemble` (pure) then builds nodes (`id, cls, level, mentions, inferred`) and edges (`head, predicate, tail, verb, inferred`), de-duplicated, self-loops dropped, and **adds the geography containment the sources implied**: a parent that was never an entity becomes an inferred geography node (England at `nation`), and every child gets an inferred `within` edge. Pulse labels `ontology_classify` / `ontology_relate`.
- **Storage.** `kg_graphs.ontology` (jsonb) holds `{nodes, edges, counts, built_at, model, entity_count, relation_count, classified, typed}`. It is not part of the in-process KG cache and is not read by the debate or the report — it is a view for the analyst today; scoping retrieval by class (brief L1-04) is the next use of it.
- **Stale.** `GET …/kg/ontology` returns `{schema, ontology|null, stale, entity_count}`; `stale` is true when the graph's entity count differs from the one the ontology was built from, and the UI also compares against the live count it already holds.
- **UI.** The Graph tab now opens with a **view dropdown**: *Knowledge graph* (the original ring graph, unchanged) or *Ontology*. Before a build the Ontology view draws the **schema itself** as a graph (class nodes in a ring, predicate edges with labels) with the class descriptions and predicate chips on the right, and a *Build from this session's graph* button (disabled with a hint until the graph has entities). After a build: a **swim-lane layout**, one column per class that has nodes (geography ordered by level, the rest by mentions), typed edges as curves with arrowheads (inferred ones dashed), inferred nodes drawn hollow, lane headers with counts, a legend that hides a class on click, and a *Rebuild* button with an amber "graph has grown since this was built" warning. Clicking a node shows its class chip, level, mention count, outgoing and incoming edges grouped with their predicate and the original verb, and the source mentions (from the existing entity endpoint; inferred nodes have none). Errors from the build (empty graph → 400, model failure → 502) render inline.
- **Tests:** `backend/tests/ontology_test.py` — schema integrity, assembly (typed edges, inferred containment, counts, mentions, self-loops/duplicates/unknown endpoints), classify batching + junk fallback, relation typing order + fallback, and the HTTP round trip (schema-only before a build, 400 on an empty graph, build → persisted → stale after the graph grows).


### 9.6 Scoped retrieval — what each twin is allowed to know (brief L1-04)

Code: `backend/app/services/scoping/{registry,profiles,engine,tagger,service}.py`, `api/v1/scoping.py`, UI `components/knowledge-graph/ScopingView.tsx`. Until this, every agent — a Blackpool patient in the most deprived decile and an Oxford consultant alike — was handed the same ranked entities and the same evidence brief, so personas quoted clinical audits they would never have read, identical inputs pushed every agent toward the same conclusion, and nothing could say which twin plausibly knew what. Scoping is one generic mechanism with three parts; none of them knows about "location" specifically.

- **Knowledge carries facets.** `tagger.tag_session` turns every chunk in the session graph (up to 240) into a `knowledge_unit`: `classify_chunk` maps the `[SOURCE …]` header to a provenance class and trust tier (quant → `official_statistic`, social → `social_signal`, personal → `client_data`, synthetic → `model_inference`, PubMed/journal hosts → `peer_reviewed`, ONS/gov.uk/NHS hosts → `official_statistic`, else `grey_literature`; an agent post → `model_inference`, `debate: <name>`), then one structured call per 12 chunks on `model_fast` (`scoping_tag`) assigns facets **only from the ontology's vocabulary** (§9.5 — built first if missing or stale): every place the unit is about, the roles it is for (HCP nodes plus `patient` / `public`), the channels a person would meet it through, conditions, stages, segments, a **register** (`lay | professional | technical`) and a date. Values outside the vocabulary are dropped. Re-tagging wipes and rewrites under a new `snapshot_id`.
- **Dimensions are registered, not hard-coded** (`registry.DIMENSIONS`): geography (hierarchical along the ontology's `within` edges — North West reaches Blackpool), role / channel / condition / stage / segment (set overlap), register (ordinal cap), time (unit date ≤ the twin's `as_of`), provenance (exact; used by rules), project (hard, deny when silent — L1-07 isolation), licence (hard, `unknown` when silent), arm (a unit tagged with an experiment arm is visible only to that arm). Each has match semantics and a default for a silent unit. A new way to scope is one new entry and one facet key.
- **Twins carry an exposure profile** (`profiles.profile_for`): geography from `demographics.region` matched to ontology places, role from the agent's role matched to HCP nodes (+ `public`; non-HCPs → `patient` + `public` when the role reads like one, else `public`), **register cap from the humanity band** (expert → technical, tempered → professional, the rest → lay), channels = every ontology channel for professional/technical twins and only the public-looking ones (`is_public_channel`: press, social, app, pharmacy counter, leaflet …) for lay twins, segment from the Studio segment. Every value records its **basis** ("humanity band defensive → cap lay"). `spawned_agents.exposure` overrides any subset (`PUT …/scoping/agents/{id}/exposure`).
- **Policies match the two and stack** (`registry.validate_rules`, `engine.evaluate`): a rule is `{dimension, when: own | unscoped | any | [values], effect: deny | require | allow (with override[]) | boost (weight) | route (text), note}`. The base match on every dimension decides visibility; rules then drop (`deny`, `require`), re-admit a base failure on soft dimensions (`allow` — never on a `hard` one), adjust the score (`boost`) or set how the twin knows it (`route`). The **public layer** from the L1-04 discussion is one rule: `channel when own → allow, override geography` — a Blackpool twin learns what Oxford gets only if it travelled on a channel the twin uses. The default session policy boosts own-channel units, routes social evidence as hearsay, and ranks the debate's own posts below real sources. Versions are immutable rows; `PUT …/scoping/policy` creates the next one.
- **Retrieval returns text plus a route** (`engine.retrieve`, `knowledge_block`): score = query-term overlap + trust + boosts; each visible unit carries a route ("via Local press", "from a research paper you read", "from people talking about it online") and the block the agent receives opens with *"Where you know each thing from is in brackets."* — which is what turns "Oxford gets it" into a modelled reaction rather than a fact everyone was handed. Hidden units carry the failing dimension and reason.
- **Where it is wired — scoping is a pipeline step, not a side view (2026-09-28; `services/scoping/auto.py`).** Tagging used to happen only when someone pressed *Tag* on the Graph tab, so a population was written, a debate run and a probe answered against one shared block unless the analyst visited that view first. Now: **(1) tagged automatically** — `auto.schedule` runs `ensure_tagged` in the background when ingestion finishes (`ingest_chunks`) and when a research run completes (`loop`), and `ensure_tagged` is awaited before a Studio build writes personas (`builder`, logged as *Knowledge scoped: N units*), before a debate starts (`orchestrator.run_simulation`) and before a probe runs (`probe.run_probe`, with `warm=False` because its own context builder already loaded the graph). It tags when the session was never tagged or the graph has grown since (units ≠ chunks), single-flight per session per event loop, and announces itself over the websocket (`scoping_started` / `scoping_complete` / `scoping_error`); a failed tag is reported and the caller carries on unscoped exactly as before. The Graph tab's *Tag* button stays as a manual re-tag; `POST /sessions/{id}/scoping/ensure` does the same on demand. **(2) Twins are written from what they can reach** — `auto.context_for_segment` builds a stand-in twin from a plan segment (`segment_seed`: first region, first occupation, register band → humanity, segment name), runs the scoped retrieval for it and hands the persona writer that block (headed *WHAT PEOPLE LIKE THIS WOULD ACTUALLY KNOW … write them so they know this and not more*) in place of the whole-graph summary; the archetype cast path does the same per place with the mould's role, falling back to the place-keyword slice. **(3) Every twin records what it can see** — `auto.annotate_agents` runs after a quick spawn, a Studio build and every (re)tag and stores `spawned_agents.knowledge` (Alembic `0015`, local `_ensure_columns`): snapshot id, policy version, visible / total units, the visible unit ids, the routes, the provenance mix, the exposure profile's values and basis, and `written_from` (*scoped* when the persona was written from scoped knowledge, *shared* otherwise). `GET /sessions/{id}/scoping/coverage` serves it for the roster. **(4) The run record says so** — a probe stores `aggregates.scoping {scoped, snapshot_id}` and every outcome record carries `provenance.scoped` / `provenance.scoping_snapshot` (L6-01 / L1-06). **On the Agents tab:** a status pill beside *Start* (*Scoping the knowledge…* / *Scoped · 38 units · each twin sees only what it can reach* / *Scoping failed — running on shared knowledge*) and a **sees 14/38** chip on every twin card whose tooltip lists its routes; clicking the chip opens the Graph tab on the Scoping view with that twin preselected (`KGPanel focus`, `ScopingView initialAgentId`). The debate, the Lab and chat still read `is_scoped` at run time — what changed is that it is now true whenever there is knowledge to scope. The report still reads the shared graph. Tests: `scoping_auto_test.py` (6). Not built: retagging only the new chunks (a stale graph is retagged whole); Ask-Report / chat do not trigger a retag.
- **UI — the Graph tab's third view, *Scoping*.** Left: a Tag / Re-tag button (stale warning when the graph has grown), facet coverage per dimension (click a value to filter), and the unit cards with provenance, register and facet chips. Right: a twin picker; the twin's exposure profile with the basis of every value; **Sees** (units with route, score and the rules that applied), **Hidden** (each with the dimension and reason it failed), **Prompt block** (exactly what the twin is given), and **Policy** (the rules as JSON, saved as a new version; previews and every later turn use it). Before tagging, the view explains the mechanism and lists the dimensions.
- **API:** `GET /sessions/{id}/scoping`, `POST …/scoping/tag` (400 empty graph, 502 model failure), `GET …/scoping/preview?agent_id=`, `PUT …/scoping/policy {rules, note}` (400 on a bad rule), `PUT …/scoping/agents/{agent_id}/exposure {exposure|null}`, `GET …/scoping/retrievals?agent_id=&limit=`.
- **Tests:** `backend/tests/scoping_test.py` — ancestors, every match semantics, the five rule effects incl. hard dimensions, ranking + hidden reasons + the block, profile derivation and override, header → provenance, vocabulary-only tagging, rule validation, and the HTTP round trip (untagged → tag → the lay patient sees the Gazette but not the audit or Oxford → the GP sees the audit → a public-layer rule opens Oxford with a hearsay route → an exposure override moves the patient → the retrieval log → stale after growth).
- **Step two** (not built): replace chunks with extracted claims so provenance can be rendered per figure (L6-03) and the brief becomes a per-profile artefact.

---

## 10. Ingestion pipeline

All four ingestion sources funnel into a shared core: `chunk_text` → `insert_chunks` (per-session KG) → publish `kg_updated` per batch → on completion set status `ready` and publish `ingest_complete`. Code: `backend/app/services/ingestion/` + `app/api/v1/ingestion.py`.

- **Chunking — `text_processor.chunk_text`:** splits cleaned text into ~1200-char chunks with 200-char overlap, preferring sentence boundaries; empty/whitespace → no chunks.
- **Text:** pasted text ingested directly.
- **Document — `document_parser.parse_document`:** routes by extension. Supports plain text (`.txt .md .rst .log .rtf`), `.pdf` (pypdf), `.docx` (python-docx), spreadsheets (`.xlsx .xls .csv .tsv .ods`), `.pptx`, data/markup (`.json .xml .html .svg`), and **images** (`.jpg .png .gif .webp .bmp .tiff …`) which are sent to **Claude Vision** for an exhaustive description (charts, text, data points). Unknown extensions fall back to UTF-8.
- **YouTube — `youtube_extractor.extract_youtube`:** runs `yt-dlp` to assemble a rich document: metadata (title/channel/views/likes/chapters), **transcript** (YouTube captions first via VTT parse with dedup, falling back to **faster-whisper** audio transcription), **thumbnail** Vision analysis, **5 key video frames** extracted via `ffmpeg` and described by Vision, and the **top ~20 comments**. Returns one concatenated text document. (Heavy: needs `ffmpeg`; can take 1–3 minutes.) **All yt-dlp calls force the `android` player client** (`_with_client`) — YouTube's default client no longer exposes captions/formats to yt-dlp, so without this the transcript silently comes back empty.
- **LLM Search — `llm_search.py`:** manufactures source material when the user has none. `categorize_query` classifies the query into one of the four categories (via `model_fast`, defaults to `strategy_stress_test`); `generate_research_paper` produces a dense, section-structured "research paper" (≥1,500 words, via `model_orchestration`) optionally grounded in an uploaded context doc. The UI uses a **generate → preview → ingest** flow (`/ingest/llm-search/generate` returns the paper for preview; the previewed paper is then ingested as text).

---

## 10a. Auto-research: web search and social listening

Code: `backend/app/services/evidence/` (ported from SuperMind's evidence stack — see the `evidence-gathering` skill) + `app/api/v1/research.py` + `frontend/src/components/ingestion/ResearchPanel.tsx`.

**What happens.** When a session is created with `auto_research` (default on; landing-page checkbox), a research run starts in the background:
1. **Frame** (`frame.py`, Claude tool-use): the query becomes 1–7 sub-questions, the responsible bodies, people, places, programmes, aliases, hashtags, stakeholder angles, look-alikes to exclude, and a time window (`live | recent | period | timeless`).
2. **Plan** (`plan.py`): 1–3 web queries (with region), 2–4 Reddit queries (optional `subreddit:` prefix).
3. **Web loop** (`loop._web_loop`): each query runs through the **engine chain** (`search/provider.py` + `engines.py`: Brave API and **Tavily** when keyed — Tavily (`evidence/tavily.py`) turns a `site:` operator into `include_domains` + `include_domains_mode: "filter"`, uses `search_depth: "advanced"` for those domain-scoped statistics hunts (`basic` otherwise; `TAVILY_SEARCH_DEPTH` overrides), asks for 3 chunks per source as the snippet, boosts the plan's `country` and dates hits; then **Claude web search** (`anthropic` engine — Claude's server-side `web_search` tool, keyed by `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, so production always has it; one `model_fast` call per query, hits from the `web_search_tool_result` block, one-line snippets from the model, Pulse label `search`); then DuckDuckGo → Bing → Yahoo → Brave HTML, keyless — measured 2026-09-16: from Railway's datacenter IP DuckDuckGo answers its bot challenge (HTTP 202), Yahoo 500s, Brave 429s and Bing pads over-specific `site:` queries with unrelated pages behind a `b_no` "no results" block (now detected and treated as empty), so the keyed engine sits ahead of them). Rate-limited engines are **benched** (120 s scraped / 15 s keyed); a **plausibility gate** drops results sharing no term with the query. Up to 5 result pages per query are read (`fetch_page.py`: httpx + trafilatura → markdown; PDFs skimmed by Claude; on 403/429/5xx/bot walls/thin pages a **Tavily Extract** retry (`extract_depth: "advanced"`, keeps tables, reranked to the query) when `TAVILY_API_KEY` is set, then a headless-Chromium retry). **Every result is then read whole by the page reader** (`judge_page.py`, one `model_fast` call per result on its full extracted text — up to the fetcher's 20,000 chars, or the search snippet when the page could not be read — a query's results judged side by side, `PAGE_JUDGE_CONCURRENCY` 6, `PAGE_JUDGE_TIMEOUT` 75 s): it rules the page on- or off-topic (look-alikes off), scores relevance 0–1, ticks the sub-questions it serves, and quotes every passage carrying a relevant fact, figure, date, position or reaction *verbatim*. Each quote is located in the page (exact, then whitespace/punctuation-insensitive, then by its first eight words), widened by 200 chars either side, snapped to word boundaries, and merged with neighbours it touches; the windows are stored as `structured.kg_snippets` (with `structured.judge = {reason, read_chars, passages, located, failed}`) and the row is persisted already scored (`on_topic`, `relevance`, `sub_questions`), so the feed card never flips later. Quotes the page does not contain are kept as the model wrote them, after the located ones. If the reader fails, the page is kept at 0.5 with no snippets (`judge.failed`). After each round the **coverage judge** (`judge_web.py`) sees each result's reader verdict and quoted findings (not a 600-char opening any more), ticks covered sub-questions, lists what is missing, and writes up to 3 refined queries; relevance is no longer its call. Max 3 rounds.
4. **Reddit loop** (`loop._reddit_loop` + `reddit.py`): search via Reddit's JSON endpoints (relevance/year + new/month), junk dropped mechanically, the **social judge** (`judge_social.py`) scores every post 0–1 and flags on-topic (posts older than the frame's window are never on-topic), top comments fetched for the best posts, refined query queued; stops at 25 on-topic posts or 6 attempts. **Reddit refuses unauthenticated JSON over plain HTTP (403) from most hosts; the reader then retries the same fetch from inside a headless Chromium page on reddit.com (`_json_browser`), which works. One warm Chromium per process (`_RedditBrowser`, lock-serialised, torn down on error, one calm retry after a 403 burst) — launching a browser per fetch was ~2 s each and drew 403s on comment bursts.** Proper names found by the web judge are passed to the Reddit judge as hints.
5. **Brief** (`brief.py`): one Claude call over the on-topic evidence → summary, key facts, stakeholder **groups** with stance, share, arguments, quotes and demographic signals, overall for/against/mixed, gaps. **Recommendations** (`recommend.py`): 3–6 tools (debate, sentiment/stance, purchase intent, price sensitivity, A/B, conjoint, MaxDiff, Kano, message testing, brand funnel, churn, framing, interviews, campaign game) with confidence, reason and a pre-filled spec (variants, price anchors, segments, attributes).

**Persistence and streaming.** `research_runs` (frame, plan, verdicts, covered sub-questions, budget, brief, recommendations, status), `research_queries` (one row per query with status/engine/counts/note), `evidence` (one shape for every source: `source_class`, `source_ref`, title, author, date, excerpt, full text, structured payload incl. comments, trust tier, relevance, on_topic, excluded, in_graph, query, attempt). Every row is written as it lands and mirrored over the WebSocket (`research_started/frame/plan/query/item/verdict/budget/brief/complete/error`), so the panel fills live and a reload rebuilds it from `GET /research` + `GET /evidence`.

**Budgets.** Defaults: 20 queries, 80 pages, 420 s (`RESEARCH_MAX_*`), `WEB_MAX_ROUNDS=3`, `WEB_RESULTS_PER_QUERY=6`, `WEB_PAGES_PER_QUERY=5`, `SOCIAL_MAX_ATTEMPTS=6`, `SOCIAL_TARGET_ON_TOPIC=25`. Query gap 6 s keyless / 0.5 s keyed. One active run per session; a new start supersedes the old (an old row whose task no longer exists is finalised `interrupted`, not left "stopping").

**Termination guarantees (added 2026-09-15 after a prod run gathered for an hour against the 420 s budget and ignored 100 Stop clicks).** The between-steps budget checks are the normal stop; on top of them: every external await is bounded (`asyncio.wait_for` on search 90 s, both judges 90 s, frame/plan 120 s with `fallback_frame`/`fallback_plan`/`heuristic_judge` fallbacks, brief 240 s, recommendations 120 s — a run lands even briefless), and the gathering loops as a whole sit under a hard wall-clock cap (`RESEARCH_HARD_CAP_SECONDS`, default `max(600, 2×RESEARCH_MAX_SECONDS)`). **Stop always does something**: the first press raises the polite flag (checked between steps); a press while already `stopping` **hard-cancels the task** (status `stopped`, note `force-stopped by user`); a press on a run whose task no longer exists — a restart killed it and the in-memory flag/task maps are empty — **finalises the row directly** instead of returning nothing. A run whose row was deleted mid-flight (session deleted under it) stands down at its next save.

**Into the graph, with provenance.** Only on-topic evidence is chunked into the knowledge graph, each chunk prefixed with a header `[SOURCE web | title | url | date]`. **A web page contributes only the reader's snippets** (`loop._kg_chunks_for`: one chunk per `kg_snippets` window, ingested as soon as the query's pages are judged, while the next query searches), never the rest of the page — the full text stays on the row for the builder (`ResearchMaterial`) and the per-item endpoint; a page judged without snippets falls back to the first 3 chunks of its opening 6,000 chars, as before. The brief (`brief._render`) likewise reads a page through its snippets. The Ingest card, expanded, shows *Read in full · N characters · k passages kept — reason* and the windows under *What went into the graph*. Chunks are prefixed or `[SOURCE social reddit | r/sub | url | date | score]` (comments included). User uploads get `[SOURCE personal | …]`, YouTube `[SOURCE youtube | …]`, and LLM Search papers **`[SOURCE synthetic | model-generated research paper — a prior, NOT evidence]`**. The simulation no longer feeds agents the first 60 entities by insertion order: `lightrag_service.rank_entities` scores entities by overlap with the query + frame terms and by how many chunks mention them, penalising synthetic-only mentions; the orchestrator prepends the **evidence brief** (`brief_for_prompt`) to every agent's context (`agent_runner` context cap raised to 4,000 chars).

**Into the personas.** `POST /spawn-agents` accepts `ground_in_evidence` (default true): in Pro mode the persona prompt receives the brief with grounding rules (match the observed groups, their stances and share, their arguments and phrasings, their place/demographic signals; calibrate sentiment/trust dials to the observed mood). Fast mode is grounded at post time through the shared context. (Archetype clustering and per-agent `evidence_refs` are next.)

**UI.** Ingest tab → **Research** (first tab, default): sub-question checklist ticking as coverage is judged, every query with status/engine/note, judge verdicts with "missing" lists, a budget meter, **Stop** (takes effect within seconds: page reads, comment fetches and further rounds are cut short; what was gathered is still judged, ingested, briefed and turned into recommendations — status `stopping` → `finalising` → `stopped`) / Run again / Add a sub-question; on the right a live evidence feed (source chip, date, relevance, on-topic/off-topic — off-topic kept but greyed, in-graph badge, expand for comments, open, exclude/include), counters (web, Reddit, in graph, stance for/against/mixed), then the **Evidence brief** and **Recommended tools** cards. LLM Search is labelled as a synthetic prior.

**API (all owner-scoped):** `POST /sessions/{id}/research/start {sources?, context?}`, `POST …/research/stop`, `POST …/research/subquestion {text}` (bounded extra run on the frame + new sub-question), `GET …/research` (run + queries + counts), `GET …/evidence?source_class=&on_topic=&limit=`, `GET …/evidence/{eid}` (with full text), `POST …/evidence/{eid}/exclude {excluded}`, `GET …/evidence/brief`, `GET …/recommendations`. `POST /sessions` accepts `auto_research` and `research_sources`.

**Environment:** `BRAVE_API_KEY`, `TAVILY_API_KEY` (optional; **all Tavily traffic — search and extract — goes through one rolling-minute limiter, `TAVILY_RPM` default 95 against the 100/min development-key limit: the 96th request in any minute waits for the oldest to age out, nothing is dropped; a 429 honours `Retry-After` once, `TAVILY_RETRY_AFTER_MAX_S` 70**), `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (enables the `anthropic` engine; `SEARCH_DISABLE=anthropic` turns it off), `SEARCH_PROVIDER`, `SEARCH_DISABLE`, `SEARCH_COOLDOWN_MS`, `SEARCH_API_COOLDOWN_MS`, `BRAVE_API_MIN_GAP_MS`, `WEB_QUERY_GAP_MS`, the budgets above. The backend image is now `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright/python:v1.60.0-noble` (Python 3.12 + Chromium) so the browser fallback and the Reddit browser path work in production; `playwright==1.60.0` must match the image tag.

---

## 11. The report system

Code: `backend/app/services/simulation/report_generator.py` (backend) + the report prompt and parser on the frontend.

- **`answer_report_query(session_id, original_query, question, db)`** fuses four inputs into one Claude call (`model_orchestration`, `max_tokens=4000`): (1) a **live KG query** keyed on the report question, (2) the **handled agent roster** (handle, name, role, age, place, stance, **where the twin came from**, truncated bio), (3) the **full verbatim transcript**, every line handled, and (4) the report question. The system prompt mandates attributing every position to the twin who held it, quoting their words, and extracting concrete metrics. Returns `(answer, sources)`, where `sources` is a provenance string (`"Knowledge graph + N posts from M agents, K cited by name"`). Each call is persisted as a `ReportQuery` row.
- **Citations — a claim traces back to the twin who made it (`services/simulation/citations.py`, brief L3-03).** The model is never asked to type a name: the roster is numbered `A1 … An` and the transcript `P1 … Pn`, and it cites `[[A7]]`, or `[[A7#P12]]` when attributing a specific statement. After the call the handles are rewritten to stable ids — `[[twin:<agent id>]]` / `[[twin:<agent id>|post:<post id>]]` — a handle that names nobody is dropped, and `repair_names` turns any name typed anyway into a citation (an exact full name, or a full-name-shaped mention whose first name belongs to exactly one twin, which repairs a **drifted surname**; possessives keep their `'s`; ambiguous first names are left alone). `collapse_repeats` then folds a name typed beside its own handle ("Gary Pendleton [[A7]]", two adjacent tokens of one twin after repair) into one token, keeping the statement citation if either side had one and any bold markers between them. The stored answer therefore holds ids, not names — the name a reader sees is read from the agent row at render time and cannot drift. **Rendering (2026-09-29):** within one paragraph or bullet the first citation of a twin renders as the full name and every later one as a small marker — `¶` when it points at a statement, the twin's initials otherwise — so "his [[A7#P12]] claim" reads as prose with a clickable pointer rather than the name a third time; the client document names a twin once per paragraph and drops a later statement pointer. The dissent list the model is shown under `== POSITIONS ==` carries the handle only (no name beside it — shown both, the model typed both), the NAMED DISSENT rule asks for one entry per twin opened with the handle once and pronouns thereafter, and the citation rules say to cite a twin once per sentence and put a statement citation after the claim, never between a pronoun and its noun.
- **The trace card (frontend).** Every citation renders as the twin's real name, underlined; clicking it opens a card: name, **origin** (cast from the *…* archetype / hand-authored in the Agent Builder / written for segment *…*), role, age, place, segment, stance, Expert↔Reactive, frame weight, the **verbatim line** being cited with its round, the agent id, and *Talk to this twin* (switches the chat to that persona). Citations resolve in report questions and agent replies too. The KPI parser splits `Label: Value` on a masked copy of the line so a citation's own colon cannot break a metric.
- **Outcome records — the report's computed backbone (brief L6-01; `services/simulation/records.py`).** A report used to be prose with numbers the model typed, pulled out afterwards as "KEY METRICS". Now every figure about the population is an **outcome record**: a generic, computed object — `{id, kind: headline|probe|experiment, instrument, label, question, basis, estimate {metric, label, format share|lift|mean, value, low, high, n}, sentence, distribution, splits, refusals, unanimity, weighted, provenance {model, seed, schema_id, prompt_hash, agents, evidence_mix, frame_level, created_at}, confidence {score, drivers[]}, caveats[], tags {condition?, journey_stage?}}` — shaped from a complete probe (`record_from_probe`) or experiment (`record_from_experiment`, the best variant's lift on the primary metric). `confidence` is deterministic from the record's own numbers (n, interval width, the unanimity flag, the refusal share, the frame level, weighting, model tier; 5–95) and `caveats` come from the same signals plus the frame report's estimated dimensions and thin cells, always ending with the synthetic-population statement. **Every report has at least one record even when no Lab tool was run**: `POST /sessions/{id}/report/generate` first calls `ensure_headline`, which reuses the latest complete **`verdict`** probe while the roster is unchanged (same agent count, no agent newer than the probe) and otherwise runs one now — a hidden instrument (`instruments/verdict.py`) that asks every twin the session question and returns a position (for / against / mixed), a confidence and a one-line verdict; its `for` share with a Wilson interval, splits, refusals and unanimity check become the **headline record**, and the one-liners are copied to `SpawnedAgent.verdict` so the Agent Opinions sidebar agrees with the report. Then every record on file (`records_for_session`: the latest verdict first, Lab probes and experiments newest first; experiment arms are not double-counted) is numbered `R1 … Rn` in an `== OUTCOME RECORDS ==` block of the report prompt, and `FIGURE_RULES` in the system prompt says the model may state no figure about the population that is not in a record, must cite the record right after the figure (`[[R1]]`), and may quote source-material figures with their source. After the call `resolve_handles` rewrites `[[R2]]` into `[[record:<id>]]` (a handle that names no record is dropped) before the twin handles are resolved; `sources` counts the records cited. Ask-Report follow-ups (`report/query`) get the same records block without running the verdict. The pharma fields of the brief's TPO (condition, journey stage) are an optional `tags` object, empty for every non-pharma question.
- **The report structure, wired to the records (brief L6-02; `services/simulation/structure.py`).** The five-section format — **## DIRECT ANSWER**, **## QUESTION**, **## SOURCE MATERIALS**, **## DISCUSSION**, **## OUTCOME** — is now owned server-side (`structure.REPORT_PROMPT`; the request body's `question` is optional and overrides it), and every part of it that can be computed is handed to the model as data and stored as a `structure` JSON object on the `report_queries` row (Alembic `0014`), returned by `report/generate` and `report/history`. **Direct answer:** the confidence label is no longer asserted by the model. `confidence_band` maps the headline record's 5–95 score to HIGH (≥70) / MEDIUM (≥45) / LOW, the system prompt (`STRUCTURE_RULES`) forbids stating one, and any `Confidence: X` the model writes anyway is stripped from the prose and kept only as `direct_answer.claimed_band` for the audit trail. The badge reads *LOW confidence · 30/100 · computed*, its tooltip lists the record's drivers, and clicking it opens the headline record card. **Source materials by role:** `load_evidence` groups the session's non-excluded evidence by class in a fixed order (statistics & datasets, web pages & documents, social & forum posts, client & personal material, model-written material) with counts, on-topic counts, trust mix and the top five items by relevance (knowledge-graph chunk count when there is no evidence row), and `frame_summary` carries the frame level, summary line, sourced dimensions, estimated dimensions and thin cells. The model gets it as `== EVIDENCE BY CLASS ==` and must describe only what is listed; the frontend renders a chip strip, the top items and the frame line under the heading, before the prose. **Discussion with named dissent:** `load_positions` reads the headline verdict probe's answers (refusals excluded) — every twin's for / against / mixed, confidence and one-line verdict. The majority is the most common position (ties break for → against → mixed) and the **dissent is everyone who did not hold it, most confident first, capped at eight**. The model sees `== POSITIONS ==` with the dissenters by handle and their verdict lines and must name its dissent from that list (never promote a majority twin or invent a minority); the frontend renders a for/mixed/against bar with counts, a *headline record* link, and the named dissent as clickable twin citations (trace card) with position, confidence and the quoted line. **Metrics:** the outcome records (L6-01). **Outcome / conclusion-changing caveats:** `caveats_from_records` takes the union of the cited records' computed caveats (every record when none is cited), deduplicated in first-seen order with the synthetic-population statement last, each carrying the record ids it came from; rendered as **What would change this · computed from the records** at the end of the document with a record link per line. The model still writes its own 2–3 analytic caveats in the OUTCOME prose and is told not to repeat the computed ones. Ask-Report follow-ups are unchanged (prose, no structure). The frontend `ReportDocument` takes `structure` and falls back to the model's own label only for reports written before it existed. Tests: `structure_test.py` (7) and the HTTP round trip in `records_test.py`.
- **Source figures with a provenance class (brief L6-03; `services/simulation/figures.py`).** Three kinds of figure can appear in a report and a reader can tell them apart at a glance. **Population figures** are the outcome records (class `model_inference`, teal; the chip tooltip and the record card now say *model-inferred · counted from the synthetic twins*). **Source figures** are numbers read from the material: the report model is handed two more numbered ledgers — `== SOURCE FIGURES ==`, every typed statistic the Studio extracted (`F1 … Fn`: value, what it measures, group, geography, year, source, class, verbatim quote; `Evidence.structured.facts`, a fact without a digit in its value is skipped) and `== SOURCE DOCUMENTS ==`, every non-excluded evidence item (`E1 … En`: class, trust tier, title, author, date, excerpt), on-topic and most relevant first, capped at 60 facts / 40 items — and `SOURCE_FIGURE_RULES` says a document figure is cited right after it with `[[F3]]` (preferred: exact value, year, source) or `[[E5]]` (the item it was read in), never invented. The class comes from `class_for_evidence`, the same host rules the scoping tagger uses on chunk headers (quant → official statistic; social → social signal; personal → client data; synthetic → model inference; PubMed / BMJ / Lancet / DOI hosts → peer-reviewed; ONS / gov.uk statistics / NHS Digital / Fingertips → official statistic; else grey literature) — the brief's L1-05 vocabulary, now rendered in output. After the call `[[F2]]` → `[[fact:<evidence id>#<k>]]` and `[[E3]]` → `[[evidence:<id>]]`, dangling handles dropped. **Unsourced figures:** last of all, `mark_unsourced` walks the answer sentence by sentence and wraps every number-shaped token (a percentage, money, a magnitude like *3.1 million*, *8 points*) whose sentence carries no citation at all as `[[unsourced:<token>]]`; a `95% CI` and a year are not figures. The flagged tokens go into `structure.figures.unsourced` beside `facts_cited` / `items_cited`, and `sources` counts them. `GET /sessions/{id}/figures` serves the ledger so the frontend resolves the tokens: a fact chip shows the value, its class and source (sky for official statistics, violet peer-reviewed, slate grey literature, orange social, pink client data), an evidence chip shows the class and title, and an unsourced figure renders struck through in grey with *· unsourced* and the tooltip *typed by the model — no record, statistic or document behind it*. Clicking a source chip opens a **source card**: class and trust tier, the statistic, the value with group / geography / year, the verbatim quote, the source and a link. A **figure key** line above every report explains the colours and counts the unsourced figures. Ask-Report follow-ups get the same ledgers and the same check. **Answers traceable to the documents the twin drew on (2026-09-29; L6-05 proper).** When a session is scoped, a probe no longer hands a twin a plain knowledge block: `scoping.service.sources_for_agent` numbers every item `[S1] … [Sn]` and returns the served items (unit id, source, provenance class, trust, text, route); `probe.with_sources` adds a `sources_used` array to every instrument's schema and `SOURCES_RULE` tells the twin to list only the numbers it actually drew on; after the call `resolve_sources` stores on the answer `used_units` (the cited documents themselves), `served_units` (everything it was given) and the cleaned `sources_used`. Every probe's aggregates carry `sources_used` — the roll-up of cited units with how many twins cited each (`records.sources_used`) — and every outcome record carries it as `sources`, so the headline verdict is traceable twin by twin to documents, not only to twins. The Barriers tool's `trace_evidence` now prefers what the citing twins cited (`basis: used`) and falls back to the could-see inference only when none of them cited anything (`basis: could_see`, labelled as such on the page). Pages: the Verdict and Barriers pages show *drew on: official statistic (ons.gov.uk/…) · social signal (…)* under each twin; the record card lists *Documents the twins drew on · by their own citation*. Tests: `sources_test.py` (3). Not built: extracting typed claims from web pages and papers (only the Studio's statistics search produces typed facts today, so a figure from a paper cites the paper, not the number); a `commissioned_research` class is in the vocabulary but nothing assigns it. Tests: `figures_test.py` (5), the HTTP round trip in `records_test.py`.
- **Export, client report and the delta view (brief L6-06) with the non-removable synthetic-population statement (brief L6-07; `services/simulation/export.py`, `delta.py`).** *Export data* (`GET /sessions/{id}/export.zip`) downloads one zip: `run.json` (the run record — question, population size and build, frame level with matched / weighted / estimated dimensions and effective n, evidence counts by class, scoping snapshot, every completed probe and experiment with instrument, schema, seed, model, prompt hash and whether it ran scoped, every report proper), `records.json` (every outcome record), `records.csv` (one row per record: estimate, interval, n, weighted figure, refusals, unanimity, confidence, frame level, scoped, model, seed, the equity cells and gap, the top barrier, documents cited, caveats), `splits.csv` (one row per record × cut × cell with interval, n and thin flag), `report.json` (the latest report proper with its structure), `report.md` (the client document), `sources.json` (the figure ledger), `roster.csv` (every twin with segment, region, deprivation cell, weight, validation score and what it could see) and `STATEMENT.txt`. **The statement** is built server-side from the run (`statement(run)`: how many synthetic twins, what the population was matched or weighted to and what was model-estimated, what evidence it had, that every number was counted not typed, and what the output must not be used for — evidence of real opinion, a substitute for real research, clinical / regulatory / safety decisions, describing an individual) and stamped into every file: a `statement` key in every JSON, a comment line atop every CSV, top and bottom of the document. *Client report* (`GET /sessions/{id}/report/client`, page `/session/{id}/report/client`): a clean light print-ready page with none of the working controls — the statement, the question, the computed confidence, the records with intervals and equity, *What's in the way*, the narrative with every citation resolved by `resolve_citations` (twin → name; record → figure with interval and *model-inferred*; fact → value, statistic, source, year and class; evidence → title and class; unsourced → *typed by the model, not counted*), the caveats, the statement again; `client_markdown` writes it and `report.md` in the zip is the same text. *Compare runs* (`GET /sessions/{id}/report/delta?a=&b=`): two reports proper — b the latest, a the one before by default, any pair by id — reshaped from their own records (`records.records_by_ids`: probes never change once complete) and compared by `delta.compare`: the headline then → now in points and **whether the change is real** (the two intervals do not overlap), the position mix, the equity gap, the dissenters who joined or left, the barriers that appeared, dropped or moved rank, the evidence base by class, the computed confidence band, the unsourced-figure count, the roster size, plus a one-sentence summary; rendered on the Report tab as plain then → now rows. Tests: `export_test.py` (4).
- **Equity stratification by default (brief L6-04; `services/population/equity.py`).** Every result is reported by deprivation level as well as the headline, without anyone asking for it. *The frame:* after the planner picks its dimensions, `ensure_dimension` inserts a **deprivation** dimension (key `deprivation`, attribute `deprivation`, flagged `equity: true`) inside the top three so it is matched exactly rather than only weighted; a dimension the planner already chose for deprivation is renamed to the shared key rather than duplicated. Fifths by default (`Q1 most deprived … Q5 least deprived`), tenths (`D1 … D10`) only when the build targets 200+ twins (`level_for`). The statistics search hunts the place's real profile (gather dimension `deprivation`, fallback query *English indices of deprivation 2019 <place> IMD deciles share of neighbourhoods*; ONS, Nomis and the gov.uk publishers cover it), and after `derive_targets` the builder calls `ensure_target`: a found or uploaded table whose labels name ranks is merged into the level's cells (a decile table becomes fifths), and **nothing on file becomes the national reference** — equal cells, which is how IMD deciles are built — stored as status `estimated`, provenance `model_inference`, `reference: true`, with a note telling the analyst to upload the local IMD profile; the ladder therefore never leaves deprivation unmatched, and the Studio's frame card shows an *equity · always on* badge on the dimension. *The twin:* the persona writer places every twin in one cell (`demographics.frame.deprivation`) and `frame_block_for_prompt` appends `persona_note` — write the persona so the cell shows in housing, work security, transport, cost pressure, health and distance to services, without naming the index. `rank_of` reads any spelling back to a rank (`Q1 most deprived`, `D7`, `decile 3`, `2nd quintile`, `most deprived 20%`, `IMD 1-2`), rescaling a decile into a quintile frame, so `category_of`, `agent_cell`, the weights and the Lab agree. *The result:* `deprivation` is a base split key (`probe.segments_for` writes the canonical cell label from the twin's demographics), so every probe, experiment and headline verdict is cut by it at no extra cost; `record_from_probe` / `record_from_experiment` attach an **`equity` block** — cells in rank order with share, interval, n and thin flag; `most` and `least`; the `gap` in points; `significant` when the two intervals do not overlap; `thin` cells — or `{available: false, reason}` for a population built without a frame (quick spawn), which also becomes a caveat. The records block gives the model one equity clause per record (`equity: Q1 most deprived 20% (n=6) vs Q5 least deprived 80% (n=6), gap -60 points — a real gap`), and `FIGURE_RULES` says the gap may be stated only as the record gives it, never read into an unavailable line. Frontend: an **Equity** strip under the records grid (every record's most vs least deprived cell, gap, *real gap* / *not distinguishable*), an equity line on each grid card, and the record card opens with the deprivation split as bars (thin cells dimmed) before the other splits. Not built: an equity split on the Lab result pages themselves (they show `deprivation` as an ordinary segment split); deciles on the reference target when the build is large; a place outside England (the label still says IMD). Tests: `equity_test.py` (7).
- **The frontend `ReportDocument` parser** turns that into a styled briefing: a teal "direct answer" callout with the computed confidence badge and its drivers in words, the *what this report rests on* line, the *At a glance* tiles, the formatted sections with their wired blocks, and every record folded at the end (see the next bullet). The old prose-scraped KPI grid is gone — a `Label: value` line in the prose is now just prose.
- **Report overhaul — understandable, and dynamic to what was run (2026-09-30).** Drishan: the report showed KPIs at 0 when tools had not been used, duplicate cards, and read as a ledger; *do all of them* on the nine-point assessment. What changed, in reading order:
  - *Reading order.* Direct answer → confidence pill with **why** (`because 40 twins answered · wide interval relative to the estimate · …`) → **what this report rests on** → **At a glance** → the prose (Question, Source materials, Discussion, Outcome) → the computed blocks (barriers, drop-off, commitments, caveats) → **All outcome records**, folded by default. The figure key lists only the classes the report actually uses and sits under the tiles, not above the answer.
  - *What this report rests on* (`structure.coverage`, `structure.coverage(records, posts, evidence_items)` / `coverage_line` / `coverage_block`): one line naming the tools that produced records ("a debate of 80 posts, a verdict poll of 40 twins, a journey with 4 candidate steps, 3 lever runs, 2 message tests") and the tools that were **not run** (barriers ranking, behaviour ranking, committed outcome…), each with what it would add on hover, "needs a journey first" where it does, and an *open the Lab* button. The same block goes to the model as `== WHAT WAS RUN ==` and a new `STRUCTURE_RULES` clause: say a tool was not simulated where it matters, never estimate what it would have shown; a record whose "in words" line says nothing moved is reported as nothing moved, not a shift of zero. The client document carries the line as **What this report rests on**.
  - *At a glance* (`SummaryStrip` / `tilesFor`): one tile per question the tools answered, not one card per run — *The answer* (verdict with "could be 3–20%" and the twin count), *Where most drop off* (the step with most stuck, people, the share reaching the end), *Biggest barrier*, *Best lever*, *Best message*, *Most responsive behaviour* (each reading its record `summary` — "No message moved the twins (4 messages tested)" — so a flat result is words, never "0 pts"), *Widest equity gap* (only a real one), *Committed forecast*. A tile shows "N runs" when the tool was run more than once at that step; every tile opens its record card.
  - *One record per tool and step* (`records.latest_runs`, `records_for_session(latest_only=True)`): re-running a message test on the same step used to add a second card beside the first. Now the newest record of each (kind, instrument, label) is kept and carries `runs: {count, superseded: [ids]}`; the verdict was already one per session and a commitment (a frozen copy) is never folded. Superseded runs stay on file: the export passes `latest_only=False` so `records.json` / `records.csv` keep every run, an old report's records still load by id (`records_by_ids`), and *Compare runs* still reads them. The records block tells the model "latest of N runs of this tool at this step".
  - *No zero for the uncounted* (`records._opt`, `_fmt_value`, `export._fmt` / `_conf` / `_equity` / `_people_range`): a number that was never counted is `None` and reads "not counted" / "interval not counted" / "gap not computed" / "confidence not computed" — never `0`, `0–0%` or `None/100`. Lever, targeting, messaging, commitment and A/B records use `_opt` for value/low/high; a targeting or messaging run with no rankable arm has a `None` value and a label saying so. Every shift record carries a reader `summary` (`_shift_summary`, `_ranking_summary`: "No distinguishable shift from 'door-knock'", "'Free liners' +12 pts (1 message tested)", "'bus pass' +10 pts if the assumption holds"), shown in place of the number on tiles, cards and the client document when the shift is not distinguishable from zero; a record chip in the prose reads "no shift" for a flat lift. The delta reports `None` (rendered "not polled" / "not checked" / "not cut") where a side has no record, and gains a `coverage` row (tools run since).
  - *Confidence re-examined* (`records.confidence_for(value=…)`, `POOR_FRAME_CAP = 65`): the interval is judged relative to the estimate — 3–20% around 8% spans 17 points but is twice the figure, so it is "wide interval relative to the estimate (3–20% around 8%)", not "tight"; tight needs width ≤ 20 points **and** no wider than the estimate; a panel whose frame match is *poor* is capped at 65 ("capped below HIGH: the panel is poorly matched to published distributions"), so it can never read HIGH. The band thresholds are unchanged. The Manchester food-caddy verdict that read HIGH 80/100 now reads MEDIUM.
  - *Plain words for every unit* (`GLOSSARY`, `<Term>`): n, the 95% interval ("could be 3–20%" on tiles), pts, Q1/Q5, the gap and "real" / "not distinguishable", confidence, movable / needs the system / structural, barrier weight, effective sample, population match, runs, shift, weighted — each a hover explanation wherever it appears. Reader-facing renames: *headline record* → *verdict record*, *Frame:* → *Population match:*, *n=40* → *40 twins*, *A/B lift* → *A/B test*, and every internal record kind has a plain label (Lever run, Behaviour ranking, Message test, Committed outcome). "Agents" is now "twins" throughout the Report tab (the chat mode is *Talk to a twin*; the picker says *Pick a twin…*).
