  - *Session-specific follow-ups* (`structure.follow_ups`, `structure.follow_ups(records, positions, names)`): up to five questions written from the records — the step where most stall ("Why do 14 of 25 twins stall at 'Tries it → Uses it consistently'?"), the top barrier, the strongest dissenter by name, where the best lever's gain goes, the widest real equity gap, a message test that moved nobody — shown in the rail beside a finished report (which used to offer nothing) and in place of the generic starters; the generic list is the fallback and now speaks of twins.
  - *Small fixes.* Messaging and targeting records carry `equity: None` and the records block now says "not cut by deprivation for this kind of record" (it used to tell the model the population had no deprivation levels); the frontend reads `null` the same way. The synthetic-population statement counts ingested text passages (`run.ingested_chunks`) beside evidence items, so it no longer says "no evidence items" beside 86 documents. A commitment with nothing on file reads "no evidence items and no calibration rules on file"; a barrier with no weight omits the weight; a candidate with no counted share says so; `NaN%` can no longer render. `structure.question.asked/answered` are `None`, not 0, when no verdict poll ran. The equity line on the drop-off block and every `n=` are hoverable terms.
  - *Tests:* `records_test.py` (+4), `structure_test.py` (+3), `export_test.py` (+3).
- **Interaction:** the Report tab is a split screen — the document on the left, a chat panel on the right offering two modes (**Ask Report** → `report/query`, **Talk to Agent** → `agents/{id}/chat`). Plus **Regenerate** and **Save as PDF** (a dedicated print stylesheet remaps the dark theme to a clean white document). Assistant replies in both chat modes render **markdown** (bold/italic/code, bullets, headings), not raw text. If an LLM call fails (bad key, rate limit, overload), the backend returns a **friendly message** (HTTP 200) instead of a 500, and the frontend API client applies a **120 s timeout** with clear network/timeout errors — so a failed question shows a readable note rather than "Failed to fetch" or an endless spinner.

---

## 11a. The Behaviour Lab — probes, instruments & experiments

Until now the product's only structured output was the **112 dials assigned at spawn**, which are *priors, not results*. The Behaviour Lab turns the population into a measurement instrument: ask each agent a structured question **once**, store the typed answer, then compute everything else deterministically with no further model calls.

### 11a.1 The probe primitive
A **probe** is a spec — instrument, question, answer schema, stimulus, context policy, agent filter, seed — run against a set of agents, returning one typed answer per agent plus aggregates. One mechanism, not a family of features: an A/B test is the same probe against two stimuli; a price slider is a probe that elicited each agent's reservation price once, then a curve you drag for free.

`services/measurement/probe.py` runs it: select agents → snapshot context once → answer every agent concurrently (`PROBE_CONCURRENCY = 32`) → persist each answer and stream it over the session websocket → aggregate in pure Python → store the aggregates on the probe row.

### 11a.2 How an agent answers (why the personas stay coherent)
The answer call reuses **the agent's own system prompt** — persona, dial-driven behavioural rules, humanity band — via `agent_runner._build_system_prompt(agent, task="probe")`. `task="probe"` swaps the Reddit-post instruction for a band-specific *how you answer this* directive, so the register carries over: a `reactive` agent answers from the gut in one line, an `expert` deliberates. The user message then shows the agent what it actually knows and has already done:

- **the topic** and the same ranked KG context + evidence brief the debate agents get;
- **what it said publicly** — its own last 3 posts, with an instruction to stay consistent or say why it changed its mind;
- **what it already decided privately** — its last 4 probe answers in this session, so the agent who said £9 was its limit does not accept £14 an instrument later;
- **its commercial dials, explicitly labelled priors, NOT the answer** — so a 9/10 purchase-intent persona can still refuse a bad price;
- **the stimulus** (with the asking price when there is one).

Two rules keep the numbers honest: **reason before number** (every schema puts a short `reasoning` field first, so the model anchors to a stated reason instead of clustering on the midpoint) and **the answer is private** (nobody in the debate sees it, so there is nothing to perform — a socially-cautious agent can admit what it would really do). The answer comes back through one Claude **tool-use** call whose `input_schema` is the instrument's schema (`services/evidence/llm.analyze` + `coerce`), so the output is always valid, correctly-typed JSON; an answer that overruns its token budget is retried once with double the room (capped at 16k) before `LlmTruncated` is raised. A call that fails after one retry **drops that agent from the denominator** — never a default value, which would corrupt the share.

Context is switchable per probe via `spec.context` (`{kg, own_posts, prior_answers}`).

### 11a.3 Instrument library
**An instrument is a self-contained tool.** It declares its own **inputs** (`InputField`, which may carry `suggestions` — ready-made phrasings the UI offers as chips), its own answer **schema**, its own **aggregator**, its own **KPIs** (`Kpi`), its **metrics** (`Metric` — the per-answer numbers an A/B test compares, one marked `primary`), optionally a `decision_key` / `driver_key` (which answer fields count as "the decision" and "why", for flip analysis), a `stimulus_key` (which input holds what the agent is shown), an optional `question_from` (the ask comes from the spec — the analyst writes the question), an optional `postprocess` hook (population-level coding after the run), a `hidden` flag (internal tools stay out of the picker), and the **page** that renders its results. The probe runner, the statistics library and the Lab shell know nothing about any particular instrument — the same rule that keeps the debate generic (§8). Tools differ; the spine stays generic.

There is no shared "stimulus + price" form: purchase intent declares a stimulus because purchase intent needs one, and a conjoint will declare attributes and levels instead. `GET /lab/instruments` publishes the whole declaration, so the UI builds its input panel and its headline strip from it and a new tool reaches the product **without the Lab shell being touched** — one backend file, plus optionally one page component. An instrument with no page registered falls back to a generic renderer (declared KPIs + any distributions its aggregator produced), so it is usable before its bespoke UI exists. Required inputs are validated from the declaration, so the API has no opinion about stimuli or prices either. Shipped so far:

| Instrument | The agent answers | The client sees |
|---|---|---|
| **`purchase_intent`** (`v1`) | `reasoning`, `would_buy` (yes/no/unsure), `likelihood_0_100`, `max_price` (own walk-away price, in the offer's currency), `key_driver` (price/need/trust/social/convenience/identity/risk), `sentiment` (-1..1) | Share who would buy with a Wilson CI; decision and driver mix; mean likelihood, walk-away price and feeling with bootstrap CIs; a **demand curve** + revenue-optimal price derived from the walk-away prices; the share whose own walk-away price clears the asking price, with a **contradiction count** where that disagrees with what they said; segment splits; verbatims grouped by answer; one plain-language sentence |

| **`survey`** (`v1`) — **the generic tool** | The analyst's own form: any number of questions of type `single`, `multi`, `scale` (min–max with end labels), `yesno`, `number`, `text` (free words → coded `theme`), `grid` (rows × columns, the Likert matrix). Every persona completes the whole form in **one call**; the answer schema is generated from the form (reasoning first, then one typed field per question; a grid is a nested object of row → column) | **Per question, by type:** single/yes-no → distribution with Wilson CIs in form order; multi → share ticking each option + mean options picked; scale → mean with bootstrap CI, one bucket per point, **top-two / bottom-two box**; number → mean CI + binned histogram; text → coded themes + every response; grid → a distribution per row. The question marked **primary** leads the summary and gets segment splits. Plus every individual response |
| **`barriers`** (`v1`) — ranked barrier attribution (brief L6-05) | `reasoning`, `blocked` (yes / partly / no), `barrier` (3–10 words), `removal` (what would have to change), `weight` 0–100 | **The outcome** is the one input (default: the session question, with suggested phrasings). After the run `postprocess` codes `barrier` and `removal` into at most seven shared labels (`themes.apply_themes`, stored as `barrier__theme` / `removal__theme`), then `trace_evidence` attaches to every barrier the knowledge units its citing twins could actually see (each twin's scoped retrieval for the barrier's words, L1-04) that share a word with the barrier — counted across the twins, top five kept with source, provenance class and trust; quiet when the session is not scoped. **Results (`lab/pages/BarriersPage`):** share who see a barrier with its interval; the ranked list — barrier, how many twins, share, mean weight, a bar, what would remove it — ordered by count then weight; open one for the twins who raised it (their own barrier phrase, removal, weight, reasoning, deprivation cell) and the evidence lines; the same *Read by* cut picker as the Verdict, which recounts the ranking inside the cut. Declares `blocked` (share, primary) and `weight` as metrics, so it is A/B-testable. **The record:** `record_from_probe` carries `barriers[]` (theme, count, share, weight, removals, agent ids, evidence) and `outcome`; the records block adds *barriers ranked: 1. … 2. …* and `FIGURE_RULES` says that list is the only source for what stands in the way, in that order, cited. **The report:** `structure.outcome.barriers` (the newest barriers record) renders as **What's in the way · ranked by the twins** under the outcome — a plain numbered list, no colour: barrier, count, weight, removals, the first four twins as the usual underlined citations, the evidence classes. |
| **`journey`** (`v1`) — candidate outcomes, one per drop-off (brief L7-01) | `reasoning`, `answering_for` (myself / people I serve), `reached` (the furthest step actually reached, an enum built from the journey), `progress` (yes / unlikely / no — will the next step happen), `barrier`, `removal`, `weight` 0–100 | **The journey** is the one input: the ordered steps a person passes through on the way to the outcome in the question (3–8, `stages[]` of `{label, definition}`, keyed `step1 …`). It is **proposed, then edited**: the tool's own builder (`lab/forms/JourneyBuilder`, registered through `form: "journey"`) calls `POST /sessions/{id}/journey/suggest` on first open — one strong-tier call (`journey.suggest_stages`: the question, the evidence brief, the sampling frame block and the knowledge graph → `{outcome, stages, basis, grounded}`) — and the analyst renames, reorders, adds or removes steps; the brief's clinical template comes out of a treatment question, take-up steps out of a service question, nothing is fixed. `schema_for` fills the `reached` enum from the steps and `question_for` renders them into the ask (so they are in the prompt hash); `validate` bounds the count and rejects duplicate names. A professional twin answers for the people it sees. **Counting (`funnel_of`, `transitions_of`, `candidates_of`):** the funnel is the share of everyone who reached each step (cumulative, Wilson); for every step → next step the at-risk set is everyone who reached the step or beyond, *through* is everyone past it plus those at it who say yes, *stuck* is those at it who say unlikely / no; the conversion is through / at-risk with its interval, the gap is 1 − conversion; barriers among the stuck are coded (`apply_themes` on `barrier` and `removal`, up to twelve labels shared across every step) and ranked by the Barriers ranker (`rank_barriers(blocked_by=…)`), then traced to what the citing twins cited or could see (`trace_evidence(collect=…)`, the candidates rebuilt from the transitions so both carry the evidence); every transition carries its own splits and deprivation block. A **candidate outcome** is every transition with at least one twin stuck — *share of those at ‹step› who reach ‹next step›* — ranked by how many are stuck, then by the size of the gap, then journey order, each with a stable id `<probe id>:<from>-><to>` so L7-02 / L7-03 / L7-07 / L7-08 can build on the same object. Headline: share reaching the last step. Declares `progress` (share, primary) and `weight` as metrics, so a message or a lever can be A/B-tested on it. **Results (`lab/pages/JourneyPage`):** the funnel (one bar per step), the sentence, then the candidates ranked — the step, the conversion with its interval, stuck of at-risk, a through/stuck bar, the barrier labels, the equity line and the computed confidence; open one for the candidate outcome in words, its barriers ranked, and under each the twins who raised it (their phrase, removal, weight, whether they answered for the people they serve, the deprivation cell, what they drew on) and the evidence; the same *Read by* picker recounts the funnel and the ranking inside the cut. **The record:** `journey[]`, `funnel[]` and `candidates[]` (`records.candidate_summary`: id, rank, step, conversion, interval, gap, stuck, the barriers, the equity block and a confidence from the transition's own numbers); the records block adds *candidates ranked: 1. … (x% get through, k of n stuck, top barrier …)* and the *funnel*, and `FIGURE_RULES` says that list is the only source for where the population drops off and which outcomes are worth pursuing. **The report:** `structure.outcome.candidates` (the newest journey record) renders as **Where the population drops off · candidate outcomes** — the funnel in one line and a plain numbered list: step, conversion with interval, stuck of at-risk, the equity gap, the top barriers with the twins who raised them as citations; the client document and `report.md` carry the same section, `records.csv` a `top_candidate` column. **Headcounts — the gap in individuals (brief L7-02; `services/measurement/headcount.py`).** After the run (`postprocess`, last) `headcount.attach` multiplies the shares through a denominator it never invents: the **in-scope slice** the Studio read from the statistics on file (`frame.sizing.sam` as a count, or as a share × `tam`; `parse_figure` reads "141,000", "1.2 million", "26%"), or a figure the analyst typed with a source (`spec.denominator {people, source, label}`, basis *client supplied*; `validate` refuses a typed figure without a source or one that is a share). A **known headcount for a step** (`stages[].people` + `people_source`) is held fixed and the steps after it are scaled from it by the panel's shares (basis *client anchored*); the transition into an anchored step is the difference of the two figures with no interval. Where the twins carry **frame weights** (L2-02) the weighted share is used (`weighted_reached`, Wilson at the effective n) and the sentence says so. Written onto the aggregates: `headcount {available, denominator, anchors, weighted, ess, sentence | reason}`, `people / people_low / people_high / basis` on every funnel step, `at_risk_people / through_people / stuck_people / stuck_low / stuck_high / basis` on every transition and candidate. With no denominator and no anchor, `available: false` with the reason and the shares stand alone. **Where it shows:** the builder shows the frame's figure with its source (from `journey/suggest`'s `denominator`) or lets the analyst *type my own* and add a known headcount per step; the results page shows ≈ people beside every step and every candidate with the basis in the tooltip and the headcount sentence under the funnel (a cut shows shares only); the record carries `headcount` and the people fields on each candidate; the records block adds *≈N people stuck (low–high)* and a *headcounts:* line, and `FIGURE_RULES` lets the model state a number of people only where the record gives one, with its denominator, and never when the record says headcounts are not available; the report block, the client document and `records.csv` (`top_candidate_people`, `headcount_basis`) carry them; `run.json` carries the frame's sizing; the synthetic statement says a headcount is a denominator multiplied by a simulated share and no more real than the share. **Movability — how much of each gap a plausible lever can reach (brief L7-03; `services/measurement/movability.py`).** Last of all (`postprocess`, after headcounts) `movability.attach` puts one question to every coded barrier across the transitions — *who could reach the removal the twins asked for?* — in one strong-tier call (`classify`: each barrier with its coded removals and up to three twins' own "barrier → removal" phrases; barriers with no removal are `none` without asking; a class the call did not return or returned badly is `unscored`, never guessed): `partner` (one partner could deliver it alone: a nurse phone line, a pharmacy follow-up, a transport subsidy, a simpler regimen, a message), `system` (needs the wider system: capacity, funding, commissioning, regulation — the prompt is strict that new public money or workforce is `system`), `structural` (outside anyone's control in a 1–3 year horizon: income, geography, the condition, culture). Each barrier keeps `reach`, `lever` (3–8 words), `actor` (who could pull it) and `reach_reason`. **The score is counted (`score`):** each stuck twin attributes its share of the gap to its barrier, so a candidate's `movability` is `{movable_count/share (partner), system_count/share, structural_count/share (structural + none + stuck twins who named no barrier), unscored_count/share, scored, levers[]}` and, where headcounts exist, `movable_people` with `movable_low/high` from the stuck interval. **The ranking changes:** `candidates_of` orders by the movable count first once anything is scored (a small movable gap outranks a large immovable one), then by who is stuck, the gap and journey order; unscored everywhere falls back to the L7-01 order. **Where it shows:** a movable / system / structural / unscored bar on every candidate on the Journey page with the movable share, the movable people and the top levers, each barrier labelled *a partner could fix it · lever · who* (tooltip: the reason), recounted inside a cut from the stored classes; the record carries `movability` per candidate and reach / lever / actor per barrier; the records block adds *40% movable (2 of 5 stuck; ≈9,588 people), 60% needs the system, levers: …* and `FIGURE_RULES` says movability comes only from the record and is never judged by the model; the report block, the client document (which says the ranking rule) and `records.csv` (`top_candidate_movable_share`, `top_candidate_movable_people`) carry it. **Lever simulation — counterfactual runs gated on calibration rules (brief L7-04 on the minimum of L4-02; `services/measurement/levers.py`, `models.CalibrationMapping`).** *If we pulled this lever, how many would move?* is answered only against a written, reviewed **calibration rule**: `calibration_mappings` (Alembic `0016`) — the lever, a description as the twins will read it, `applies_to` (a segment map over `probe.segments_for` keys: `{deprivation: [Q1 …]}`, empty = everyone), `deltas` (`{"friction.time_cost": -3}` in points on the 0–10 dial scale, validated against `DIALS_SCHEMA`, each within the rule's `bound` of at most ±6, default ±4), the evidence it rests on (`[{ref, note}]`, **optional** — `basis_class` is `evidence_anchored` with at least one line, else `assumption`: the reviewer signs the dial changes as a scenario and the shift is labelled *assumed effect, not a forecast* on the page, in the record label and caveats, in the records block the report model reads (`ASSUMPTION … NO evidence`, with a `FIGURE_RULES` clause binding the model to what-if wording) and in the export), the `basis` line, the author, and `status` draft → reviewed with `reviewed_by` / `reviewed_at` (`POST …/rules/{id}/review`; editing a rule sends it back to draft). **The system drafts, the human reviews** (`draft_rule`, `POST /sessions/{id}/rules/draft {lever, journey_probe_id?, candidate_id?}`; *Draft the rule* beside the lever box on every Journey candidate and *Draft it for me* in the rule book): one orchestration-model call reads the candidate's step and barriers, what the twins said would remove them, the evidence the twins cited for those barriers, the session's typed facts and on-topic evidence items — numbered `E1…` (`evidence_pool`, ≤40) — and proposes the description, `applies_to` (allowed values only), 2–4 dial changes with reasons, evidence **by handle only** (an unknown handle is dropped; empty when nothing speaks to the lever → an assumption) and the basis; `clean_draft` keeps real dials, clamps to the bound, drops zeros, sets `author` = *drafted by the system*; saved as a **draft** — nothing runs until someone signs it by name. `GET /sessions/{id}/rules` lists them with the dial vocabulary; `run.json` carries every rule as the assumption log (L4-01). **A lever run** (`POST /sessions/{id}/levers/run {journey_probe_id, candidate_id, lever, rule_id?, mode}`) first finds a reviewed rule for the lever (`find_rule`: by id, else by normalised name / containment) and **refuses with 409** and the missing rule named when there is none (`refusal`: *no rule* vs *a draft exists, not reviewed*) — there is no fallback that asks the twins to imagine the change. With a rule it creates a within-subjects experiment on the Journey instrument (`levers.start`): the same twins, the same seed, the same steps, two arms — `baseline` and `lever`, the latter's spec carrying `lever` = the rule frozen (`lever_spec`) — and `levers.run` runs both (`experiment.run_experiment`, so the journey postprocess codes, traces, headcounts and scores each arm) then counts the **shift** (`attach_shift` → `shift`): at the candidate's step, the twins the rule covers answer with `adjusted_dials` applied in `probe.answer_one` (clamped to the scale and the bound; the stored twin is never written) and `counterfactual_block` in the user message (*A CHANGE NOW IN PLACE … answer as you would if this were really there for you — no more helpful than it would actually be*); the conversion then → now with a paired bootstrap interval (`stats.paired_lift` on per-twin through flags), the stuck count then → now, how many twins moved through / fell back / were unchanged (the dispersion), the share reaching the end of the journey then → now with its interval, the same by every split the rows carry (deprivation first), `covered` (twins the rule applied to) and — where the baseline had headcounts — the people moved with a range. Stored on `experiment.results.lever`; `GET /sessions/{id}/levers` lists the runs. **The record:** `records.record_from_lever` (kind `lever`, format `lift`, the rule with its reviewer in provenance and a caveat, the movement, the end-of-journey shift and the people moved) and a records-block line *lever run under rule '…' (reviewed by …; dials …; applies to …); ≈N people moved (…); end of journey x% → y%*; `FIGURE_RULES` says a modelled shift may be stated only from a lever record with its interval, rule and reviewer, and that an unsimulated lever is said to be unsimulated. **On the Journey page:** *Simulate this lever* on every candidate (the movability levers pre-filled, or any lever typed) → the run starts, or the refusal is shown with the missing rule named and a *Write a rule* button opening the **Rule book** (`lab/RuleBook.tsx`: the session's rules with status, a form — lever, description, who it applies to, the dial changes group by group, the bound, evidence, basis, author — and *Review* / *Withdraw review* with the reviewer's name); lever runs list under the candidate with the shift sentence, the then → now conversion, the movement, the end of the journey, the people moved and the rule. Not built: an analyst override of a movability class, deriving rules from evidence automatically (L4-03), sensitivity with dials at their bounds (L4-05), gating the rest of the Lab behind calibration (L4-04), any comparison across sessions (L7-07). **Behaviour targeting — which behaviour to change, as a counted ranking (brief L7-05; `services/measurement/targeting.py`).** *Which behaviour do we go after first?* is answered by measurement: on any candidate, *Rank behaviours* runs a within-subjects experiment over the twins at risk at that step (`at_risk_ids`: reached the 'from' step or further in the baseline run) — a baseline arm plus one arm per behaviour, each arm the same twins answering the journey again with **one dial nudged by the same few points** (`spec.nudge {dial, points}` applied in-call by `probe.answer_one` through `levers.adjusted_dials`; nothing is described to the twin — a nudge is a disposition, not an event in the world). The behaviours are the session's question-specific dials (`dynamic.<key>`, ticked by default) plus any fixed dial the analyst adds; ≤12; nudge 1–3 points, default 2. `rank` counts, per behaviour, the paired shift in conversion at the step (`levers.shift`) ÷ points → **movement per point** with its interval; the sign gives the **direction to push** (a nudge up that lowered conversion means push it down); behaviours whose interval excludes zero rank first, then by size. **Backfire has equal billing**: `hurts` (raised, it lowered conversion) and per-band backfire (a deprivation band of ≥3 paired twins whose interval sits on the other side of zero from the overall direction) are flagged on the row, listed in `backfires`, opened in the sentence with *BACKFIRE:* and shown in a red block above the ranking. People per point follow the baseline headcount. Nudge arms skip theme coding, tracing and movability (`journey._targeting_kinds`); the baseline arm keeps only headcounts. Arm keys are short (`n1`, `n2` …) because `probes.variant_key` is VARCHAR(48) in Postgres (SQLite ignores the limit, so this only bites in prod); the behaviour travels in the arm's `spec.nudge.dial`. `records.record_from_targeting` (kind `targeting`, estimate = the top behaviour's movement per point) with the ranked list, the backfires and the caveat that this is the **modelled population's sensitivity** to each behaviour, not an intervention's effect (that is the rule book's and the lever run's job); the records block lists the behaviours in order with direction, per-point movement, interval and people per point, then *BACKFIRE:* or *no backfire found*; a `FIGURE_RULES` clause binds the report model to quote the list in order with every backfire and never to rank a behaviour on its own. **Message testing against the panel (brief L7-06; `services/measurement/messaging.py`).** *Which framing gets the stuck through?* The Lab's A/B tool already pairs two framings on the same twins; L7-06 is the frame around it. On any candidate, *Test messages* takes up to six messages (a name and the text exactly as people would read it; `clean_messages` drops blanks and duplicates, caps text at 1,500 characters) and runs a within-subjects experiment over the twins at risk at that step (`targeting.at_risk_ids`): a baseline arm (kind `targeting_baseline`, headcounts only) and one arm per message (`spec.message {key, label, text}`, short keys `m1…` for the VARCHAR(48) `variant_key`), each arm the same twins answering the journey again having **read the message** — `probe.build_user_message` adds `messaging.message_block` (*A MESSAGE YOU HAVE JUST SEEN … no more persuasive than it would actually be*) after any lever block; nothing about the twin changes and no dial moves — a message is a stimulus, not a change in the world. Message arms skip coding, tracing and movability like nudge arms (`journey._targeting_kinds`). `rank` counts per message the paired shift in conversion at the step (`levers.shift`) with its interval, `direction` helps / hurts / none, the movement, the end-of-journey shift, the people moved where the baseline had headcounts, and the shift by every split; where the twins carry frame weights (L2-02) the same shift is counted again with each twin at its weight (`stats.weighted_paired_lift`: weighted mean of the paired differences, bootstrap over twins, Kish effective n) and the run says *weighted to the frame*. Order: messages whose interval sits above zero first (largest first), then the indistinguishable by size, then the ones that hurt. **Backfire has equal billing**: `hurts` (lowers conversion overall, interval below zero) and per-band backfire (a deprivation band of ≥3 paired twins whose interval sits below zero while the message helps or does nothing overall) are flagged on the row, listed in `backfires`, opened in the sentence with *BACKFIRE:* and shown in a red block above the winner. **The calibration gate** (the brief ties L7-06 to L4-04's calibration): a message is not refused for want of a rule; instead every result carries the label **a modelled reaction to a framing, not a forecast of uptake** — the sentence, the page (a badge and the closing line), `records.record_from_messaging` (kind `messaging`, estimate = the top message's shift, the ranked list with each message's text, the backfires, the caveat), the records-block line (*messages tested (…): 1. 'Cost' +12.3 (CI …) … ; BACKFIRE: … ; this is a modelled reaction to a framing, NOT a forecast of uptake*) and a `FIGURE_RULES` clause (*WHICH MESSAGE WORKS* comes only from that record, in order, every backfire as prominent as the winner, never a message the record does not list). A forecast of what a message would do in the world remains the rule book's and the lever run's job. `POST /sessions/{id}/messaging/run {journey_probe_id, candidate_id, messages[{label?, text}], mode}` starts it (400 with the reason when nothing can run), `GET /sessions/{id}/messaging` lists the runs with `results.messaging` once counted; `lab/pages/JourneyPage` shows the form (name + text per message, add / remove, the at-risk count) and `MessagingRun` (the ranked list with the text under each, helps / hurts pill, shift with interval, weighted shift, people moved, movement, end of journey, by deprivation, the backfire block). **Commitment record — freeze the modelled baseline when an outcome is chosen (brief L7-08; `services/measurement/commitments.py`, `models.Commitment`, Alembic `0017`).** A candidate's numbers live inside a session that keeps changing; a commitment takes a **copy, not a pointer**, so what was promised can later be compared against what happened. *Commit to this outcome* on any Journey candidate, signed by name with an optional target (a share or percentage, normalised), horizon and note: `freeze` gathers, with no model call, the candidate as it stood (`records.candidate_summary`: step, conversion with interval, stuck, headcount and basis, barriers with reach / lever / actor, movability, equity, confidence), the journey run it came from (stages, funnel, headcount sentence and denominator, answered, seed, model, prompt hash), every lever run, behaviour ranking and message test on that candidate as records, the population build and frame (`export.run_record`: build id, n, level, matched / weighted / estimated dimensions, effective n, geography, sizing) plus the frame weights' effective n, the evidence on file (the `figures` ledger's items and typed facts with provenance class — a list of what was on file, not the corpus: L1-06 is not built), the scoping snapshot id, every calibration rule with its status, and the synthetic statement (`build_baseline`). `commitments` rows: `journey_probe_id`, `candidate_id`, `label`, `committed_by`, `committed_at`, `target`, `status` open → closed / superseded, `superseded_by`, `closed_by` / `closed_at` / `close_note`, `baseline`, `observed`. **Never edited**: committing the same candidate again supersedes the open one; `close` is signed by name with a note; nothing else in the Lab is blocked by a commitment. **The loop closes later** (`observe`, `compare`): an observed result is a share (or percentage) with a **required source and date** (`clean_observed` refuses one without), an optional range, who entered it and a note; the comparison against the frozen candidate is counted — the delta in points, whether the observed figure sits inside the modelled interval, whether the target was met — from the latest observation by date, never judged. `payload` carries the row, the comparison and a sentence (*Committed by … on …: the share of those at A who reach B, modelled at x% (CI) on population build … ; target …; Observed y% on … (source): +n points against the forecast, inside the modelled interval; target met*). **Where it shows:** `GET / POST /sessions/{id}/commitments`, `GET …/{cid}`, `POST …/{cid}/observe`, `POST …/{cid}/close`; on the Journey page a *committed* badge on the candidate row, the commit form, and a `CommitmentCard` per commitment (frozen forecast and what it rested on, target, the observed block green inside the interval and red outside, every observation, *show the frozen baseline* with the run, frame, barriers and movability then, runs, rules, evidence and the statement as frozen, *enter an observed result*, *close*). **The record:** `records.record_from_commitment` (kind `commitment`, estimate = the frozen conversion, `commitment{…}` with the build, the counts, the related runs, the target, the observations and the comparison) is listed by `records_for_session` and `records_by_ids`; the records block adds *COMMITTED outcome (frozen DATE by NAME; population build …; N evidence items, R rules; status …); target …; OBSERVED y% on … : +n points, inside the modelled interval; target met | no observed result yet; the frozen forecast is the only figure that may be quoted*, and a `FIGURE_RULES` clause binds the report model to the frozen forecast, never the live figure, and to an observed result only where the record gives one, never calling a forecast met or missed on its own. `structure.commitments_from_records` → `structure.outcome.commitments` renders as **Committed outcomes · the frozen forecasts** in the report (`ReportChat.CommittedOutcomes`) and in the client document; the export zip carries `commitments/<id>.json` per commitment with the statement. Not built: freezing the corpus itself (L1-06); the cross-session portfolio table over frozen rows (L7-07). **Refresh with the simulation amendments (2026-09-30; `levers.start_amended` / `run_amended` / `growth` / `attach_growth`).** A lever run answers one question — what one signed rule does at one step — and its shift lived only in the experiment panel, so the funnel never showed the growth the rule book had earned. Now the Journey page has a **Refresh with amendments** button beside the rule book (disabled, with the reason, until at least one rule is signed): it creates a fresh journey probe on the same steps, seed and filters with every reviewed rule frozen into `spec.amendments.rules[]` (`amendment_pack`: rule id, lever, description, who it applies to, the deltas and bound, the reviewer, the basis class), and the same twins answer again — each twin's dials shifted by every rule that covers it, in rule-book order, each within its own bound (`amended_dials`), and the covering changes described to it together as a world, numbered, with the same *no more helpful than they would actually be* line (`amendments_block`; a twin no rule reaches answers as things are). The twin's **earlier journey answers are hidden** from its history for this run (`_agent_history(hide_instruments=("journey",))`): the standing instruction not to contradict an earlier answer would otherwise pin it where it was. The amended probe runs the full journey postprocess (coding, tracing, headcounts on the same denominator, movability), so it is a complete journey run in its own right and sits under *Past runs*. Then **`growth`** is counted on the twins that answered both runs: per step the share reached then → now with a paired bootstrap interval and the counts, per transition the conversion then → now with the interval, who moved up or fell back, the end of the journey, the number of twins the pack covered, and where the base run had headcounts the people at each step then → now and the difference (the amended run's own figure on the same denominator; a client-anchored step stays fixed); `assumed` when any rule in the pack rests on no evidence, and the sentence then opens *Assumed effect, not a forecast*. Stored as `aggregates.amended` on the amended probe (`available, n, covered, rules, funnel[], transitions[], end, movement, sentence, base_probe_id`). **On the page:** the newest complete amended run's growth draws as a lighter green extension on each funnel bar with *→ now% +pts* beside it (a *show on the funnel* toggle), and a panel *The journey with the amendments in place* lists the rules as chips (assumed ones in yellow, dials and reviewer on hover), then each step then → now with the lift, the interval on hover and the people moved, then the growth sentence; while the run is in flight the button reads *Refreshing with amendments · 12/50*; a refusal names what is missing with a link to the rule book. Opening the amended run itself shows an *Amended run* banner and its own growth panel; it cannot be refreshed again (refresh from the base). **The record:** a journey record from an amended run carries `amended` and the records block adds *AMENDED RUN — the journey re-read with N signed rule(s) in place (…); growth: …; the figures above are the amended ones*, so the report never reads an amended funnel as the baseline. Tests: `levers_test.py` (+2). |
| `ask` (`v1`, hidden) — superseded by the survey's *Reaction* template; still the A/B base for text material | `reasoning`, `verdict` (yes/no/mixed — "does this work for you / would you go along with it"), `strength` 0–100, `feeling` -1..1, `key_factor` (3–8 free words) — then, after the run, `theme` (the factor coded into one of ≤7 population-level themes) | Yes share with a Wilson CI; **open to it** (yes or mixed); strength and feeling with bootstrap CIs; verdict mix; **coded themes** (the driver mix, without an enum per question); segment splits; verbatims with their theme chip; one sentence |
| **`verdict`** (`v1`, hidden — run by the report, §11) | `reasoning`, `position` (for / against / mixed), `confidence` 0–100, `verdict` (one quotable line) | **Its own page (`lab/pages/VerdictPage`, 2026-09-28):** the *for* share with its Wilson interval and the position mix; a **cut picker** — *Read by* deprivation (first, equity by default), stance, age band, segment, the population's demographics and every dynamic dial, then a value chip — which recomputes the headline (Wilson interval, a *small cut* warning under 8), the position mix and the mean confidence for that cut from the stored answers; every split as a table (deprivation in rank order; a heading click selects that cut); and **In their own words**: every twin's position, confidence, deprivation cell, quoted verdict line and reasoning, filtered by the cut and by for / mixed / against, most confident first. Listed under the Lab's *Past runs*. |
| `choice` (`v1`, hidden) | `reasoning`, `choice` and `runner_up` (enums built from the option keys in the spec), `confidence`, `key_factor` → `theme` | The choose-between-them experiment design's own results (§11a.7) |

**The survey is what makes the Lab generic.** A survey is the general form of "ask them anything": Ask (below) is a one-question survey with a fixed question set, and it survives as the survey's *Reaction* template. The builder (`lab/forms/SurveyBuilder`, registered through the instrument's `form` key — the shell renders a tool's own builder when it declares one, the generic `InstrumentForm` otherwise) offers **templates** (Reaction, Email test, Concept test, NPS + why, Consequences — plain data in `instruments/survey.py::TEMPLATES`, published on `GET /lab/instruments` as `templates`), a title, optional material shown first, and the question list with per-type controls, a primary star, reordering and a "preview as they see it" mode. Validation is the instrument's own (`Instrument.validate`, called by the API): text on every question, ≥2 options, grid rows and columns, a sane scale. The agent sees the rendered questionnaire (`SURVEY: … 1. … [choose one: …]`) plus the material, and the tool-use schema carries each question's text as its field description. Open-text questions are theme-coded per question after the run (`apply_themes(fields=[…])`, stored as `<key>__theme`). **Results** (`lab/pages/SurveyPage`) follow the form-tool convention — **Summary / Question / Individual** — but every chart is drawn for its type (`Donut`, `OptionBars`, `Histogram` with the mean marked, `StackedRows` for grids in diverging colours, theme chips for text), carries its interval, and **clicking any mark filters to the personas behind it** with what they wrote; the Question view lists every response to one question, the Individual view shows one persona's completed form with its reasoning. CSV: one column per question (a grid cell is its row → column map).


**Forms — the survey, with the friction taken out (2026-10-06; `services/measurement/lab_brief.py`, `services/measurement/forms.py`, `api/v1/forms.py`, `lab/forms/FormsStudio`, `lite/LiteForms`).** The Survey instrument is labelled **Forms** in the picker (key still `survey`; past runs unchanged). The idea: a form should come from what the session already knows, not from a blank list, and the same three doors are offered in both views — the pro Lab, which keeps every manual control, and the simple view, which is the main home for it. **The Lab brief.** On opening, the tool reads (or rebuilds) the session\x27s standing brief — one strong-tier call (`lab_brief.build`, label `lab_brief`) over the question and its dynamic dials, the roster (stance, segments, places, a few names, the twins\x27 debate verdicts), the ten most-liked posts, past Lab runs and A/B verdicts, the latest report\x27s opening, the evidence brief, the Studio\x27s detected population and segment plan with its evidence coverage, the sampling-frame block and the knowledge graph — into `{summary, population, what_we_know[] (each tagged [evidence] / [statistics] / [debate] / [lab run] / [report]), tensions[], challenges[{title, why, measure}] (the open questions a form should go after), already_measured[], vocabulary[] (the population\x27s own words), gaps[], sources[]}`, stored on `lab_briefs` (§6.11; one row per session; Alembic `0018_lab_briefs`) with a **fingerprint** of what it read (agent, post, probe, experiment and graph-entity counts; the latest research run, build and report ids), so a session that has moved on reads as `stale` and is rebuilt on the next open; `ensure` prepends the stored brief (stale or not — a slightly old brief beats a slow turn) to every forms call and builds one only when none exists. **Three ways in**, all ending in the Survey spec, cleaned by `forms.clean_form` (unique keys; a choice question with fewer than two options becomes open text with a note; an inverted or absurd scale is repaired; a grid without columns gets the agree–disagree set; exactly one primary, the first closed question when none was marked; then the instrument\x27s own `normalise` + `validate`, whose problems travel back as `problems[]`): **(1) Have one already** — drop or choose a file (`POST …/forms/import/file`: PDF, Word, text, CSV through `parse_document`) or paste it (`POST …/forms/import`); one call (`forms_import`) keeps every question in order with its wording, options, rows, columns and scale ends, drops headings and consent boilerplate into `notes`, and a heuristic parser (numbered lines → questions, bullets under them → options, *yes/no* and *scale of 1 to 10* recognised) stands in when the model fails, so something always lands on screen; into a non-empty form it **appends**. **(2) Write it for me** (`POST …/forms/generate {goal?, length: short | standard | deep, existing?}`): the brief, the analyst\x27s one-line goal (the input is prefilled with the top challenge; the brief\x27s challenges are offered as one-click goals) and the form on screen, if any, to extend → a form with `rationale` and `covers[]`. **(3) Talk it through** (`POST …/forms/chat {messages[], form}`): stateless — the client sends the history (last 24 turns) and the form on screen; the system prompt is the colleague\x27s brief (short, warm, one question at a time, lead with a concrete suggestion, bias to action — a stated goal is drafted at once — name the brief\x27s open questions and tensions, flag leading or double-barrelled questions and anything a past run already answered, use the population\x27s words) plus the brief, a question-type guide and the form; the answer is one forced tool call `{reply, chips[] (2–4 quick replies), form_changed, form, change_note}`, so the colleague can reply in words, hand back the **complete** changed form, or both; an empty history returns an **opening line written from the brief with no model call** (the top challenge by name, *Draft: …* chips); a change that would empty the form is refused. Every change the model makes replaces (or extends) the form on screen with a one-line note and **Undo**, and the changed questions flash; the draft and the chat are kept per session in `sessionStorage`, so switching tabs loses nothing. **In the pro Lab** (`lab/forms/FormsStudio`) the builder takes the whole tab — the form registry declares `layout: full`, the shell hands it the run controls (who answers, model, estimate, Run) and the tool\x27s past runs to place, and keeps its two-column layout for reading results; nothing in the shell names the instrument: a header (All tools · Forms · a brief chip — *Knows this session · N open questions* / *Reading the session…* / *Session has moved on · refresh* — and Import / Write it for me / Templates once a form exists), then **chat · form · run rail** (`xl:grid-cols-[400px_1fr_300px]`; the chat a drawer below 1280px, the rail under the form below 1024px). The middle column is the manual builder in full — title, an optional *what they see first* block, one editable card per question (`lab/forms/QuestionEditor`: type, text, options with Enter-to-add, scale ends, grid rows and columns, the model\x27s one-line *why*, primary star, reorder, delete, and the instrument\x27s rules mirrored as an amber line before the run is refused), *preview as they see it*, *Add* chips per type, templates — with the three doors around it; the rail lists the brief\x27s challenges under *Worth measuring*, each with a *draft* button. After a run the shell\x27s left column offers **Edit this form & run again** (the run\x27s spec back into the studio) and **New form**. **In the simple view** (`components/lite/LiteForms`, `?view=lab`, which replaces the Lab placeholder) the same engine in plainer words, full viewport: the colleague\x27s chat on the left (*Think it through with me*, with *what I know* opening the brief), the form on the right; before there is a form, no cards (since 2026-10-06, afternoon): one field at reading width — *What about? e.g. <the top open question>* with **Write them for me** inside it (`length: short`) and up to three open questions as quiet chips under it — then *Already have questions?* (a light dashed drop zone, *or paste them in*), *Or start from a ready-made one* (quiet chips) and *Earlier forms* as hairline-separated rows (title · *N answered · when*); the header is a plain *Back* link with no breadcrumb, the chat panel's heading is *Think it through* with *What I know* opening the brief, and the chat's quick replies are capped at three (a *Draft: …* chip shows without its prefix); once there is one, a name, one plain card per question (*They answer with: pick one · pick any · yes or no · a score · a number · their own words · rate a few things*, answers editable, *make it the main one*), *Add a question*, *Add from a file*, *Rewrite for me*, and one floating bar — **Ask the N people** (a Fast survey probe) / *Start over*; the run is polled every 2.5 s (*Asking 40 people · 12 of 40 answered*, *Stop here*), and the answers render through the pro `SurveyPage` inside a light card with *See in detail* into the Lab, *Change the questions* and *Ask something else*. Below 1024px the chat is a drawer behind a *Think it through* button. Tests: `forms_test.py` (9).

**Ask** (hidden since the survey arrived; past runs still render): Every "which email lands", "does this poster make you want to go", "what happens in your street if this goes ahead" has the same shape — show a person something, ask them to react — so the schema is fixed and the **question is free text**, written by the analyst (`question_from="question"`, with suggested phrasings as chips: *Would you open this email and act on it?*, *What would happen for you and people like you if this went ahead?* …). The reason is free words rather than an enum; **`services/measurement/themes.py`** codes every key factor in the population into at most seven themes in one cheap call after the run (unique factors deduplicated, at most 400 sent, anything the model misses keeps its own words), writes `theme` onto each stored answer and re-aggregates — so the driver mix, the coded reasons among movers and the CSV all work exactly as they do for purchase intent's enum. An experiment codes **all its arms in one pass** so the vocabulary is shared (the per-arm call is skipped when a probe belongs to an experiment). Anything with a price still belongs to purchase intent, which declares four metrics — `would_buy` (share, **primary**), `likelihood_0_100`, `max_price` (money), `sentiment` — with `decision_key="would_buy"` and `driver_key="key_driver"`. Ask declares `verdict` (primary), `open_to`, `strength`, `feeling`, with `decision_key="verdict"` and `driver_key="theme"`.

Specialist tools are added only when their numbers cannot come from free text — price sensitivity (Van Westendorp), conjoint, MaxDiff, Kano, budget allocation — which is exactly when they earn a form of their own. Any of them becomes A/B-testable the moment it declares metrics.

### 11a.4 Statistics (`services/measurement/stats.py`)
Pure Python, numpy-free, unit-tested: Wilson intervals for every proportion; bootstrap intervals for means (seeded, so an interval is reproducible); paired lift for within-subjects A/B and an unpaired (independently resampled) lift for between-subjects; segment splits that **flag thin buckets rather than hiding them**; raking (IPF) to a target census; demand/revenue curves and the revenue-optimal price; test-retest agreement; polarisation.

### 11a.4a Persona curation from a survey (Pro)

Uploading a survey or profile doc (Agents tab, `.txt`/`.csv`) sends it as `doc_context`, and Claude translates the responses into dial values. Two things about it are easy to get wrong:

- **It is Pro-only.** The Fast path samples the pre-built bank with no LLM call, so an uploaded doc is discarded. The upload now says so rather than letting it look used.
- **The survey and the stance sliders compete.** Left alone, the stance quota wins: the model fills its "direct = domain expert" slots with invented analysts, and a 24-respondent consumer panel ends up diluted by people who were never in it. **Mirror the panel** (on by default once a doc is attached in Pro) makes the panel authoritative — one agent per respondent, keeping their demographics, circumstances and answers, with stance derived from each person's real relationship to the topic rather than the sliders.

**Duplicate repair.** Persona batches (`_BATCH_SIZE = 10`) are generated concurrently from an identical prompt and cannot see each other, so each batch independently invents the most obvious archetype for the topic — a 22-agent spawn produced two identical 38-year-old urban mobility economists. After the batches return, collisions are detected by name *and* by role-at-the-same-age, and only the collided slots are regenerated in one extra call that lists who is already taken; if that call fails, names are made unique deterministically so the roster stays navigable.

**Truncation.** `SURVEY_CHAR_LIMIT` (40,000 characters since 2026-10-06; 8,000 before) is shared by both ends — the browser trims to it and reports how many rows survived (the Pro Sources panel and the simple view's survey card alike), instead of the file being cut at 12,000 client-side and again at 8,000 server-side with nothing said.

### 11a.5 Cost estimate
`POST /sessions/{id}/probes/estimate` prices the **model that will actually run**, resolved from the app's configured tier, not the "Fast"/"Pro" label — production sets `MODEL_AGENTS` to Sonnet, so pricing the label understated a Fast probe by ~12x. Per-million list prices live in `api/v1/measurement._PRICE_PER_MTOK`; an unrecognised model is priced at the top tier, because an estimate that flatters the bill is worse than one that overshoots. The Lab shows the resolved model id under the estimate, so the tier label can never imply a model the deployment is not using. `POST /sessions/{id}/experiments/estimate` prices `agents × variants` calls for a within-subjects test and `agents` for between-subjects.

### 11a.6 Reproducibility
Every probe stores its **seed, model, schema id and prompt hash**, and the agent sample is drawn with a seeded RNG — so "the same 100 agents" means the same 100 agents on a re-run, and two results are only comparable when the ask matched. Answers are never mixed across schema versions.

### 11a.7 Experiments — the A/B test (`services/measurement/experiment.py`)
The Lab's second primitive. An **experiment** is one instrument and 2–6 **variants** (two offers, two prices, two messages). Each variant becomes an ordinary probe carrying `experiment_id` + `variant_key`, so every arm is a full probe result in its own right; what the experiments layer adds is the **comparison**. It is generic over instruments: it reads the base instrument's `metrics`, `decision_key` and `driver_key` and knows nothing about purchase intent.

**Base tools.** Any instrument that declares metrics: purchase intent and Ask today (Ask stays available here as *Reaction* even though the picker no longer offers it). The survey does not yet declare metrics — its questions are dynamic — so it cannot be an A/B base until metrics are derived from the form (planned).

**Three designs.**
- **Choose between** ("Choose between"): every agent sees **all** the options at once, labelled `OPTION A — <label>` …, and picks a winner and a runner-up in **one call per agent**. This is the direct form of "which is best" (which email, which poster, which name). Implemented as a single probe on the hidden `choice` instrument (arm key `all`), whose spec is composed from the base tool's variants (`compose_choice_spec`: each variant's `stimulus_key` material, the option keys and labels, and the question — the experiment's own `question`, else the first variant's, else the base tool's). Results: **preference share per option with a Wilson interval** and whether the winner is settled (the top two intervals do not overlap), runner-up counts, the most common rankings (head-to-head), and per option: coded themes for why it won, share choosing it by segment, verbatims. Costs `agents` calls.
- **Within-subjects** (default, "Rate each"): every chosen agent answers every variant, in separate calls. The probe runner **hides the sibling arms from the agent's history** (`_agent_history` excludes probes with the same `experiment_id`), so an agent answers B with no memory of what it said to A — the only way the difference measures the stimulus. The lift is a **paired** difference (each agent is its own control), so the interval is tight even at 50 agents. Costs `agents × variants` calls.
- **Between-subjects** ("Split population"): the filtered population is shuffled by the experiment seed and split into disjoint, equal-as-possible groups, one per variant (`split_between`; written into each arm's `agent_filter.agent_ids` at run time). Unpaired lift, wider interval, each agent sees one offer. Costs `agents` calls. Use it when the variants would contaminate each other.

Rate-each is the blind design (nobody sees both, so the lift measures the stimulus); choose-between is the direct one (a preference, cheaper, but subject to contrast effects). Both are offered on one switch. Scenario branching (re-running the whole debate under two stimuli) is **not built**.

**Run.** `POST /sessions/{id}/experiments` validates from the declaration (instrument must declare metrics; 2–6 variants with unique keys; each variant's required inputs), creates the experiment row and one queued probe per arm (same seed → a within-subjects sample is the same agents in every arm; each arm's spec = shared spec + variant spec), then `run_experiment` runs **all arms concurrently** with `PROBE_CONCURRENCY` divided between them (so k arms in flight still add up to one probe's worth of calls), and analyses when they finish. Stopping an experiment stops every arm; whatever paired up is still compared and the status is `stopped`.

**Results** (`experiments.results`), per variant vs the control (the first variant):
- **Lift per metric** — control level, variant level, `lift {mean, low, high, significant}`; shares in points, means and money in their own units. `significant` = the 95% interval clears zero.
- **Flips** (within only) — a `matrix` of every paired agent's from→to movement on `decision_key` (stayers included, for the flow diagram); the agents whose answer changed, with *both* reasonings, the variant answer's `driver_key`, and their segments; plus the direction mix (`no → yes`…) and the **coded reasons** (driver distribution among flippers).
- **Segment map** — the primary metric's lift inside every bucket of `stance`, `age_band`, `humanity_band`, `purchase_intent_prior`, thin buckets flagged (n < 3) not hidden; bootstraps run at 600 iterations per bucket to keep analysis fast.
- **Sentence & verdict** — plain language, quotable: *"£8 first month beats Full price on would buy by 58 points (67% vs 8%; 95% CI +33 to +83 points), driven by trust among the 7 who changed their answer; the effect is not reliable for direct and neutral stance."* When the interval includes zero it says so: *"No reliable difference between A and B on would buy: B is +4 points … but the 95% interval (−6 to +14 points) includes zero."* For A/B/n the verdict is about the strongest variant and names the others.

**UI — built to read as a comparison, not as two probes.** The Lab picker shows an **A/B test** card whenever any instrument declares metrics. `lab/ExperimentPanel` (the tool) — base instrument, name, design (**Rate each · Choose between · Split population** on one switch), a variant editor that renders the base tool's own `InstrumentForm` per variant (for choose-between the per-variant question is hidden and one question box, with chips, is asked once for all options) (A prefilled from the session query; a new variant copies the last so the analyst edits the difference; 2–6 variants), "who answers", Fast/Pro, a live estimate (`agents × variants = calls · $`), Run. **While running** (`LiveComparison` + `PairedDots`): not one dot grid per arm but **one pill per agent split into one segment per arm**, each segment taking the colour of that agent's answer as it lands, a ring marking an agent whose decision already differs between arms; counters per arm, "answered both", and **"changed their answer" in amber**, with the latest movers named. `lab/pages/ExperimentPage` (results). For choose-between: the verdict, a **preference race** (one bar per option with its interval, winner first, "2nd for n"), the most common rankings, and a per-option panel (click a bar) with why it won as coded themes, share choosing it by segment, and verbatims; the live view is one dot per agent coloured by the option it chose, with per-option counters. For rate-each and split, in reading order: the **verdict**; the primary metric as a **before/after with one big signed delta** and its interval (`DeltaHero`); the lift on every metric (`LiftBar` drawn around zero, grey when the interval includes it); **how the same agents moved** — a flow diagram (`FlowDiagram`, from the results' `flips.matrix`: control answers on the left, variant answers on the right, ribbons per from→to group, movers solid and stayers faint) beside the coded reasons, then the movers' own words with a toggle to see what they told the control; the segment **heat-map** (`HeatCell`); and only then **the arms, one compact line each** (key, label, stimulus, headline share, n) as context. CSV: one row per agent, every arm's answer fields side by side, plus `flipped`. Past runs list experiments alongside standalone probes; arm probes are not listed on their own, and an arm completing never hijacks the shell's probe view.

---

### 11a.8 Benchmarks — scoring a run against a real poll (2026-10-08)

A **benchmark** (`backend/app/data/kits/benchmarks/<id>/`) is a real poll: `benchmark.json` (every table of the pollster's crosstab workbook, every banner column, bases, verbatims), `form.json` (its questionnaire as a Forms survey with the workbook's own option labels) and `qmap.json` (which sheet answers which question). Built by `python -m app.services.kits.build_benchmark <workbook.xlsx> <id>` (`services/kits/crosstab.py` reads the .xlsx with the standard library: banner groups → column keys such as `Segment: Loyal Nationals`, the pollster's roll-up rows — Net, Trust, Agree, Heard combined — flagged `derived`, open-text sheets kept as verbatims tagged by segment/generation/gender; contact details dropped). Shipped: **`mic_11london_june2023`** — More in Common for 11 London, 15–19 June 2023, n=2,018 GB adults online, weighted (age×gender, education, ethnicity, region, 2019 vote); 22 questions on health priorities, NHS and health trust, clinical trials, medication, free NHS treatment, government vs charities, giving, developing countries, compromise and polarisation; banner: gender, region, GE 2019, long-term condition, ethnicity, generation and the seven 2020 segments.

**Running it.** The questionnaire appears in Forms as the template **Real poll · …** (`_benchmark_templates`), or `POST /sessions/{id}/benchmarks/{bid}/run`. Q7 (a split sample in the poll) is asked as two grids, each scored against its own half; Q12 is routed (`show_if` Q11 = Yes), so its base is the agents who said yes, as the poll's was.

**Scoring** (`benchmark.score`, `GET /sessions/{id}/probes/{pid}/benchmark/{bid}`): simulated shares are computed the pollster's way (single: share of base; multi: share ticking; grid: per row; routed bases; agent weights), roll-ups left out, for every banner column the population carries (total, segment, generation, gender, region). Per item: MAE in points, max error, top-choice match, total-variation distance, rank correlation; per column a summary with two baselines — **uniform** (every option equally likely) and, per segment, **national** (the segment answers like the country). **Segment gradients** list every option where the poll's segments differ by ≥8 points with the correlation of simulated vs real across segments — whether the twins reproduce the left/right and young/old splits the deck reports. First real run (prod, 200 Sonnet twins): 18.9 pts total — see the Changelog for what it exposed. Sanity run: answers sampled from the poll's own segment rows score 0.7 pts MAE on the total and 1.3–2.4 per segment, against national baselines of 3.6–6.6 and a uniform baseline of 16.5. `benchmark.responses_from_csv` scores a probe export (the CSV now carries segment, age, gender, region, past vote and weight, with list/grid answers as JSON).

**Forms extensions this needed** (`instruments/survey.py`): `max_choices` (select up to N; schema `maxItems`), `exclusive` options (*Don't know*, *None of the above* — only on their own), `show_if` routing (rendered to the agent; aggregation counts only the routed), up to 16 options/rows, `max_tokens` 4500 for long forms, and the directive lines on honest *Don't know* and unvarnished answers.

## 12. Backend reference

### 12.1 Tech
FastAPI 0.115 on uvicorn; SQLAlchemy 2.0 async (`aiosqlite` / `asyncpg`) + **Alembic** migrations; **PyJWT** (JWKS verification of Supabase user tokens, `app/core/auth.py`); pydantic 2 + pydantic-settings; `anthropic` 0.40; ingestion libs (pypdf, python-docx, openpyxl, xlrd, python-pptx, striprtf, lxml, Pillow, yt-dlp, faster-whisper). App: `backend/app/main.py` (CORS fully open; mounts all routers under `/api/v1`; `/health`; `/ws/{session_id}`; startup creates tables).

### 12.2 Complete API reference (all under `/api/v1`)

**Authentication:** every route below (except `/health`) requires `Authorization: Bearer <Supabase access token>` when auth is on. Tokens are verified against `{APP_SUPABASE_URL}/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json` (ES256/RS256; HS256 projects fall back to `GET /auth/v1/user`). Missing/invalid → **401**. Any session, agent, preset or graph that the caller does not own → **404** (never 403, so ids are not confirmed). `POST /sessions` and `POST /presets` stamp `user_id`; `GET /sessions` and `GET /presets` are filtered to the caller. **Admins** bypass ownership everywhere and may call `GET /sessions?scope=all` (adds `owner_email`, `is_mine`). Public: `GET /config` → `{auth: {enabled, supabase_url, anon_key}}` (the frontend fetches this at runtime, so it needs no build-time Supabase vars). `GET /me` → `{id, email, role, is_admin}` (also creates the profile lazily on SQLite dev). Admin only (403 otherwise): `GET /admin/users`, `PATCH /admin/users/{id}/role {role}`. The WebSocket `/ws/{id}?token=…` closes with **4401** (bad token) or **4404** (not your session). `GET /health` reports `auth` and `database` mode, plus **`schema_ok` / `schema_error`** — a migration that fails at startup now degrades health instead of only printing a warning (see §15.4).

**Sessions** (`/sessions`)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/sessions` | Create a session (`{title, query}`) → status `created`. |
| GET | `/sessions` | List newest 50 sessions. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}` | Fetch one (404 if missing). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/kg` | Knowledge-graph data `{entities, relations}`. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/kg/sources` | What the graph was fed, by material (2026-10-05): `{sources: [{kind, name, ref, chunks}], chunks, entities, relations}` read off each chunk's `[SOURCE …]` header; `kind` ∈ file, text, video, page, research, social, statistics, synthetic, other. The simple view's flow diagram reads it. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/kg/entity/{name}` | Entity drill-down (relations + source mentions). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/kg/ontology` | The typed ontology (§9.5): `{schema, ontology\|null, stale, entity_count}`. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/kg/ontology/build` | Classify every entity and type every relation against the schema, add implied geography containment, persist; returns the GET shape. **400** on an empty graph, **502** on a model failure. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/scoping` | Scoped retrieval state (§9.6): dimensions, units (≤300), facet counts, active policy, snapshot, stale. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/scoping/tag` | Facet-tag every chunk (builds the ontology first if needed); wipes and rewrites the session's units. **400** on an empty graph, **502** on a model failure. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/scoping/preview?agent_id=` | One twin's exposure profile with bases, what it sees (route, score, rules applied), what is hidden (dimension + reason), and the exact prompt block. |
| PUT | `/sessions/{id}/scoping/policy` | `{rules[], note}` → a new policy version. **400** on an unknown dimension / effect / `when`. |
| PUT | `/sessions/{id}/scoping/agents/{agent_id}/exposure` | `{exposure}` overrides any subset of the twin's profile; `null` clears it. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/scoping/retrievals?agent_id=&limit=` | The log of what twins were shown (purpose, snapshot, policy version, unit ids, routes). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/posts` | All simulation posts. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/dials` | Population dial dashboard (scorecard, heatmap, group stats). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/opinions` | Generate a 10–15-word verdict per agent that has posted (Claude `model_fast`, **batched 25 agents/call, ≤6 calls in parallel**, integer keys, `max_tokens` scaled per batch, truncated JSON salvaged). Optional body `{"agent_ids": [...]}` regenerates a subset. Verdicts are **persisted** on `spawned_agents.verdict`. Always HTTP 200: `{"opinions": {agent_id: verdict}, "generated": n, "total": m, "error": string\|null}` — `opinions` includes previously persisted verdicts; `error` carries a friendly LLM error instead of a 500. Code: `services/simulation/opinions.py`. |
| DELETE | `/sessions/{id}` | Delete session (no cascade). |

**Behaviour Lab** (§11a)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET / POST | `/sessions/{id}/rules` | Calibration rules (brief L4-02, minimum): list `{rules[], dials, bound_max, bound_default}` / create a draft `{lever, description, applies_to, deltas, bound, evidence[] (optional → `basis_class` assumption), basis, author}` (400 with the problems named); every rule carries `basis_class`. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/rules/draft` | `{lever, journey_probe_id?, candidate_id?, mode?}` → the system drafts a rule from the candidate's barriers, the twins' removals, the facts on file and the documents the twins cited (evidence chosen by handle from that material only; empty → assumption), saved as a draft (`drafted: true`, `material: n`); 422 when the draft is unusable. |
| PUT / DELETE | `/sessions/{id}/rules/{rule_id}` | Edit (back to draft) / remove a rule. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/rules/{rule_id}/review` | `{reviewed_by, approve}` — sign a rule off (only a reviewed rule can be simulated) or withdraw the sign-off. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/levers/run` | Lever simulation (brief L7-04): `{journey_probe_id, candidate_id, lever, rule_id?, mode?}` → the experiment (baseline + lever arms on the same twins) with the rule; **409** `{refused, lever, reason, drafts[], missing}` when no reviewed rule exists — never a guess. The counted shift (`results.lever`) carries **`why`** — the twins' own words behind the count: every twin at risk at the step is classed `through` / `back` / `still_stuck` / `already_through` (`levers.twin_moves`), the movers are coded into 1–4 reasons per group by one fast-tier call (`levers.why_moved`, label `lever_why`), each with its count, the twins it covers and ONE sentence copied verbatim from a twin (`_verbatim` checks the quote is really in that twin's words; otherwise the twin's own first sentence stands in), and those still stuck are grouped by the barrier theme they still name; the coder failing leaves the uncoded form (one twin per line, `coded: false`), never nothing. `why.sentence` is appended to the shift's `sentence`, so the record and the report can say *why* as well as *how much*. Runs counted before this existed are backfilled in the background when listed (`ensure_why`). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/levers` | Every lever run, newest first, with `results.lever` (the shift) when counted. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/journey/{probe_id}/amend` | **Refresh with the simulation amendments** (2026-09-30): `{rule_ids?, mode?}` → a fresh journey probe on the same steps, seed and filters with every signed rule (or the ones named) frozen into `spec.amendments {base_probe_id, rules[], requested_at}`; the same twins answer again with the covering rules' dial shifts stacked and the changes described together; the growth is then counted against the base run and stored on the amended probe's `aggregates.amended`. **409** `{refused, reason, drafts[], missing}` where no rule is signed (a draft is not enough); **400** for an incomplete base run or when the run given is itself an amended run. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/journey/{probe_id}/amendments` | Every amended run counted against this journey run, newest first (probe payloads; `aggregates.amended` carries the growth once complete). Its growth carries the same **`why`** over the whole journey (`twin_moves(k=None)`: through = gets further than before, back = stops earlier) — who the amendments moved forward and who they pushed back, in their words, with those still stuck by remaining barrier; older amended runs are backfilled when listed (`ensure_growth_why`). The Journey page disables *Refresh with amendments* while an amended run is queued or running; a **stop** link beside it releases a run that is not moving (`POST …/probes/{probe_id}/stop`), and the newest amended run that failed or was stopped is named under the button so a missing growth is not read as "nothing changed" (2026-10-01). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/targeting/behaviours` | Behaviour targeting (brief L7-05): what can be ranked — the session's question-specific dials (`dynamic.<key>`, ticked by default) and the fixed dial vocabulary, with `points_default` (2), `points_max` (3), `max_behaviours` (12). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/targeting/run` | `{journey_probe_id, candidate_id, behaviours?[], points?, mode?}` → a within-subjects experiment over the twins at risk at the candidate's step: a baseline arm plus one arm per behaviour with that single dial nudged by `points`; the ranking lands in `results.targeting` when counted. 400 when fewer than two twins are at risk or nothing can be ranked. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/targeting` | Every behaviour-targeting run, newest first, with `results.targeting` when counted. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/lab/brief` | **The Lab brief** (§6.11, Forms): the stored brief with `stale`; `{brief: null, status: "none", stale: true}` before one is written. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/lab/brief` | `{mode?, force?}` → (re)write the brief from everything on file — one strong-tier call over the question, roster, debate, past Lab runs, report, evidence brief, Studio plan, frame and graph; a current brief is returned as it is unless `force`. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/forms/import` | `{text}` → a typed form (`title, intro, questions[], notes[], problems[]`) from pasted questionnaire text (`forms_import`), the heuristic parser when the model fails. 400 on empty text. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/forms/import/file` | multipart `file` (PDF, Word, text, CSV, through `parse_document`) → the same, plus `filename`. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/forms/generate` | `{goal?, length?: short \| standard \| deep, existing?}` → a form written from the brief and the goal (`forms_generate`), extending `existing` when given; adds `rationale`, `covers[]`, `grounded`. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/forms/chat` | `{messages[{role, content}], form}` → `{reply, chips[], form_changed, form, change_note}` (`forms_chat`, one forced tool call over the history); an empty history returns the opening line written from the brief with no model call. Stateless — the client holds the history. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/journey/suggest` | The Journey tool's proposal (brief L7-01): `{question?, mode?}` → `{outcome, stages[{key,label,definition}], basis, grounded, denominator}` (the frame's in-scope headcount with source and year, or null — L7-02) — the ordered steps to the outcome in the question, drawn from the evidence brief, the frame and the graph (`grounded` false when the session has none and the steps came from the question alone). Nothing is stored; the analyst edits the steps in the builder and they go into the probe spec. |
| GET | `/lab/instruments` | The instrument library — key, label, description, question, **`inputs`** (the tool's own declared controls, with `suggestions`), **`kpis`**, **`metrics`** + `supports_experiments` + `decision_key` (what an A/B test can compare), `stimulus_key`, `question_from`, `hidden`, `page`, answer schema. The single source of truth both sides read: the UI builds its form and its headline strip from this. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/probes/estimate` | How many agents match the filter, which model, and the estimated cost — **before** you run. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/probes` | Run an instrument: `{instrument, spec{stimulus, price, currency, context, agent_filter}, mode, seed}`. Returns the probe row immediately; the run happens in the background and streams. **400** if the session has no population, **404** for an unknown instrument. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/probes` | All probes for the session, newest first. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/probes/{probe_id}` | One probe with its `aggregates`, plus every typed answer with the agent's name, role and segments (`?include_answers=false` to skip). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/probes/{probe_id}/stop` | Stop a running probe; answers already collected are kept and still aggregate. |
| DELETE | `/sessions/{id}/probes/{probe_id}` | Delete a finished standalone probe and every answer under it (204). **409** while it is queued/running (stop it first) and **409** for an arm of an A/B test (delete the experiment instead). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/probes/{probe_id}/export.csv` | Flat file for the client's analyst — one row per agent, segments + every answer field as columns (plus the coded `theme` where the tool has one). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/experiments/estimate` | Agents matched, number of calls (`agents × variants` within; `agents` between or choice), model and cost — before you run. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/experiments` | Run an A/B/n test: `{instrument, design: within\|between\|choice, name, question (choice only), variants: [{key, label, spec}], spec, agent_filter, mode, seed}`. A `choice` design creates ONE probe on the hidden `choice` instrument (variant key `all`). Creates the experiment and one queued probe per arm, returns both immediately; the run and the comparison happen in the background. **400** for a non-testable instrument, <2 or >6 variants, duplicate keys, a variant missing a required input, an unknown design, or no population; **404** for an unknown instrument. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/experiments` | All experiments for the session, newest first. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/experiments/{experiment_id}` | One experiment with its `results` and its arm probes (`probes[]`, each with aggregates). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/experiments/{experiment_id}/stop` | Stop every running arm; what paired up is still compared. |
| DELETE | `/sessions/{id}/experiments/{experiment_id}` | Delete a finished experiment, its arm probes and their answers (204). **409** while it is queued/running. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/experiments/{experiment_id}/export.csv` | One row per agent, every arm's answer fields side by side (`A_would_buy`, `B_would_buy`, …), plus `flipped`. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/apply-preset` | Wipe agents, load a preset population (background task). |

**Ingestion** (`/sessions`)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/ingest/text` | Ingest pasted text (background). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/ingest/document` | Ingest an uploaded file (parsed then background-ingested). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/ingest/youtube` | Ingest a YouTube URL (background extraction). |
