| POST | `/sessions/{id}/ingest/url` | Ingest a web page the user handed over (`{url}`; 400 unless http/https). Read with the research loop's page fetcher (`evidence/fetch_page.fetch_page`: plain → Tavily → headless Chromium) and ingested as it is — tagged `[SOURCE web \| user-supplied page <url>]`, never judged for relevance or filtered out; an unreadable page ingests a one-line note naming the address. The simple view's *Add anything* box. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/ingest/llm-search/generate` | Generate a research paper, return it (no ingest). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/ingest/llm-search` | Generate a paper and ingest it (background). |

**Simulation** (`/sessions`)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/spawn-agents` | Wipe + build a population (`{count (1–1000), mode ("fast"\|"pro"), profile_query, direct_pct, indirect_pct, neutral_pct, doc_context, humanity, humanity_coverage}`; background). Fast samples the bank; Pro curates via Sonnet. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/simulate/start` | Start the debate (`{intensity (1–20), mode ("fast"\|"pro")}`). 409 if still ingesting; 400 if no agents. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/simulate/pause` | Status → `paused`. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/simulate/stop` | Status → `complete`. |

**Population Studio** (§7.8; all owner-scoped)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/population/sources?geography=` | The statistics publishers the Studio can search (`sources[]`) and the keys ticked by default (`default[]` — every publisher since 2026-10-02; `geography` is accepted but no longer narrows the list). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds` | Start a build: `{mode, count, constraints, sources{quant, quant_sources[], quant_query, quant_auto}}` (`quant_auto` — the ticks were never changed, so the build may re-derive them from the detected geography). Supersedes any active build — **except** that a simple-view start (`constraints.auto_run`) while a simple-view build is already in flight returns that running build unchanged (2026-10-05: a page reopened before it knew the build existed, a double click or a second tab must not restart the work; the pro Studio's start still supersedes). Runs detect → gather → clarify → plan in the background; returns the build row. **`constraints.auto_run = {intensity, mode}`** (the simple view) makes the build hands-off: when the plan reaches `awaiting_review` it is approved as proposed (logged as a decision), and when the roster is written the debate is started server-side with those settings, exactly as `POST /simulate/start` would (waiting while the session is still ingesting, up to ten minutes; not started if the build was stopped early). Startup recovery stops an `awaiting_review` build whose `auto_run` approval task died with the process; a plan waiting for a person is left as it is. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/latest` | `{build}` — the newest build with detected, questions, plan and log (rebuilds the page on reload). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/{bid}` | One build. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/{bid}/answers` | `{answers{qid: text}, skip}` — record the analyst's answers and continue to the plan. **409** unless the build is `clarifying`. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/{bid}/segments/{sid}` | `{decision: accept\|reject\|edit, edits?, reason?}` — accept, edit (merged, counts as accepted) or reject (regenerates the slot from the reason). **409** unless the plan awaits review; **400** for an unknown decision. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/{bid}/replan` | `{constraints?, count?, keep_accepted}` — re-compose the plan with the current dials, keeping accepted/edited segments. **409** while a stage runs. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/{bid}/frame/{dim_key}` | The ladder for one frame dimension (§7.9): `{action: estimate\|upload\|proxy\|skip, categories?, source?, proxy_of?}`. **400** on a bad action / unknown dimension / an upload with fewer than two rows; **409** while a stage runs. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/{bid}/frame/estimate-all` | Resolve every open gap with a labelled model estimate (the model declines attitudinal ones). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/{bid}/approve` | `{count?, mode?}` — wipe the session's agents and build the roster from the non-rejected segments (background). **409** unless `awaiting_review` / `stopped` / `complete` / `error`; **400** with nothing to build. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/builds/{bid}/stop` | Stop the running stage: detect/gather/clarify → the plan is composed now from what is on file; spawning → the personas already written are kept. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/quant-search` | `{query, sources[], build_id?}` — search the chosen publishers now (background); pages land as `quant` evidence and stream as `research_item` + `population_log`; `population_quant_done` marks the end. **400** on an empty query. |
| GET | `/kits` | `{kits[], benchmarks[]}` — the population kits (§7.14: id, title, segments, share presets) and the real polls on file (§11a.8). |
| GET | `/kits/{kit_id}` | The whole kit (segments, distributions, cited beliefs, frame, assumptions). **404** for an unknown kit. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/population/kit` | `{kit_id, count, mode, preset?, seed?, approve?}` — a build planned from the kit: every segment accepted, cards drawn, frame attached, status `awaiting_review` (or spawning at once with `approve: true`). Supersedes any active build. |
| GET | `/benchmarks/{bid}/form` | The real poll's questionnaire as a Forms spec (also offered as the survey template *Real poll · …*). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/benchmarks/{bid}/run` | `{mode, seed?}` — run that questionnaire on every agent (a `survey` probe). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/probes/{pid}/benchmark` | The same scorecard against whichever real poll the run's form reproduces (matched by question keys); **404** when none. `?estimator=auto\|draws\|likelihood`. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/probes/{pid}/benchmark/{bid}` | The scorecard: `headline`, `per_question`, `by_column` (with uniform and national baselines), `segment_gradients`, `units` (per option real vs simulated). `?min_n=` drops thin cells (default 5). |

**Agents** (no prefix, mounted at `/api/v1`)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/agents` | List a session's agents. |
| GET | `/agents/{agent_id}` | Fetch one agent. |
| POST | `/agents/{agent_id}/chat` | Chat 1:1 with an agent (KG-grounded). History is in-memory/process-local, not persisted. |

**Reports** (`/sessions`)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/report/generate` | The report proper (§11, brief L6-01 / L6-02): `{question?, mode?}` (the section spec is server-side; a non-empty `question` overrides it). **One at a time per session (2026-10-05, `run_report`)**: the write runs as a shielded task on its own database session, a second call while it is in flight awaits the same task and gets the same report, and a browser whose call was cut off finds the finished report in `report/history` (rows now carry `created_at`). → ensures the headline outcome record (runs the hidden `verdict` probe when the roster changed — slow), loads every record, the evidence by class and the verdict positions, writes the narrative around them; returns `{id, question, answer, sources, records[], structure}`. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/export.zip` | The structured export (§11, brief L6-06 / L6-07): run record, records as JSON and flat CSVs, the latest report with its structure and as a client document, the source ledger, the roster; every file stamped with the synthetic-population statement. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/report/client` | The client-facing document as data + markdown (brief L6-06 / L6-07). |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/report/delta` | What changed between two reports proper (`?a=&b=`, default the latest against the one before; brief L6-06); 404 with fewer than two. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/scoping/coverage` | Per-twin scoping records for the Agents tab (§9.6): tagged / stale, unit count, and for each twin visible of total units, routes, snapshot, what it was written from. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/scoping/ensure` | Tag now if untagged or stale, then annotate every twin; returns the scoping state. The pipeline does this on its own; this is the manual form. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/figures` | The source-figure ledger (§11, brief L6-03): `{facts[], items[]}` — every typed statistic and evidence item on file with its provenance class; what `[[fact:…]]` / `[[evidence:…]]` citations resolve to. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/records` | Every outcome record on file: `{records[]}`, the headline verdict first. |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/report/query` | Ask the report engine (`{question}`) with the records on file as context; persists a `ReportQuery`. |
| GET | `/sessions/{id}/report/history` | All report Q&A for the session (`{id, question, answer, sources, structure?}`; `structure` is set on reports proper, null on Ask-Report follow-ups). |

**Presets** (`/presets`)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/presets` | List presets (header only: id, name, agent_count, created_at). |
| GET | `/presets/{id}` | One preset with its `agents[]`. |
| POST | `/presets` | Save the current session's population as a named preset. |
| POST | `/presets/custom` | Agent Builder (§7.10): a new lineup from hand-authored agents `{name, agents[], as_archetype?}` (`as_archetype` also stores each agent as an archetype, §7.11). **400** when an agent lacks a name / role / description or a complete 112-dial profile. |
| POST | `/presets/{id}/agents` | Append hand-authored agents to a lineup `{agents[], as_archetype?}`. **409** when a name is already in it. |
| DELETE | `/presets/{id}` | Delete a preset. |
| GET | `/archetypes` | The caller's archetypes (§7.11): `{id, name, role, summary, created_at}`. |
| GET | `/archetypes/{id}` | One archetype with its full `profile`. |
| DELETE | `/archetypes/{id}` | Delete an archetype (204). |
| POST | `/sessions/{id}/agent-builder/profile` | Build sentiment profile (§7.10): `{agent}` (the draft; `dials` holds only the fixed values) → `{dials (all 112, fixed ones kept), humanity, reading}`. **502** when the model fails. |

**Non-versioned:** `GET /health` → `{status, auth, auth_issuer, database, database_ok, database_error, schema_ok, schema_error}` (`status: degraded` when the DB probe or the startup migration failed; the DB probe is cached 30 s so a wrong password cannot trip the Supabase pooler's circuit breaker); `WS /ws/{session_id}` → live event stream (keepalive `ping` on 30s idle).

### 12.3 WebSocket event vocabulary
All JSON, `type`-tagged, published to channel `session:{id}`:

| Event | Payload | Emitted when |
|---|---|---|
| `agent_spawned` | `{agent, index, total}` | each agent created (apply-preset path) |
| `agents_spawned_batch` | `{agents[], spawned, total}` | a chunk (~40) of a freshly spawned population (Fast/Pro spawn) |
| `agents_ready` | `{count}` | population complete |
| `spawn_error` | `{error}` | spawn failed |
| `ingest_complete` | `{source}` | an ingest finished |
| `kg_updated` | `{new_entities, new_relations, source}` | KG grew (ingest or a post) |
| `simulation_started` | `{agent_count}` | debate began |
| `post_created` | `{post, agent}` | an agent posted |
| `like_added` | `{post_id, agent_id, agent_name, new_likes}` | an agent liked a post |
| `simulation_complete` | `{message}` | all rounds done |
| `probe_started` | `{probe_id, instrument, agent_count}` | a probe began |
| `probe_answer` | `{probe_id, agent_id, agent_name, avatar_color, answer, segments}` | one agent answered (streams into the Lab's dot grid) |
| `probe_complete` | `{probe_id, status, answer_count, failed_count, sentence}` | a probe finished; aggregates are on the probe row |
| `experiment_started` | `{experiment_id, probe_ids, agent_count, design}` | an A/B test began; its arms then stream as ordinary `probe_*` events |
| `experiment_complete` | `{experiment_id, status, verdict}` | the comparison is stored on the experiment row |
| `population_log` | `{build_id, entry{ts, stage, level, message, detail}}` | one line of a Population Studio build's log (§7.8); `build_id` is null for a standalone statistics search |
| `population_build` | `{build}` | a build's state changed (stage, detected, questions, plan, decisions) — the whole row |
| `population_quant_done` | `{build_id}` | a statistics search finished |
| `ping` | — | 30s keepalive |

### 12.4 Usage monitoring — AI Agent Pulse (direct-to-Supabase, no n8n)

Optional integration that reports **exact LLM token usage and cost** to the shared **AI Agent Pulse** monitoring dashboard. Pulse's standard onboarding assumes a project runs **in n8n** (an n8n "Token Logger" workflow scrapes token *estimates* from the n8n execution API). 11 Minds calls the Anthropic SDK directly, so it instead logs **its own exact usage straight to Supabase**.

- **Code:** `backend/app/core/monitoring.py`. `tracked_messages_create(client, *, session_id, label, **kwargs)` wraps every **async** `messages.create` (9 call sites: `post`, `chat`, `spawn`, `report`, `kg_extract`, `kg_query`, `opinions`, `llm_classify`, `llm_paper`); `record_usage_sync(...)` covers the 2 **synchronous** Vision paths (`youtube_vision`, `doc_vision`). The `label` is stored as `execution_id` so the dashboard shows *what kind* of call it was; `session_id` is the `AnalysisSession.id`.
- **Exact, not estimated:** it reads `response.usage.input_tokens` / `output_tokens` directly off the SDK response, then **fire-and-forgets** a row to `POST {SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/execution_logs` (PostgREST). It never sends `cost_usd` — the table's `calculate_cost()` trigger computes it from `llm_pricing` on insert.
- **Safe & opt-in:** a complete **no-op** unless `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` and `PULSE_PROJECT_ID` are all set; every failure is swallowed (logging never blocks or breaks a request).
- **Model strings emitted** (must exist in `llm_pricing` or cost stays NULL → `pricing_gaps`): `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` (Fast posts + all KG/report/opinions/classify/paper + YouTube Vision), `claude-sonnet-4-6` (Pro spawn + posts), `claude-opus-4-5-20251101` (document image Vision). All three are already priced in the shared Pulse table.
- **Onboarding (replaces Pulse's n8n "Step 3"):** (1) register the project in `project_registry`; (2) confirm the three model strings above are priced; (3) set the three env vars. Steps 1, 2 and 4 (verify) are unchanged.

---

## 13. Frontend reference

### 13.1 Tech & design
Next.js 14.2 (App Router, `src/app/`) + React 18 + TypeScript 5. Tailwind 3.4 with a **dark-only**, CSS-variable theme (near-black neutral background with a faint cool cast, hairline borders, **teal** as the one accent; emerald / amber / red / sky reserved for status). `globals.css` carries a small design system used across the app (2026-09-29 restyle): `.surface` / `.glass` (one quiet card style — `.glass` is kept as an alias so older screens calmed down without a rewrite), `.btn` + `.btn-primary` / `.btn-secondary` / `.btn-ghost` / `.btn-outline` / `.btn-danger` / `.btn-accent` (+ `.btn-sm` / `.btn-xs`), `.field` (+ `.field-sm`), `.chip` + `.chip-on` / `.chip-ok` / `.chip-warn` / `.chip-bad` / `.chip-info` / `.chip-violet` / `.chip-outline`, `.seg` / `.seg-item[data-on]` (segmented control), `.switch`, `.dot`, `.section-title` / `.eyebrow` / `.hint`, and restyled native controls (range sliders with a filled track driven by `--range-pct` / `--range-fill`, custom select arrow, no number spinners). `components/population/controls.tsx` wraps them as `Range`, `Switch`, `SwitchRow`, `Seg`, `Section`, `Divider`. Icons via `lucide-react`. `clsx` + `tailwind-merge` (`cn()`). System fonts only. Radix + framer-motion are installed but largely unused (UI is hand-rolled). `next.config.js` inlines `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` (default `http://localhost:8000`) and `NEXT_PUBLIC_WS_URL` (default `ws://localhost:8000`) at build time.

### 13.2 Pages
- **`/login`** — sign in / create account / magic link / **forgot password → emailed reset link → set a new password** (Supabase Auth; a recovery link lands on `/login?mode=reset` or with `type=recovery` in the URL fragment and shows the new-password form). A wrong password gets a plain-language message pointing at password managers and the reset flow. **Every emailed link (confirmation, magic link, reset) is redirected by Supabase to the project's Site URL unless the app's origin is in the Redirect URLs allow-list** — Authentication → URL Configuration must have **Site URL = `https://populations.11-minds.com`** (the frontend's custom domain, Railway service `amiable-courage`) and Redirect URLs listing `https://populations.11-minds.com/**` and `https://11-population.up.railway.app/**` (the same frontend's Railway domain), otherwise links land on `localhost:3000`. The `11-Population` Railway service (`11-population-production.up.railway.app`) is the backend API and is never a redirect target. Every other page is wrapped in `RequireAuth` (root layout) and redirects here with `?next=` when signed out. The Supabase project is resolved at runtime: build-time `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` if present, else the backend's `GET /api/v1/config`, else the gate is a no-op. The landing header shows the signed-in email, an **Admin** chip for admins, and Sign out; admins get a **Mine / All users** toggle on the session list.
- **`/admin`** — admin-only user list (email, role, session count, joined) with Make admin / Make member.
- **`app/page.tsx`** — landing: create-session form + recent-sessions list + the four use-case cards + hero illustration (`/minds-network.png`) + a **"no database" notice** warning that sessions aren't persisted (a restart/redeploy wipes them, so stale sessions can 404). A **Simple / Pro** switch in the header (`lib/lite.useUiMode`, `localStorage` key `ui_mode`) renders **`components/lite/LiteHome`** in its place when *Simple* is remembered: the light, airy simple home — the question (title auto-filled from it, editable), an *Add anything* box that classifies what is dropped or pasted (file → `ingest/document`, YouTube link → `ingest/youtube`, other link → `ingest/url`, text → `ingest/text`; items are held locally and ingested one by one after the session is created with `auto_research: true`), a single switch, **Research the web for me** (on by default; off sends `auto_research: false` so the people read only what was added — the one setting the simple home has, because research takes 3–5 minutes), **Start**, and *Your questions* (the reader's sessions with a plain-words status, each opening `/lite/[id]`, each with a *See in detail* pill into `/session/[id]`). **The simple view's cover (2026-10-02; `components/lite/LiteHome`):** a ten-second looping film of three illustrated people (`public/minds-people.mp4`) stands above the headline or, on a wide screen (≥1280px), fills the right-hand column of a wider two-column grid (80rem, 96rem from 1536px) and is centred on the question block (headline to Start), the list of questions running on below the question block alone (2026-10-05; before, the grid was 72rem wide, the film was small and pinned to the top of a narrow column with empty page around it, and it was drawn at 720×405). The film is drawn frame by frame onto a canvas at its own 1280×720 and its white ground keyed out pixel by pixel (whiteness ≥250 → transparent, a soft step down to 240), so the people stand on the tinted page rather than in a white box — a CSS blend mode on `<video>` was not honoured everywhere. Decorative only (`aria-hidden`); with *prefers-reduced-motion* it shows the first frame and does not play.
- **`app/lite/[id]/page.tsx`** — the **simple view of a session** (§4). Light theme (`.lite` redefines the design tokens; `useLiteRoot` paints `html`/`body` light while mounted) — and, since the pro components are reused as they are, `globals.css` carries a block of `.lite` overrides that map their dark-portal tints to light-page colours: the status chips (`chip-ok` …), the `text-*-300` labels, and (2026-10-05) every tint the report uses — teal text, washes, borders and dots become the simple view's `--primary`; yellow / amber / emerald / red / sky / pink / orange / indigo / violet / slate / zinc text steps down to its 700 weight; the position bar and evidence dots keep saturated 500-weight fills — so `ReportChat` reads in the simple palette without a fork, one quiet header (`components/lite/LiteShell`: back, title, the Ask · People · Conversation · Report · Tools step rail — **buttons** on a session page (`onStep`): *Ask* opens `?view=ask` (the question; a *Research the web* card that says where research stands — running with dots, done with the count of useful pages, stopped early — with **Start research** / **Research again** calling `research/start`; and an *Add more material* box that ingests at once), *People* opens `?view=people` (`ReadyCard`: who was asked, each name a way into the chat box; or the progress card / the people form while there is no roster — and, under the roster, **How these people were made** (`components/lite/HowMade`, 2026-10-05; drawn rather than listed since the same day): the latest Studio build read out visually, nothing recomputed, across the full page width (`max-w-6xl`, like the conversation; the roster card above it is the same width with four columns of names and 16 shown before *Show all*) — one lead sentence saying what the people were written from (question, the reader's description, their survey, the pages found online if the research had finished before the plan, published statistics); the cards in a two-column grid on a wide screen (*What went in* and *How they talk* span both columns; the rest pair up: who they stand for | the groups, matched to published figures | what had to be assumed); *Where these people came from* (2026-10-05, in place of the ingredient tiles) as a **flow diagram**: three columns joined by connectors drawn in an SVG from the boxes' measured positions — *What went in* (Your question · Your description · Your survey · Things you added, counted from `GET …/kg/sources` · Found online · Published statistics, each with its real figure, faint with a dotted line when unused, amber and dashed straight into the writing step when the research landed after the plan), *The engine · Population Studio* (numbered steps read from the build log — understand who to ask, gather published figures, plan the groups, write the people, check they behave when validation ran — each marked done / with gaps / running / failed / skipped with an icon and a word) and *What came out* (the people with a share bar of the groups, the conversation with its post count, the report); the engine's steps and the three outputs link to the pro page where they are analysed, while **each input box opens a summary dialog** (2026-10-06; `components/lite/InputDialog`, portalled, Escape or the backdrop closes it, *See it in Pro* at its foot opens the input's pro page): a lead paragraph written from the record with every figure in bold, a row of figure tiles, then sections with tables and outside links where the data has them — *Your question* (the question quoted; words, how sure the engine is who to ask, the signals read from the wording, the question's own dials; a table of who to ask / where / topic / decision / kind of population from `detected`; the groups it hinted at; the dials with their ends and why), *Your description* and *Your survey* (the text quoted, or a *Read it all* clamp for a long survey; words, characters, the signals taken from it by `demographic_signals.source`; the frame dimensions whose figure came from the upload as a shares table; or, when none was given, what one would do and where to add it), *Things you added* (items, pieces of knowledge and their share of the graph's chunks, entities and relations from `GET …/kg/sources`; a table of item · kind · pieces with a link for a page or video), *Found online* (run status and when it finished, searches by source, pages read, on topic and in the graph, whether it landed before or after the plan; a by-source table; the brief's summary and key facts with the figures bold and its gaps; the on-topic pages ranked by relevance with the page reader's reason under each title, a relevance bar and an outside link — `GET …/evidence` is fetched when the dialog opens; the kept posts with subreddit and score; the searches with found / read / on-topic counts), *Published statistics* (publishers searched as chips, each carrying the number of statistics it gave; what the people had to match as a table of dimension · standing · shares and source; the statistics found — statistic, value in bold, who · where · when, a link to the publisher's page — from the quant evidence rows' `structured.facts`; the TAM/SAM/SOM sizing when the frame has it; or, when off, what turning it on would do); below 768px the columns stack with arrows between them; the description clamped to two lines with *Read it all*, and, beside it, the publishers searched as small chips under *Statistics searched* with a line saying how many of the frame's dimensions found a figure; **the five reading cards are folded (2026-10-06; `Fold`)**: each shows its heading and one line read from the same record — *who*: the population and *n% sure*; *groups*: the two largest with their shares and *k more*; *matched*: what matched, how many assumed, how many with no figure, the overall level; *assumed*: the evidence share when the coverage line states one, the count of assumptions and of things not found; *voice*: *Like experts* / *Between experts and ordinary people* / *Like ordinary people* and the first sentence of the reason — and a chevron (or the heading) opens the card, the line giving way to the content; closed by default so the page reads as findings first, the flow card above stays open; *Who they stand for* (`detected.target_population` and geography, each demographic signal as a chip with a small tag of where it came from, and a **confidence ring** — amber under 60%, with a one-line caution); *The N groups* (`plan.rationale`; a **share bar** — one validated categorical colour per group in share order, 2px gaps, a ninth and beyond fold into a grey *Other* — hovering a segment dims the rest and highlights its row; under it one row per group: colour dot, full name, *k people · s%*, and a second line with stance and mood in words and ✓ *published figure* or ⚠ *assumed*; a click opens the group's description, ages / places / income / example jobs and its *Based on:* evidence line or the assumed rationale); *Matched to published figures* as a **heat grid** (one row per sampling-frame dimension: its status with a coloured square, icon and word — Published figure / Closest figure / Your upload / Assumed / No figure found — plus source and year; to the right the frame report's cells as one-hue heat blocks, paler the closer the people's share sits to the published share (4-point steps), each labelled with the people's share and a hover title with both figures; a dashed *not matched* or *the people were not placed on this scale* box when there are no usable cells), then an **Overall match** four-step meter (none / poor / fair / good) with a caution under *poor*); *What had to be assumed* (`plan.evidence_coverage`, then `plan.assumptions`, the estimated dimensions and how many groups rest on general knowledge, each with a warning icon; `detected.gaps` as dashed chips under *Could not be found*); *How they talk* (a **scale** from *Like experts* to *Like ordinary people* with the voice value as a marker, then `plan.voice.reason`). Status colours never stand alone — each carries an icon and a word. Every card hovers *See in detail* into the Studio (the ingredients card into the Ingest tab). A roster with no Studio plan (an older session, or the pro spawn button) gets one card saying the people were written straight from the question and what was added, with three stance tiles), *Conversation* the flow, *Report* the report, *Tools* (2026-10-06) the Forms screen (`?view=lab`), which is lit as the current step while it is open — and a *Full portal* pill). Its own slim state (`components/lite/useLiteSession`: session, roster, posts, the latest build, research state, verdicts, the last report and its chat — the same endpoints and socket events the pro page uses, 8 s poll of the session **and the latest build**; the first build lookup retries three times before giving up and exposes `buildLoaded`, and `runSimulation` looks the build up once more and does nothing if one is in flight). Until the latest build is known, a session with no roster shows a quiet *Checking where this question is…* line rather than the people form (2026-10-05: the form reappearing over a build still running on the server read as *it stopped*, and a second *Run* then superseded the live build). Screens by state: **Who should we ask?** (description + survey file: text-like files become `doc_context`, anything else is ingested as a document instead; **Run simulation** → `population.start` with `skip_questions`, `quant_auto` and `auto_run`), **Getting everyone ready** (reading your material / creating the people n of N / starting the conversation, mapped from the build status; *Running for N min* counts from the build's own `created_at`, so leaving and returning shows the true elapsed time, and the lead says the work runs on the server and carries on if the tab is closed; *Try again* and a detail link on failure; above the steps, `components/lite/ConjureGrid` — a crowd of procedurally drawn little people conjured one by one with a job label, looping on its own; decoration only, not tied to the real build's timing or count), **N people are ready** (a roster built in the pro portal with no debate yet: *Start the conversation*), **the conversation** (`ThreadView` as it is, `onTalk` on every person; a floating bar: *They're talking it through · n messages · Stop* while it runs, then **Talk to a person · Make the report · Lab tools**), `?view=report` (the session question in a slim *Your question* strip above the report, two lines at most with the full text on hover, hovering *See in detail* into the Ingest tab — then `ReportChat` as it is, or a *Make the report* card; **a cut-off call does not fail the report (2026-10-05)**: `makeReport` snapshots the history's ids, marks the session *pending*, calls `report.generate` with its ten-minute timeout, and if the call is cut off shows a calm *the server is still writing it — waiting* line and polls the history until the new report lands; a page reopened within 15 minutes of asking, with no report landed since, resumes that wait instead of offering *Make the report* again), `?view=lab` (**Forms**, 2026-10-06 — `components/lite/LiteForms`, §11a.3: the colleague who has read the session, write-the-questions-for-me, drop or paste a questionnaire, one plain card per question, *Ask the N people*, the answers through the pro `SurveyPage`; before 2026-10-06 a placeholder with a link into the pro Lab). **`components/lite/TalkBox`** is the floating chat: pick a person (*Change*), hover the name for `PersonaName`'s card (age, role, place, stance in words, segment, verdict, first line of their background, *See in detail* → the agent page), openers, the same `agents.chat` endpoint. **`components/lite/Detail`** wraps anything with the hover pill **See in detail ↗** (`DetailLink` is the inline form); `lib/lite.proLinks` names the pro page for each thing (`?tab=` on the session page, the Studio, the agent page). A build in flight is one with an active status, or `awaiting_review` with `auto_run` (a plan waiting for a person in the pro Studio shows the people form instead).
- **`app/session/[id]/page.tsx`** — the **orchestrator component**: holds all shared state (session, agents, posts, KG entities/relations/activity, spawn progress, report, opinions), runs the WS dispatcher, polls every 8s, defines the `REPORT_PROMPT`, and renders the six tabs (Ingest · Agents · Thread · **Lab** · Graph · Report — shown to the reader as *Sources · Twins · Debate · Lab · What they know · Report*, numbered 1–5 with done-ticks and a *Next:* button), and reloads the last report proper and the Ask-Report history from `report/history` so a refresh keeps them.
- **`app/session/[id]/agents/[agentId]/page.tsx`** — standalone full-page 1:1 agent chat.
- **`app/session/[id]/population/page.tsx`** — the **Population Studio** (§7.8), reached only from the Agents tab. One 56px rail (title, status chip, the six-stage progress rail — Detect · Gather · Clarify · Plan · Review · Build — and the Sampling frame / N agents / Stop actions) over three columns: **Inputs** (what the plan reads — brief groups, graph size, statistics on file; the analyst's audience profile and survey upload; the statistics publishers to tick — gathered automatically during Detect & plan, with a live status line — the fact cards with their quotes and an ignore/use toggle, and a collapsed optional lookup box), **Build** (a stage stepper, the streaming build log, the detected-population card, the clarifying-questions card, and the plan review — one card per segment with share, count, stance, mood, **register chip**, demographics, the "why this segment" rationale, its arguments and evidence, and Accept / Edit (incl. register) / Reject-with-reason controls), and **Dials** (population size, Fast/Pro, demographics, sentiment, stance mix; humanity is a note pointing at the per-segment register) with the primary action for the current state: *Detect & plan*, *Approve & build N agents* + *Re-plan with these dials*, or *Stop*. The session page honours `?tab=agents` so the Studio can send the analyst back to the roster.

### 13.3 Components
- **`ingestion/InputPanel`** — the four ingestion modes (Text / File drag-drop / YouTube / LLM Search with generate→preview→ingest), with an "ingested sources" tracker and a "Continue to Agents" gate.
- **`simulation/AgentDirectory`** — three phases: **(1) the Population Studio**, embedded (`population/PopulationStudio`, the same component the `/population` route renders; `embedded` drops the back arrow; saved lineups are offered under its empty state; it stays open through a build until *View agents*), **(2) live spawn dashboard** (mode-aware elapsed/ETA KPIs, agents streaming in via `agents_spawned_batch` — only the latest 60 cards are rendered at scale), **(3) ready/simulating/complete** roster grouped by stance (capped at 60 cards/stance with a "+N more" note) plus a compact **run-settings card** (mode + intensity) shown before Start. Includes `AgentCard` (stance pill, **"% human" badge**, **dominant-emotion chips**) + a **`DialViewer`** that renders all 9 dial groups as collapsible bars.
- **`simulation/AgentBuilder`** — the *Build your own agent* page (§7.10) at `/session/[id]/agents/builder`: the person in words (identity, description, stance, Expert ↔ Reactive, place, the five Character fields) on the left, the 112 dials with per-dial hand / system state and *Build sentiment profile* on the right, and the lineup target (new or existing) in a sticky footer.
- **`population/PlanReview`** — the plan's segment cards; each carries an `ArchetypePicker` (§7.11: *cast from …* / *model invents* chip plus a select over the analyst's archetypes) and the header counts segments cast from archetypes. `PopulationStudio` loads `api.archetypes.list()` and shows how many are on file under its empty state.
- **`population/KitPicker`** (2026-10-08) — the Studio's *Or start from a published segmentation* card (§7.14): one row per kit with its segments and a *segment shares* select; *Plan N agents* calls `POST /sessions/{id}/population/kit` and the plan review opens.
- **`population/DialsPanel`, `SourcesPanel`, `BuildLog` (+ `Stepper`, `QuestionsCard`), `PlanReview`** — the Studio's four panels (§7.8, §13.2). `AgentCard` shows a **segment chip** and a **demographics line** (gender · place · income) for Studio-built agents.
- **`simulation/ThreadView` + `PostCard`** — the live, threaded debate feed (debate flags, like counts, markdown rendering) + a per-agent **opinions** sidebar. The sidebar shows, per agent: the Claude verdict if available → a live "summarising…" indicator **only while a request is in flight** → otherwise an italic excerpt of the agent's first post → "forming opinion…" if it hasn't posted. Its header carries a **Refresh** button (or **Retry** + the error text when generation failed). Auto-scroll **sticks to the bottom only while the user is already there** — scrolling up to read is never interrupted by incoming posts, and a floating **"Jump to latest"** button appears when scrolled away.
- **`PersonaAvatar`** (2026-10-02) — every twin's portrait, drawn in code from its record (no image assets): the demographics' gender picks the hair cut (long / bun / fringe, or short / cropped; a roster written before the Studio recorded gender falls back to a list of common first names, and a name on neither list or a non-binary twin draws from both), age greys the hair (from 45 up, certain from 68), adds glasses more often and fine lines from 60 (more from 72) and sometimes thinning hair for older men, the role adds a hard hat (builders, electricians, plumbers…) or a chef's toque (chefs, bakers, cooks), the twin's `avatar_color` becomes the shirt and the tint behind the face so it stays recognisable beside the dots and chips that use the colour, and skin, smile and earrings come from a hash of the id so the same person always looks the same. Used wherever a twin is shown: the roster card, the thread and its opinions sidebar, the agent chat page, the report's twin picker and popover, and every simple-view surface. `lookFor(agent)` derives the look; `Face` draws one; the simple view's `ConjureGrid` draws its crowd with the same `Face`.
- **`simulation/SimulationControls`** — header Pause/Resume/Stop (Start lives in AgentDirectory). `ThreadView` and `PostCard` take an optional `onTalk(agent)` (the simple view): a small *Talk* control beside each name in the thread and on each opinions card; absent in the pro portal, where nothing is rendered.
- **`knowledge-graph/KGPanel`** — the Graph tab: a **view dropdown** (*Knowledge graph* / *Ontology*) above either the original SVG node-graph (ring layout, live "new entity" flashes, live activity feed, click-to-inspect entity detail panel) or **`knowledge-graph/OntologyView`** (§9.5 — schema graph before a build, swim-lane typed graph after; build/rebuild, stale warning, class legend with hide-on-click, node detail with typed edges and mentions), or **`knowledge-graph/ScopingView`** (§9.6 — tag/re-tag, facet coverage with click-to-filter, unit cards; a twin picker with the exposure profile and its bases, Sees / Hidden / Prompt block / Policy panels). `KGPanel` now takes `agents` from the session page.
- **`lab/LabPanel` + `lab/InstrumentForm` + `lab/pages/*` + `lab/Charts`** — the **Lab** tab (§11a). `LabPanel` is a **shell**: tool picker (one card per instrument with its icon from the `lab/icons.ts` registry, its KPI chips and its run count, plus the A/B card), run/stop, live streaming, cost estimate, CSV, **past runs** (a collapsible list under the picker — count badge, per-row icon, one-line result, answered/agents or the variants, relative time, a status pill when not complete, and a hover trash icon with an inline Delete/Keep confirm; running rows and A/B arms cannot be deleted; the open/closed choice is remembered in `localStorage`) and provenance — and nothing instrument-specific (there is no `if instrument === …` in it). `InstrumentForm` renders a tool's input panel from its own declaration; `pages/index.ts` maps an instrument to its own results page (`PurchaseIntentPage`, `AskPage`, `SurveyPage`, `VerdictPage`, `BarriersPage`, `JourneyPage`), falling back to `GenericPage` when a tool has none yet; `forms/index.ts` maps a tool's `form` key to its own builder (`SurveyBuilder`, `JourneyBuilder` — the latter takes `sessionId` so it can ask the backend to propose the journey); hidden instruments are not shown in the picker. `InstrumentForm` renders `suggestions` as one-click chips under a free-text input. The shell routes between its two primitives: a probe (this panel) and an experiment — **`lab/ExperimentPanel`** + **`lab/pages/ExperimentPage`** (§11a.7), the A/B tool, itself generic over instruments. `lab/filters.ts` holds the shared "who answers" segment filters. Left: instrument picker (populated from `GET /lab/instruments`), stimulus editor, asking price/currency, "who answers" segment filters, Fast/Pro model choice, a live **cost estimate before you run**, and the list of past runs. Right: while the probe runs, a **dot grid — one dot per agent, coloured by its answer** — fills in from `probe_answer` events with the latest reasonings underneath; when it finishes, the headline share with its **confidence band**, the plain-language sentence, mean stats, decision and driver bars, the **demand curve** (with the asking price and the revenue-optimal point marked), segment splits (thin buckets greyed, not hidden), verbatims, a CSV export, and a provenance line (model, seed, schema, how many failed and were excluded). Charts are hand-rolled inline **SVG** — no chart library (`Charts.tsx`: `ShareBar`, `CategoryBars`, `DemandCurve`, `DotGrid`, `MeanStat`, `SegmentTable`, `LiftBar`, `HeatCell`, `dotColor`, `fmtLevel`/`fmtLift`). **Inside a tool, its own past runs are listed too (2026-10-01):** the tool's right pane, before any run is open, lists that tool's runs (newest first, open / delete) under its question, and the left column keeps a compact *Past runs with this tool* list for switching between runs while reading one; the A/B tool does the same with its experiments (`past` prop from the shell). The Lab-level *Past runs* list stays as the cross-tool view.
- **`lab/forms/FormsStudio` + `lab/forms/QuestionEditor` + `lab/forms/formUtils`** (2026-10-06) — the Forms studio, the Survey instrument's builder given the whole Lab tab (§11a.3). `forms/index.ts` now also declares each builder's **layout** (`formLayout`: `side` — the Lab's 340px column — or `full`), and `InstrumentFormProps` gains `controls`, `aside` and `onBack`, which the shell passes to a full-layout builder; the shell still names no instrument. `SurveyBuilder` is gone (its question card lives on as `QuestionEditor`, with `FormPreview`, `TYPES`, `blank`, `nextKey`, `problemsOf`); `formUtils` holds the draft helpers and the bold-and-bullets renderer shared with `lite/LiteForms`.
- **`report/ReportChat`** — the report document renderer (`ReportDocument`/`parseReport`: direct-answer callout, confidence badge, the **outcome-records grid** and `[[record:…]]` chips with the **`RecordCard`** popover, §11) + dual-mode chat (Ask Report / Talk to Agent) + Save-as-PDF + Regenerate.

### 13.4 API client & WebSocket
- **`lib/api.ts`** — thin REST client over `${NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL}/api/v1` (`request(path, options, timeoutMs = 120 s)`; `report.generate` passes ten minutes), grouped namespaces (`sessions`, `research`, `ingest`, `simulation`, `agents`, `report`, `presets`, `lab`), plus full TypeScript types (`Session`, `Agent`, `AgentDials`, `Post`, `SpawnOptions`, `AgentPreset`, `Instrument`, `Probe`, `ProbeAggregates`, `Interval`, `Experiment`, `ExperimentRequest`, `ExperimentResults`, `Comparison`, `Lift`, and the `WSEvent` discriminated union). File uploads use `FormData` directly; the probe and experiment CSVs go through `apiFetch` and downloads as a blob so the auth header is attached. The KG is fetched directly (no `api.kg` namespace).
- **`lib/supabase.ts` / `lib/auth.tsx`** — the `supabase-js` client, `getAccessToken()`, `authHeaders()`, `AuthProvider` (session state) and `RequireAuth` (route gate). `api.ts` attaches the bearer token to every call (`apiFetch` for multipart) and sends a 401 to `/login`.
- **`lib/reportWait.ts`** (2026-10-05) — waiting for a report the server is still writing: `snapshotReportIds` before the call, `waitForNewReport` polls `report/history` every 10 s (up to 12 min) for a report proper not in that set, `isCutOff` tells a timeout / dropped connection from a refusal, and `markReportPending` / `reportPendingSince` / `clearReportPending` keep a per-session marker in `localStorage` so a page reopened mid-write resumes waiting. Used by the simple view's `makeReport` and the pro page's *Make the report*.
- **`lib/websocket.ts`** — a **singleton per session** (`getSessionWS(id)`): one `SessionWebSocket` shared by all subscribers, auto-reconnect after 3s on close, `subscribe(fn)` returns an unsubscribe closure. No auth on the socket.

### 13.5 Live-update plumbing
The session page subscribes once and maps each `WSEvent` to state: `agent_spawned`→append+progress, `agents_ready`→stop spinner, `post_created`→append only (**no forced tab switch** — the user can sit on any tab while posts stream), `like_added`→update count, `kg_updated`→grow graph + prepend activity, `ingest_complete`/`simulation_complete`→refresh (the latter also regenerates opinions). An 8s poll backstops the socket.

**Opinion loading (`loadOpinions` in the session page):** verdicts are seeded from `agent.verdict` on every roster refresh (so a reloaded session shows them instantly with no LLM call); the first generation fires once posts ≥ agents (skipped if every posting agent already has a persisted verdict); the call is retried up to 3× with backoff on network/LLM failure; a failure surfaces as an error + Retry button in the sidebar instead of an endless "summarising…".

## 14. Tech stack summary

| Layer | Technology |
|---|---|
| Frontend | Next.js 14 (App Router), React 18, TypeScript 5, Tailwind 3, lucide-react |
| Backend | FastAPI 0.115, uvicorn, Python (3.11 in Docker / 3.9 in nixpacks) |
| Data (relational) | SQLAlchemy 2.0 async; SQLite (local) or Postgres (prod via `DATABASE_URL`) |
| Data (knowledge graph) | Per-session JSON files on disk (`lightrag_data/{id}/kg.json`) |
| Realtime | Native WebSocket + **in-process** asyncio pub/sub (no external broker) |
| AI | Anthropic Claude (orchestration / agents / fast tiers; Vision for images & video) |
| Ingestion | pypdf, python-docx, openpyxl, xlrd, python-pptx, striprtf, lxml, Pillow, yt-dlp, faster-whisper (+ ffmpeg) |
| Deploy | Railway via Nixpacks (Dockerfiles also present) |

## 15. Deployment & operations

### 15.1 Local development
`./start.sh` from the repo root: brings up Postgres 16 + Redis 7 via `docker-compose.yml` (Redis is vestigial — unused), creates/activates the backend venv and installs requirements, runs **backend** `uvicorn app.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --reload`, and **frontend** `npm run dev` (port 3000). Without `DATABASE_URL`, the backend uses SQLite at `backend/eleven_minds.db`. API docs at `http://localhost:8000/docs`.

### 15.2 Production (Railway)
Two services, each built from its own `nixpacks.toml`:
- **Backend:** nixpacks installs `python39` + `ffmpeg`, `pip install -r requirements.txt`, starts `uvicorn app.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port $PORT` (single process, no workers).
- **Frontend:** nixpacks installs `nodejs_20`, `npm ci`, `npm run build`, starts `npm start` → `next start -p $PORT`. **`NEXT_PUBLIC_*` are inlined at build time** — they must be set as Railway build variables *before* build or the bundle falls back to localhost.

Dockerfiles also exist (backend `python:3.11-slim` + ffmpeg/gcc; frontend multi-stage `node:20-alpine`) as an alternative path. There is **no `railway.json`/`Procfile`/CI**.

**Database & auth (Supabase project "11 Minds Population", `pkgfqfdnmikolrbampig`, eu-west-1):** the frontend no longer needs Supabase build variables (it reads `/api/v1/config` from the backend); the backend needs `DATABASE_URL` (use the **session pooler** string, port 5432, host **`aws-1-eu-west-1.pooler.supabase.com`** — the `aws-0` host answers `tenant/user … not found` for this project; Railway is IPv4-only; transaction mode on 6543 is also handled: NullPool + prepared statements off), `APP_SUPABASE_URL` and `APP_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`. The frontend build needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`. On startup the backend runs `alembic upgrade head`; the project was created with the `0001_initial` migration applied and stamped, so the first boot is a no-op. Sign-ups are open with email confirmation (Supabase's built-in mailer, rate-limited); restrict via Authentication → Providers in the dashboard if needed.

### 15.3 Environment variables
| Var | Service | Purpose | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | backend | Claude auth — **mandatory for all intelligence** | `""` (must set) |
| `DATABASE_URL` | backend | Supabase Postgres DSN (session pooler; rewritten to `postgresql+asyncpg://`); unset → SQLite | unset → SQLite |
| `APP_SUPABASE_URL` | backend | Supabase project URL; **setting it turns user auth ON** | `""` (auth off, dev user) |
| `APP_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | backend | Publishable/anon key (HS256 fallback only) | `""` |
| `LIGHTRAG_DATA_DIR` | backend | Legacy: a `kg.json` found here is imported into `kg_graphs` once | `./lightrag_data` |
| `MODEL_ORCHESTRATION` | backend | Heavy-reasoning model tier | `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` |
| `MODEL_AGENTS` | backend | Per-post model tier | `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` |
| `MODEL_FAST` | backend | KG/classification/verdict tier | `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` |
| `MODEL_PRO_ORCHESTRATION` | backend | Pro-mode persona curation | `claude-sonnet-4-6` |
| `MODEL_PRO_AGENTS` | backend | Pro-mode debate posts | `claude-sonnet-4-6` |
| `SPAWN_CONCURRENCY` | backend | Parallel Pro persona-generation batches | `8` |
| `SIM_CONCURRENCY_FAST` | backend | Parallel Haiku posts per phase | `48` |

| `SUPABASE_URL` | backend | AI Agent Pulse monitoring Supabase URL (all 3 required to enable) | `""` (disabled) |
| `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` | backend | Supabase service-role key for `execution_logs` inserts | `""` (disabled) |
| `PULSE_PROJECT_ID` | backend | `project_registry` UUID for this project | `""` (disabled) |
| `REDIS_URL` | backend | **Unused** (vestigial) | `redis://localhost:6379` |
| `PORT` | both | Runtime port (Railway-injected) | 8000 / 3000 |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` | frontend | REST base (build-time inlined) | `http://localhost:8000` |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_WS_URL` | frontend | WebSocket base (build-time inlined) | `ws://localhost:8000` |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` | frontend | Supabase Auth project URL (build-time) | `""` (login disabled) |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | frontend | Supabase publishable key (build-time) | `""` |

### 15.4 Writing a migration (asyncpg rule)

The app runs Alembic at startup over the **asyncpg** driver, and asyncpg executes through prepared statements — which accept **exactly one command**. Handing `op.execute()` a multi-statement script therefore dies with *"cannot insert multiple commands into a prepared statement"*, and because startup only logs that failure, the deploy looks healthy while the tables are missing.

**So: a migration whose `UPGRADE_SQL` holds more than one statement must call `run_script(UPGRADE_SQL)`** (`app/core/migrations.py`), which splits the script — quote-, dollar-quote- and comment-aware, so a `;` inside a string or a `do $$ … $$` body does not split — and executes each command on its own. All four shipped migrations use it, and `backend/tests/migrations_test.py` fails the build if a new one does not. This was only discovered on 2026-09-11: migrations `0001`–`0003` were applied to the Supabase project by hand and the project was stamped, so `0004` was the first to actually execute through Alembic.

### 15.5 Persistence & scaling implications
- **Durable storage is Supabase Postgres.** With `DATABASE_URL` set, nothing lives on the Railway disk any more (graphs included), so redeploys no longer wipe sessions. Without it the backend silently falls back to SQLite on the ephemeral disk — check `/health` (`"database": "postgres"`).
- **Single replica only.** Because pub/sub is in-process and the KG/SQLite are disk-local, running >1 instance would break live updates and split data. Horizontal scaling would require an external broker (e.g. real Redis) and shared storage.

---

## 16. Known issues, gotchas & limitations

**Operational (current):**
- **Invalid Anthropic key breaks everything silently.** As of the last verification (2026-06-07), the configured `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` returned **401 invalid x-api-key**. Because all intelligence is Claude, this yields empty graphs and zero posts. A logging fix now surfaces extraction failures in the server logs (previously they were swallowed). **A valid key is the single hardest dependency** — set it in `backend/.env` locally and in Railway's env for prod.

- **Verdict generation depends on output budget.** Fixed 2026-09-09: the Agent Opinions call used to be one un-batched request with `max_tokens=1200` keyed by UUIDs; Pulse logs showed every call ending at exactly 1200 completion tokens (truncated → unparsable → silently `{}`), so the sidebar stayed on "summarising…" forever. Now batched/salvaged/persisted (§12.2). If verdicts ever go missing again, check Pulse `execution_logs` where `execution_id='opinions'` for `completion_tokens` pinned at the cap.

**Architectural / by-design:**
- **Single-replica constraint** (in-process pub/sub). Storage is durable on Supabase once `DATABASE_URL` is set (§15.4).
- **No cascade deletes** — deleting a session orphans its agents/posts/reports; spawning/applying a preset wipes a session's agents but not its posts.
- **Graphs cascade with their session** now (`kg_graphs.session_id` → session, on delete cascade); a session that 404s can still leave the UI title on "Loading…".
- **Agent chat history is process-local and unbounded** (in-memory dict, never persisted, lost on restart).

**Behavioral subtleties worth knowing:**
- **Fast vs Pro tradeoffs:** Fast personas come from a **topic-agnostic** bank — they are diverse and instant but generic, becoming topic-aware only at post time. Pro personas are query-curated on Sonnet (richer) but spawning is slow and, at high counts, **costly**: Pro is *allowed* up to 1000 agents but a 1000-agent Pro debate is many minutes of Sonnet calls and significant API spend (the UI warns about this).
- **The bank is committed & procedural:** `app/data/agent_bank.json` ships in the repo, generated with a fixed seed by `seed_bank.build_bank()` (no API key). Regenerate it by deleting the file (it rebuilds on next Fast spawn) or re-running the builder. Above the bank size, Fast sampling draws with replacement + small dial jitter, so very large Fast populations contain near-duplicates.
- **Dial asymmetry:** the LLM populates all 112 dials, but the behavioral rule engine reads only 4 of 9 groups (sentiment/motivation/friction/trust); the other 5 feed analytics only.
- **Humanity is spawn-time & not carried by presets (yet):** the Humanity/Coverage controls (§7.7) only affect newly spawned agents — existing agents keep `humanity = 0`, and saved presets do not yet persist the per-agent humanity value.
- **No dial validation** — the 0–10 integer range and schema completeness are enforced by prompt only, not code.
- **KG dedup is exact-match only** — "Nvidia" and "Nvidia Corp" are distinct entities; re-ingesting identical text adds few new nodes but isn't perfectly idempotent (LLM nondeterminism).
- **Likes never create posts** despite `PostType.LIKE` existing; they only bump a counter. An agent can like the same post repeatedly.
- **`"stopped"` is not a handled status** — the orchestrator's stop checks only look for `paused`/`complete`/missing.
- **Model defaults ≠ docs** — `.env.example` advertises Opus/Sonnet but code defaults to Haiku 4.5 everywhere.
- **Build-path version drift** — Dockerfile pins Python 3.11; nixpacks (Railway's actual path) uses Python 3.9. Validate ML wheels (faster-whisper) on 3.9.
- **YouTube ingestion is heavy** — needs `ffmpeg`, downloads media, and can take 1–3 minutes; it falls back gracefully (captions → Whisper → placeholder).
- **YouTube extraction tracks yt-dlp/YouTube changes** — YouTube periodically breaks yt-dlp (caption/format access, bot checks). The code forces the `android` player client to keep captions working; if videos stop extracting, bump `yt-dlp` and re-verify the player client. Extraction failures are caught per-step and (at the endpoint) fall back to a placeholder, so a broken extractor shows as an *empty result, not a UI error*.

---

**Population kits & benchmarks (2026-10-08):**
- The June 2023 poll is broken down by the **2020** segments; the website now shows the **2025** segments. Score that poll with `britains_choice_2020`; the 2025 kit has no benchmark yet.
- Neither report publishes a 2019/2024 general-election vote mix for every segment, so kit agents carry their EU-referendum vote and the poll's *GE 2019* banner is not scored. Shattered Britain publishes no region mix per segment (national mix used) and no by-segment figures for 13 of its 21 quiz items. The 2020 report has no segment measures of trust in vaccines, pharma, charities or health apps — those answers rest on the persona's psychology. All listed in each kit's `assumptions`.
- Beliefs are drawn item by item (cohering through one latent score); the reports do not publish how items co-occur within a person.
- More in Common's quiz scoring algorithm (on its *Discover your segment* page) is marked as licensed and is **not** used or copied; agents are assigned to segments by construction, not classified.
- The benchmark data (client poll results) is visible to every signed-in user through `/kits`, the Forms template and the scorecard.

## 17. Glossary

- **Session** — one analysis run: a question + its ingested sources + spawned agents + the debate + reports.
- **Agent / persona** — an AI participant with a role, background, stance, and a 112-dial psychological profile.
- **Dials** — 112 integer (0–10) psychological parameters across 9 groups that define an agent's emotional/behavioral makeup.
- **Stance** — `direct` (domain expert), `indirect` (adjacent perspective), or `neutral` (skeptic/public).
- **Energy** — 0.3–1.0; how often an agent posts and how likely it is to debate.
- **Knowledge graph (KG)** — per-session entities + relations extracted from sources and from the debate, used to ground reasoning.
- **Round** — one batch of concurrent agent contributions in the simulation loop.
- **Preset / lineup** — a saved, reusable snapshot of a population.
- **Scorecard / dial dashboard** — the population-level synthetic market-research metrics (adoption, purchase intent, WTP, virality, retention, churn risk, …).
- **Report** — the structured executive briefing distilled from the KG + transcript.
