## 19. Changelog

- **2026-10-08** — **Run 3 on prod: 5.6 pts total (§11a.8).** Same 200 twins as run 2 (session *Benchmark run 2*, probe 99ae207b), answered fresh with the fence-sitting guidance, scored from stated chances: 5.6 pts off on average (run 2: 6.3 by draws, 5.8 by stated chances), 52% of answers within 5 pts, same top answer 76% of the time. Groups 5.3–9.1 pts v 3.6–6.6 "like the country" — only Progressive Activists beat it. The guidance helped the agree/disagree batteries (Q22 5.8 → 4.4) but not the opt-outs (Q6 *None of the above* 8% v 38%, Q19 *Don't know* 1% v 21%); the largest single miss is foodbanks as the government's responsibility (74% v 42%). Q16 (giving next year) worsened 9.2 → 11.5.
- **2026-10-08** — **A real poll's questionnaire is answered as a fresh respondent (§11a.2, §11a.8).** A probe shows each twin its earlier Lab answers (*WHAT YOU ALREADY DECIDED…*); for a re-run of a benchmark form that meant the twin saw its last run's answers to the same questions, anchoring the new run to the old. `probe.answer_one` now hides prior Forms answers whenever the form matches a benchmark (`benchmark.match`); ordinary forms keep the continuity. Run 3's first attempt was stopped for this and re-run.
- **2026-10-08** — **Scoring from the twins' stated chances, a *real poll* tab on Forms results, and fence-sitting guidance (§11a.3, §11a.8, §12.2, §13.3).** Run 2's segments were noisy, not compressed (simulated segment spread 25 pts v 17.5 real, slope 0.30): one random draw per twin in cells of 15–45 twins. `benchmark.score(…, estimator="auto")` now averages each twin's stated chances where the run recorded them (`_likelihoods` normalises single / grid / multi — stand-alone options first, the rest scaled to the cap), i.e. the same twin asked many times; `"draws"` keeps the old reading. `GET /sessions/{id}/probes/{pid}/benchmark` finds the real poll a run reproduces (`benchmark.match`: the form's question keys) and scores it; the survey results page shows a **Real poll** tab (`lab/pages/RealPollScore`): points off on average in plain words, per question, per group beside "like the country", and the answers furthest off. The likelihood instructions now say the middle and the opt-outs (*Neither*, *About the right amount*, *Don't know*, *None of the above*) are among the commonest real answers, and that a twin's own beliefs should move its chances rather than be hedged away.
- **2026-10-08** — **Run 2 on prod (likelihood answers + staged routing + health facts): total MAE 6.3 pts, from 18.9 (§11a.8).** Same kit, mix and model (session *Benchmark run 2*, probe 03b4ef3b). Every question improved (Q11 49.6 → 9.3, Q17 26.3 → 2.9, Q10 14.2 → 3.2); rank correlation 0.86, 38% of items within 5 pts; *Don't know* 7.1% v 11.1% real (run 1: 2.0%). The perfect-model floor at 200 agents is 2.1 pts total and 4.3–7.5 per segment (cells of 15–45 twins), so per-segment MAE (7.7–10.9) must be judged against "every twin answers like the nation, sampled at the same n" (5.7–9.8): run 2 beats it for Progressive Activists and matches it for Civic Pragmatists, not yet for the rest. What remains: *Neither agree nor disagree* under-used (−14 pts), *None of the above* / *Don't know* under-used (Q6 6% v 38%, Q19 1% v 21%), and segment gradients flattened (median correlation 0.22 v 0.49 in run 1) — spreading every twin's answer also blurred the differences between segments.
- **2026-10-08** — **Three realism changes after run 1 (§7.14, §11a.3, §11a.8).** Drishan: *build all three and re-run on prod.* (1) **Likelihood answers** (`survey.distribution_mode`, per question `likelihood: true`, set on every closed question of the benchmark form): the twin gives the chance it would pick each option (0–100; independent chances for tick-all / up-to-N), `survey.realise` draws the recorded answer from them (seeded per twin and probe, so re-runs repeat), and the chances are kept under `answer._dist`. A twin asked for one pick gives its single most likely option, which made run 1 near-unanimous (97% v 20%). (2) **Routed follow-ups in a second call** (`survey.stages`, `probe.answer_one`): questions with `show_if` are held back and asked only of the twins whose first answers route them there, with a *FOLLOW-UP: you answered …* line — so the follow-up cannot pull its gate question (Q11 *ever not taken medication* was 100% *Yes* when Q12 was visible). (3) **Personal health facts** (`data/kits/population_health_gb/`: `health_facts.json` with every figure's source — Census 2021 long-term condition and unpaid care by age×sex, HSE 2016 prescribed medicines, HSE 2022 obesity, APMS 2023/4 CIS-R 12+, ONS 2025 smoking, Rowlands et al. 2015 health literacy (16–65); `authoring/build_person_facts.py` → `person_facts.json`): each kit agent draws its own at the published rate for its age and sex (`registry._draw_fact`), the writer builds the life around them, and they reach every answer as **FACTS OF YOUR LIFE** (`character.life_facts`). Also: a directive line that people outside medicine have rarely heard of rare conditions (run 1 overclaimed PANDAS 82% v 19%). Survey `max_tokens` 7000. Tests: `kits_test.py` (18).
- **2026-10-08** — **First benchmark run on prod, and two fixes it exposed (§7.14, §11a.8).** 200 agents from `britains_choice_2020` at the poll's segment mix (Sonnet), the June 2023 questionnaire on Sonnet: total MAE **18.9 pts** against a uniform baseline of 16.5 and per-segment MAE 17.7–25.0 against national baselines of 3.6–6.6 — the twins rank options roughly right (top-choice agreement 0.74, rank correlation 0.74; segment-gradient correlation median 0.49 where the poll's segments differ) but answer near-unanimously where people split (Q19 *hunger and poverty* 97% v 20%; Q11 *ever not taken advised medication* 100% *Yes* v 25%), barely use *Don't know* (2% v 11% across 81 items) and overclaim knowledge of rare conditions. Fixes: (1) multi-choice answers repeated the same option to fill an *up to three* cap (Q9 was three copies of one statement for every agent) — the schema now sets `uniqueItems`, the form says *different options*, and aggregation counts a repeat once; (2) the Studio's *Plan vs frame* line judged a kit plan from the segment summaries (reported "poor — 89 pts off") — `frame.planned_distribution` now counts the drawn cards (reports "good — 3.5 pts").
- **2026-10-08** — **Population kits and benchmarks: build the British public from a published segmentation and score the twins against a real poll (§7.14, §11a.8, §12.2, §13.3, §16, §18).** Drishan: *analyse the codebase and More in Common's seven-segments site and make the most realistic agent creation kit; the aim is to compare our agents' answers with the real June 2023 survey.* Before, every population came from the planner's reading of the research, nothing could compare a run with a real poll, and Forms had no "up to three", no routing and pushed every respondent off *Don't know*. Now: two cited kits from More in Common (*Britain's Choice* 2020 — the segments the June 2023 poll uses — and *Shattered Britain* 2025 from the current site, plus its 2026 mental-health findings), every demographic mix and 27–29 beliefs per segment cited to a page and generated by build scripts that refuse to invent a number; each agent drawn as a card (stratified facts + its own beliefs at the published shares) and written by a new `spawn:kit` path that enforces the card; `POST /sessions/{id}/population/kit` and a *start from a published segmentation* card in the Studio. The June 2023 11 London poll is on file as a benchmark parsed from the crosstab workbook (22 questions, 38 banner columns), offered as a Forms template, and scored per question, per segment and per demographic cut with uniform and national baselines and segment-gradient correlations (`GET …/probes/{pid}/benchmark/{bid}`). Forms gained `max_choices`, `exclusive`, `show_if` routing, 16 options and an honest-*Don't know* / unvarnished-answer directive (More in Common found chat models answer its quiz as centre-left); the probe CSV export carries segment, age, gender, region, past vote and weight. Tests: `kits_test.py` (13).
- **2026-10-06** — **Simple view: *Tools* is the fifth step in the header rail (§13.2; `components/lite/LiteShell.tsx`, `app/lite/[id]/page.tsx`).** Drishan: *add tools in navigation panel, after report.* Before, the Forms screen was reached only from the *Lab tools* button on the conversation's floating bar, and while open the rail lit *Conversation*. Now the rail reads Ask · People · Conversation · Report · Tools; *Tools* opens the Forms screen from any step, and the Forms screen lights *Tools*. The floating button stays.
- **2026-10-06** — **Simple view: the Forms screen is quieter (§11a.3, §13.2; `components/lite/LiteForms.tsx`, `globals.css`).** Drishan: *make it a bit more minimal, clean the UI more.* Before a form exists the two bordered cards, their icons and the *Lab tools · Forms* breadcrumb are gone: one field with the write button inside it and at most three open-question chips, then *Already have questions?*, the ready-made templates and *Earlier forms* as plain sections at reading width (earlier forms are hairline rows, not cards). A new quiet chip (`.lite-pill-quiet`: no border, faint fill) replaces the bordered pill for suggestions, templates, the chat's quick replies (now at most three, a *Draft:* prefix hidden), *Add from a file* / *Rewrite for me* and *Change the questions*; question cards lose their shadow; the file panel inside a form is no longer boxed. Verified against a stubbed backend locally.
- **2026-10-06** — **Forms: the survey with the friction taken out — upload it, have it written, or talk it through, all from one brief of the session; built for the simple view, with the pro Lab keeping every manual control (§11a.3, §6.11, §12.2, §13.2, §13.3).** New per-session **Lab brief** (`lab_briefs`, Alembic `0018`): one strong-tier read of the question, roster, debate, past Lab runs, report, evidence brief, Studio plan, frame and graph into a summary, what is known, the tensions, the **open questions worth a form**, what is already measured, the population's own words and the gaps — fingerprinted against what it read, so it goes stale and is rebuilt when the session moves on; prepended to every forms call. Three ways to a questionnaire, all ending in a runnable Survey spec: **import** a file or pasted text (model, with a heuristic fallback), **write it for me** from the brief and a one-line goal, and a **brainstorm chat** — short, warm, one question at a time, drafts on a stated goal, hands back the complete changed form with an undo; the opening line comes from the brief with no model call. The simple view's Lab placeholder becomes `LiteForms` (chat beside the form, two cards to start, plain-words question cards, *Ask the N people*, results through the pro survey page). The pro Survey tool is relabelled **Forms** and its builder takes the whole tab (`FormsStudio`: chat · form · run rail; the shell now supports a `full` builder layout generically) with the manual builder intact in the middle and *Edit this form & run again* after a run. API: `GET/POST /sessions/{id}/lab/brief`, `POST …/forms/import[/file]`, `…/forms/generate`, `…/forms/chat`; `llm.analyze` takes an optional `messages` history. Tests: `forms_test.py` (9); 466 passing overall (the 3 bare-async `kg_ingestion_test` failures predate this). Verified in the browser against a stubbed model in both views.
- **2026-10-06** — **Simple view: the six highlight tiles are gone from the People screen (§13.2; `components/lite/HowMade.tsx`).** Drishan, with the row *40 people · 8 groups · pages read online · publishers searched · figures matched · sure who they are* on screen: *I think we don't need these KPI titles on People page.* The strip under the lead sentence is removed; every figure it carried is still on the page — in the flow diagram's input boxes, in the folded cards' one-liners and inside the input dialogs — so nothing is lost, and the section opens straight on *Where these people came from*. The three stance tiles on the no-plan card stay.
- **2026-10-06** — **Simple view: the reading cards fold to a heading and one line (§13.2; `components/lite/HowMade.tsx`).** Drishan, with *Who they stand for*, *The 8 groups*, *Matched to published figures* and *What had to be assumed* on screen: *Can we hide them in a drop down and a one liner on the heading?* Those four and *How they talk* are now `Fold` cards: closed by default, a chevron and the heading open them, and the one line under the heading is read from the build record (the population and how sure; the two largest groups with shares; what matched / assumed / no figure and the overall level; evidence share, assumptions and gaps; the voice in words and the reason's first sentence). The flow diagram above stays open. The grid aligns cards to the top so a closed card does not stretch to its open neighbour. Verified in the local browser on the Greater Manchester childcare roster.
- **2026-10-06** — **The frame matcher reads a dimension through its attribute, and the statistics triage judges pages against the fact target, not the question (§7.8, §7.9; `population/frame.py`, `population/sources.py`).** Drishan, from the simple view's *Matched to published figures* card showing one row matched and four *No figure found*: *why did other things not matched in our last execution?* The Manchester caddy build's log showed two faults. (1) For *Age and household lifecycle stage* the matcher listed the Census 2021 age bands (summing to 89%, the 65+ bands dropped as "not relevant") and reported the dimension **missing** because the label asked for two tables, so the distribution was thrown away. Now the dimension picker is told one attribute per dimension; the matcher is shown the reason and the picker's named proxy (`dimension_brief`) and told to read a compound label through its attribute, keep every band, and look for the proxy; and `target_from_answer` reads a listed distribution with a source as **found** even when the model called it missing, and notes a short sum. (2) The gather stage's **triage** skipped every Census tenure and household-composition result because "none contain data on attitudes toward food-waste caddy collection", the **refine** step then searched for environmental attitudes, and the page reader marked three MSOA populations as answering a *by ward* target. The triage, fact-extraction and refine prompts now put the fact target first, label the question as context that the page need not be about, require the target's breakdown and geography for `answers_target`, and keep the retry on the same fact. Tests: `frame_test.py` (compound label read as found with a rescale note; the named proxy reaches the call), `population_test.py` (triage keeps the tenure table, prompt order, refine stays on target). The simple view still skips the estimate / upload / proxy ladder for open gaps (it runs with questions skipped) — unchanged here.
- **2026-10-06** — **Simple home: the File button adds the file (§13.2; `components/lite/LiteHome.tsx`).** Drishan's screenshot, a text item already listed: *file not being uploaded in here, or not able to see the confirmation.* Reproduced locally: the hidden file input is cleared right after its change handler, and `addFiles` read the live `FileList` inside the state updater, which React runs later whenever the component already has a pending update — so once any text or link item was listed, a chosen file vanished before it was read (it worked only on an empty list, when React computes the new state eagerly; drag-and-drop was unaffected because `dataTransfer.files` is never cleared). The files are now snapshotted before the state update. Verified in the local browser: text item, then two files via the input, three pills.
- **2026-10-06** — **The Studio reads the whole survey, the whole fact list and the whole statistics page (§7.8, §7.9, §11a.4a; `population/sources.py`, `builder.py`, `frame.py`, `agents/agent_factory.py`, `SourcesPanel.tsx`, `app/lite/[id]/page.tsx`).** Drishan, after the research-loop fix: *check if everything is well with Survey and web searches we do with agent creation in terms of all data coming in and all data being judged correctly.* Found: the fact extractor judged each statistics page on its first 9,000 of up to 20,000 chars and kept 8 facts; detect and plan read the first 4,000 chars of the uploaded survey and the target planner 2,500; the fact list reaching detect, plan and the target planner was cut at 3,000 chars with six facts per page; the simple view trimmed a survey to 8,000 chars silently. Now: the extractor reads the whole page (up to 24 facts, 12 signals, all of them on the row and in the graph chunk); detect, plan and the target planner receive the full survey, the full fact list (24,000-char budget, best-relevance rows first), the full brief and the full graph context; the frame's `material_text` carries every fact (16,000 budget; the estimate call 8,000); persona generation gets 6,000 chars of facts instead of 1,500. `SURVEY_CHAR_LIMIT` is 40,000 on both ends and the simple view's survey card says when a file was trimmed and how many rows reached the people. Tests: whole survey and fact list in every prompt, whole page in the extractor, all facts in the chunk.
- **2026-10-06** — **Simple view: each input box opens a data-led summary dialog (§13.2; `components/lite/InputDialog.tsx`, `components/lite/HowMade.tsx`).** Drishan: *every clickable for what went in should open a dialogue box where a well written summary (highly data driven and data highlighted, even tables if needed and links of the websites if needed) of that input is shown, and an additional button to go see it in pro mode.* The six *What went in* boxes on the flow diagram are now buttons; each opens a dialog summarising that input from the record — a lead paragraph with its figures in bold, figure tiles, and tables with outside links where the data has them (the brief's summary and key facts, the on-topic pages ranked by relevance with the reader's reason and a link, the searches run, the publishers and the statistics they gave with a link to each page, the frame's dimensions and their shares, the items added and their pieces in the graph, the question's dials and what the engine read from the question) — and a *See it in Pro* button to the page where it is analysed in full. The engine's steps and the outputs still link straight to the pro pages. Nothing is written by the model for the dialog: every line is read from the session, the build, the research run, the evidence rows and the knowledge-graph source summary. Verified locally against a seeded research run and statistics pages.
- **2026-10-06** — **The research agent reads every page whole, and only the relevant passages reach the graph (§10a; `evidence/judge_page.py` new, `loop.py`, `judge_web.py`, `brief.py`, `ResearchPanel.tsx`).** Drishan: *I think the research agent is not accessing complete websites* — it was: the fetcher kept up to 20,000 chars, but the relevance judge scored each page on its first 600, the graph took the first three chunks of its opening, and the card showed the same 600. Then: *relevance Judge should look at the whole page, all extracted data. KG should have only relevant snippets (up/down 200 additional char near all relevant data)… It also needs to be quick.* Now a page reader judges each result on its full text (parallel per query), quotes the relevant passages verbatim, and those quotes — located, widened 200 chars each side, merged — are the only part of the page that goes into the graph and the brief. The round judge reads the reader's verdicts and findings instead of page openings and no longer sets relevance. Rows are persisted already scored; graph ingest happens per query, not per round. The expanded card shows what was read and what was kept. Tests: quote location/widening/merging, graph chunks from snippets, and the loop end to end.
- **2026-10-05** — **The simple view's report in the simple palette (§13.2; `globals.css`).** Drishan: *the current report in simple view is just copying the UI of the pro one, it's good but the colour palette is not matching at all.* `ReportChat` stays the pro component; its dark-portal tints (teal-300 figures and headings, yellow-400 confidence, pastel evidence chips) washed out on the light page. A block of `.lite` overrides maps each tint: teal → the simple view's accent, the rest → their 700 weights, bar and dot fills kept saturated. Checked headless on a local session with a full report. Committed without pushing.
- **2026-10-05** — **A report that outlives its HTTP call is waited for, not failed (§11, §12.2, §13.2, §13.4; `api/v1/reports.py`, `lib/reportWait.ts`, `lib/api.ts`, `components/lite/useLiteSession`, the pro session page).** Drishan's screenshot of the simple report: *The request timed out — the model may be busy. Please try again*, over a *Make the report* button. The report proper can take several minutes (it runs the verdict probe over the roster first); the client gave up at two minutes while the server finished and saved the report, and pressing the button again would have written a second one. Now the call carries a ten-minute timeout; a cut-off call (timeout, dropped connection, 502/503/504) switches to polling the history for a report not seen before and shows it when it lands, in both the simple and the pro view; a per-session marker lets a page reopened mid-write resume waiting; and the server writes one report at a time per session — a second generate joins the one in flight (`run_report`, shielded, own database session). History rows carry `created_at`. Tests: `report_inflight_test.py`. Committed without pushing while a session is in progress.
- **2026-10-05** — **A simple-view build no longer looks stopped — or gets stopped — when the reader moves about (§12.2, §13.2; `builder.start_build`, `components/lite/useLiteSession`, `app/lite/[id]/page.tsx`).** Drishan, on *Getting everyone ready*: *you state this but when I go to other pages like Ask or other executions/sessions, it stops.* The build itself runs as a server task and was never tied to the page; what stopped it was the page. Reopening a session showed the people form until the latest-build lookup returned (and for good if that lookup failed — it was never retried), so the build looked dead and a second *Run* superseded it (prod on 2026-10-02 shows sessions with two and three builds a few minutes apart, each stopping the last). - **2026-09-30** — **Why it moved, in the twins' own words — on every lever run and every amended journey (§11a.3, §12.2; follow-up to L7-04 / the amendments refresh).** Drishan, reading a lever run (+27 points) next to an amended journey that *fell* from 27% to 18% with six twins moving backward: the figures said how much, nothing said why, and the report's *Contradictions and risks* could only guess at intrusive calls or a mis-specified rule. Now `levers.twin_moves` classes every twin answered in both arms — at the candidate's step for a lever run (through / back / still stuck / already through), over the whole journey for an amended run (gets further / stops earlier) — carrying its reasoning, barrier and removal from each arm; `levers.why_moved` codes the movers into 1–4 reasons per group in one fast-tier call (`lever_why`), each reason with its count, the twins it covers and one sentence copied **verbatim** from a twin (checked against the twin's words; a paraphrase is replaced by the twin's own first sentence; a twin the coder skips becomes its own line), and lists those still stuck by the barrier theme they still name. Stored as `results.lever.why` on the experiment and `aggregates.amended.why` on the amended run, appended as a sentence to the shift's / growth's `sentence` so the lever record and the report can state the reasons, and shown under each figure on the Journey page as *Why it moved · in their words* (moved through / fell back / still stuck, with the quote and who said it). Runs counted before this existed are backfilled in the background the next time they are listed (`ensure_why`, `ensure_growth_why`; one pass per run). The coder failing leaves the uncoded form (one twin per line), never nothing. Tests: `levers_test.py` (classing at a step and over the journey, uncoded reasons, verbatim check, coder failure, the record).
- **2026-09-30** — **Journey: refresh with the simulation amendments, so the growth the signed rules earn shows on the funnel (§11a.3, §12.2, §13.3).** Drishan (from the Journey page): *also add an option to refresh with simulation amendments, so that new journey growth shows up.* Before, a signed rule could only be simulated one at a time at one candidate step, and the shift lived in the experiment panel; the funnel stayed as it was. Built: **(1)** the tabs read as numbered steps — *1 Sources · 2 Twins · 3 Debate · 4 Lab · 5 Report*, *What they know* beside them — with a tick on each step done and a *Next: …* button in the header; **(2)** one vocabulary on every reader-facing surface — twins, not agents; *affected directly / indirectly / observer* for the three stances with hovers; *everyday voice* for the humanity dial; the landing page, the Ingest header and the roster copy rewritten without "spawn", "knowledge graph" or "multi-agent"; **(3)** Ingest leads with the result (*22 useful pages found of 60 read · 25 now part of what the twins know · 5 of 6 sub-questions covered*) and folds the queries, judge notes and budget under *How it searched*; the stance tile is blank rather than 0/0/0 when the brief has no split; *Recommended tools* became *What to run next, from the evidence*, each mapped to where it lives in this app (Debate tab, Verdict poll, Purchase intent, A/B test, Survey — with "closest fit" said where a conjoint or MaxDiff is not built) and an *Open the Lab* button; *worst cell N pts off* and *effective n* are hovers on chips that read *poor match* and *worth ≈21 of 40 twins*; **(6)** every number on a twin card has a hover (energy, everyday voice, stance) and the roster groups by population segment when the Studio built it, by stance otherwise; model · seed · answer format sit behind *run details*; the Journey page's *Rule book 0/0* reads *empty* or *2 signed of 3*, *not scored* explains itself on hover, the brief id left the footer, and the builder warns when a new run's last step is worded differently from the earlier runs (a new journey the report would show beside the old); **(9)** a reload keeps the last report proper and the Ask-Report questions (from `report/history`); **(10)** the whole session card on the home page opens the session — only the delete controls swallow the click. - **2026-09-29** — **A twin named once, not three times, in a report sentence (§11 citations, §13.3).** Drishan: *why are the named in the report appearing so many times in a sentence?* Cause: the model typed each dissenter's name *and* its handle (it was shown both on the dissent list), `repair_names` turned the typed name into a second identical token, and every token — including the statement pointer in "his [[A7#P12]] claim" — rendered as the full underlined name. Fix on four fronts: `citations.collapse_repeats` (run after `repair_names`, tested) folds adjacent duplicate tokens of one twin; the report renderer shows a twin's first citation in a block as the name and later ones as a `¶` (statement) or initials marker, and folds adjacent duplicates itself so reports stored before the fix read the same; the client document names a twin once per paragraph and drops a later statement pointer; and the prompt no longer hands the model the name beside the handle, asks for one dissent entry per twin opened with the handle once, and says to cite a twin once per sentence with the statement citation after the claim.
- **2026-09-29** — **UI restyle: a calmer, more current look, the Population Studio first (§13.1, §13.2, §13.3).** Drishan: *make the UI a bit more new age, right now it's very cluttery, specially in Studio.* A small design system in `globals.css` (surfaces, buttons, fields, chips, segmented control, switch, type roles, restyled sliders and selects — see §13.1) replaces the per-element Tailwind soup; `.glass` is kept as an alias of the new card so every other screen quietens down without a rewrite. The **session page** merges its header and tab strip into one bar (title and status on the left, the six tabs as a segmented control in the middle, run controls on the right). The **Studio** loses its second header, its uppercase-tracked card headings and most of its explanatory paragraphs: one rail carries the title, status, a six-stage progress rail and the actions; the **Inputs** column is plain sections (the question; what is on file as three status rows; audience profile and upload; statistics with a switch for automatic gathering, publisher chips, fact cards and the folded lookup); the middle column is centred at a reading width and ordered by what the analyst reads next (Detected → Questions → Plan → Population built → Sampling frame → Activity); the **Activity** panel (the build log) opens while a stage runs and folds to its last line once a plan exists; the **plan** header shows the composition as a stacked share bar in one hue per segment, and each segment card carries the same hue as a left rule, its share as the one large number, two or three chips (stance, mood, register, heat), a rationale as a quoted paragraph, and ghost buttons for accept / edit / reject; the **Dials** column is sections separated by hairlines (Population with the count as a large figure and Fast/Pro as a segmented control; Who they are; How they feel; Stance mix; Expert ↔ Reactive) with switches instead of checkboxes and a filled-track slider for every dial, and 'Skip clarifying questions' as a switch at the bottom; the questions card, sampling-frame card and empty state follow the same vocabulary. No behaviour, prop or endpoint changed; every control is the same control in the same place.
- **2026-09-29** — **Commitment record: the modelled baseline frozen when an outcome is chosen (§11a.3, §12.2, §13; brief L7-08).** Drishan asked what L7-07 was; recommendation: do L7-08 first (smaller, no model calls, and the portfolio then reads frozen rows). Explained plainly (a copy not a pointer; signed by name; never edited; observed results entered by hand with a source; forecast vs observed counted, not judged); Drishan: *go for it*. New `models.Commitment` + Alembic `0017_commitments`, `services/measurement/commitments.py` (`build_baseline`, `clean_target`, `clean_observed`, `compare`, `sentence`, `payload`, `freeze`, `observe`, `close`, `list_for_session`, `get`), five endpoints under `/sessions/{id}/commitments`, `records.record_from_commitment` + listing + records-block line + a *COMMITTED OUTCOME* clause in `FIGURE_RULES`, `structure.outcome.commitments`, the *Committed outcomes* block in the report page and the client document, `commitments/<id>.json` in the export, and on the Journey page the *committed* badge, the commit form and `CommitmentCard`. Verified end to end against a copy of the local database (freeze, refusal when unsigned, observe, supersede, close, records, export). Not built: freezing the corpus (L1-06); the portfolio table (L7-07). Tests: `tests/commitments_test.py`.
- **2026-09-29** — **Message testing against the panel (§11a.3, §12.2; brief L7-06).** Drishan asked how it differed from Survey and A/B; answer: Survey cannot compare framings, A/B already pairs them and breaks the lift out by segment, so L7-06 is a thin layer — *who answers* (the twins at risk at one Journey step, started from the candidate), backfire carried into the record, the records block and `FIGURE_RULES` rather than living only in a page sentence, a weighted shift where the frame has weights, and the calibration label. Drishan: *okay do it*. New `services/measurement/messaging.py` (`clean_messages`, `message_block`, `start`, `run`, `rank`, `sentence`, `attach_ranking`), `stats.weighted_paired_lift`, a message block in `probe.build_user_message`, message arms skipped by `journey._targeting_kinds`, `records.record_from_messaging` + records-block line + a *WHICH MESSAGE WORKS* clause, `POST /sessions/{id}/messaging/run` + `GET /sessions/{id}/messaging`, *Test messages* + `MessagingRun` on every Journey candidate. Every surface says *a modelled reaction to a framing, not a forecast of uptake*; a message run is never refused for want of a rule. Not built: tying a message run to a signed rule so it can drop the label; message testing outside the Journey (the A/B tool covers it). Tests: `tests/messaging_test.py`.
- **2026-09-29** — **Behaviour targeting: which behaviour to change, as a counted ranking (§11a.3, §12.2; brief L7-05).** Drishan: *yes, let's do L7-05 now* (after a plain explanation: every behaviour nudged by the same small amount for the stuck twins, ranked by movement per point, backfire shown as prominently as the winner, labelled as sensitivity not forecast). New `services/measurement/targeting.py` (`behaviours_for`, `valid_keys`, `nudge_spec`, `at_risk_ids`, `start`, `run`, `rank`, `sentence`, `attach_ranking`); `probe.answer_one` applies `spec.nudge` in-call; `journey.postprocess` skips coding/tracing/movability for nudge arms and keeps only headcounts for the targeting baseline; `records.record_from_targeting` + records-block line + `FIGURE_RULES` clause; three endpoints. Frontend: *Rank behaviours* on every Journey candidate (behaviour chips from the question's dials, add a fixed dial, nudge size), the ranked result with direction, per-point movement, interval, movement, people per point, by-band rows and a red *Backfire* block. Tests: `targeting_test.py` (5).
- **2026-09-29** — **Rules: the system drafts, the human signs; evidence optional and its absence visible (§11a.3, §12.2; brief L4-02 follow-up to L7-04).** Drishan, after testing the rule book by hand: writing a rule (translating a lever into dial points, then finding evidence for a change nobody has tried) was the confusing step; most levers are hypothetical. Agreed: keep the gate (nothing runs without a named signature), remove the typing, and label rather than block where there is no evidence. Built: `levers.draft_rule` / `evidence_pool` / `clean_draft` + `POST /sessions/{id}/rules/draft` (evidence only by handle from the material shown — never invented); `validate_rule` no longer requires evidence; `basis_class` (evidence_anchored | assumption) on every rule payload, lever spec and shift (`shift(assumed=…)` → the sentence opens *Assumed effect, not a forecast*); `record_from_lever` labels the record *Assumed effect:* with an ASSUMPTION caveat; the records block and a new `FIGURE_RULES` clause bind the report model to what-if wording. Frontend: *Draft the rule* on every Journey candidate, *Draft it for me* in the rule book, assumption / evidence-anchored badges, the drafted banner, an orange *Assumed effect* header on lever results. Also new: `test-case-basic/` — a plain council food-waste-caddy scenario with test data and a step-by-step script for the whole tool. Tests: `levers_test.py` (9).
- **2026-09-29** — **Lever simulation, gated on a calibration rule book (§11a.3, §12.2; brief L7-04 on the minimum of L4-02).** Drishan: build both parts. New `calibration_mappings` (Alembic `0016`) and `services/measurement/levers.py`: a rule names a lever, who it applies to, which dials it moves and by how many bounded points, the evidence, the basis, the author and its review; a lever run is a within-subjects experiment on the Journey instrument — the same twins answer with the rule's dial shifts applied and the change described — and the shift is counted (conversion then → now with a paired interval, movement, end of journey, by deprivation, people moved). No reviewed rule → 409 with the missing rule named, no fallback. Lever records in the report with the rule and its reviewer; the rule book and *Simulate this lever* on the Journey page; rules in `run.json`. Tests: `levers_test.py` (7).
- **2026-09-29** — **Movability: how much of each gap a partner could move (§11a.3, §11; brief L7-03).** Drishan: build it (after a plain-terms explanation: two gaps of the same size are not worth the same; sort each barrier into a-partner-could-fix-it / only-the-system / nobody-soon; count; reorder by what can be moved). New `services/measurement/movability.py`: one call classes every coded barrier by who could reach the removal the twins asked for (partner / system / structural; none when nothing was named; unscored when the call fails — never guessed), the score is counted from the stuck twins' barriers, movable people follow the headcount, and candidates are ranked by the movable gap first. Bar + levers on the Journey page, reach on every barrier, movability in the record, the records block (the model may only quote it), the report block, the client document and the CSV. Tests: `movability_test.py` (5).
- **2026-09-29** — **Headcounts: the gap in individuals, not percentages (§11a.3, §11, §12.2; brief L7-02).** Drishan: build it with the in-scope slice as the denominator and a hand-entered fallback. New `services/measurement/headcount.py`: the Journey tool's shares are multiplied through the Studio's in-scope sizing figure (SAM, or SAM share × TAM, with source and year) or a figure the analyst types with a source (client supplied); a known headcount for a step is held fixed and the steps after it scale from it; frame weights are used where they exist; with no denominator and no anchor the result says so and gives no number of people. People beside every step and every candidate on the Journey page, in the record, the records block (the model may state a headcount only where the record gives one, with its denominator), the report block, the client document, the CSV and the statement. Tests: `headcount_test.py` (8).
- **2026-09-29** — **Journey: candidate outcomes, one per step where the population drops off (§11a.3, §11, §12.2, §13.3; brief L7-01).** Drishan: build it, keep it generic. Before, the analyst had to nominate one outcome up front and run Barriers on it; now the system proposes the journey to the outcome in the question (`journey.suggest_stages`, `POST /sessions/{id}/journey/suggest`, editable in the tool's own builder), every twin places itself on it (furthest step reached, whether the next step will happen, what stops it, what would move it; professionals answer for the people they see), and the tool counts a funnel and one candidate outcome per drop-off — share of those at a step who reach the next — ranked by who is stuck then by the gap, each with its interval, coded and traced barriers, equity block, computed confidence and a stable id for L7-02 / L7-03 / L7-07 / L7-08. Its own results page with the cut picker; *Where the population drops off · candidate outcomes* in the report, the client document and the export; `FIGURE_RULES` binds the model to the list. `rank_barriers` takes a `blocked_by` test and `trace_evidence` a `collect` function; `apply_themes` takes `max_themes`. Tests: `journey_test.py` (10).
- **2026-09-29** — **Export, client report and the delta view, with the synthetic-population statement on every export (§11, §12.2, §13.2; brief L6-06 / L6-07).** *Export data* zips the run record, every record as JSON and two flat CSVs, the report with its structure and as a client document, the source ledger and the roster; the statement (what the twins are, what they were calibrated against, what the output must not be used for) is generated server-side from the run and stamped into every file. *Client report* is a clean print-ready page with every citation resolved. *Compare runs* shows what changed between two reports proper — headline and whether the change is real, positions, equity gap, dissent, barriers, evidence base, confidence. New `export.py`, `delta.py`, `records.records_by_ids`, three endpoints, `/session/{id}/report/client`. Tests: `export_test.py` (4).
- **2026-09-29** — **Answers traceable to the documents the twin drew on (§9.6, §11, §11a; L6-05 proper).** Drishan: L6-05 is about tracing the agent and the documents it used to answer, not what it could have seen. Now the knowledge block is numbered, every probe answer cites the items the twin drew on (`sources_used` → `used_units` / `served_units`), records roll the citations up as `sources`, the Barriers evidence is what its twins cited (could-see only as a labelled fallback), and the Verdict / Barriers pages and the record card show *drew on* per twin. Tests: `sources_test.py` (3).
- **2026-09-29** — **Barriers: ranked barrier attribution per gap as a Lab tool (§11a.3, §11; brief L6-05).** Drishan: no more colour codings — a new page or a tool. Built as an instrument: every twin names what stands in the way of an outcome and what would remove it; the answers are coded into shared barriers, ranked by who cited them then by weight, and each barrier is traced to the twins who raised it and to the scoped evidence those twins could see that speaks to it. Its own results page with the Verdict's cut picker; a plain numbered *What's in the way* list in the report from the barriers record, which the model may only quote in order. Tests: `barriers_test.py` (5).
- **2026-09-28** — **The Verdict gets its own Lab page (§11a.3).** Drishan: the verdict results had no filtering. `VerdictPage` — a cut picker (deprivation first, then stance, age, segment, demographics, dynamic dials) that recomputes the headline, the position mix and the twins' own words for that cut; splits in rank order; position chips. Answers why a cut answered the way it did, from the twins who did.
- **2026-09-28** — **Scoping wired end to end (§9.6, §12.2, §13.3; brief L1-04).** Drishan: the Scoping view sat on its own in the Graph tab, so agents were written and debates run without it unless someone pressed Tag. Now tagging runs on its own when ingestion or research finishes and is awaited before a Studio build writes personas, before a debate starts and before a probe runs (retagging when the graph has grown); every persona is written from the scoped knowledge a twin of its segment could reach; every twin stores what it was written from and can see (`spawned_agents.knowledge`, Alembic `0015`) and shows it as a *sees N/M* chip on the Agents tab that opens the Scoping view on that twin; a status pill beside *Start* says where scoping stands; probes and outcome records carry whether they ran scoped. New `services/scoping/auto.py`, `GET /scoping/coverage`, `POST /scoping/ensure`, websocket `scoping_*` events. Tests: `scoping_auto_test.py` (6).
- **2026-09-28** — **Equity stratification by default (§11, §7.9; brief L6-04).** Every sampling frame now carries a deprivation dimension inside the matched top three (IMD quintiles; deciles at 200+ twins), found from the statistics search or filled with the labelled national reference so it is never left unmatched; every twin is written into a cell it shows in its life; `deprivation` is a base split on every Lab result; every outcome record carries an `equity` block (most vs least deprived cell, gap, whether real, thin cells) and one equity clause in the report prompt; the report shows an Equity strip, the grid cards an equity line, the record card the deprivation bars first; a population built without a frame says so as a caveat. New `services/population/equity.py`, `equity_test.py` (7).
- **2026-09-28** — **Every figure carries its provenance class and a citation (§11, §12.2; brief L6-03).** Numbers read from the material now cite the typed statistic (`[[F1]]` → `[[fact:<evidence id>#<k>]]`) or the document they were read in (`[[E1]]` → `[[evidence:<id>]]`), each classed by the scoping tagger's host rules (official statistic, peer-reviewed, grey literature, social signal, client data, model inference — the brief's L1-05 vocabulary) and rendered as a coloured chip with a source card (value, group, geography, year, verbatim quote, link). A number the model types with no citation in its sentence is wrapped as `[[unsourced:…]]` and shown struck through as the model's own — the check left open in L6-01. Records now say *model-inferred*. New `services/simulation/figures.py`, `GET /sessions/{id}/figures`, `structure.figures`, a figure key above every report. Tests: `figures_test.py` (5).
- **2026-09-28** — **Report structure wired to the records (§11, §12.2; brief L6-02).** The five-section report keeps its shape but every part that can be computed now is: the direct answer's confidence band comes from the headline record's score (the model is told not to state one; a label it writes anyway is stripped and kept as `claimed_band`), SOURCE MATERIALS opens with the session's evidence by class and the frame, DISCUSSION opens with the verdict probe's for/against/mixed bar and the **named dissent** (every twin off the majority position, most confident first, as clickable citations with their verdict line — the model must name its dissent from that list), and the document ends with **What would change this**, the deduplicated union of the cited records' caveats. New `services/simulation/structure.py`; `REPORT_PROMPT` moved from the frontend page to the backend; `report_queries.structure` (Alembic `0014`, local SQLite `_ensure_columns`); `report/generate` returns and `report/history` serves `structure` (the history endpoint now has a real response model — it could not serialise its ORM rows before). Tests: `structure_test.py` (7), `records_test.py` extended.
- **2026-09-28** — **Outcome records: every figure in a report is computed, cited, never typed (§11, §12.2, §13.3; brief L6-01).** Drishan's steer: keep it generic (no pharma schema) and make it work when no Lab tool was run. New `services/simulation/records.py` shapes a complete probe or experiment into an outcome record (estimate + interval, splits, refusals, unanimity, weighted figure, provenance, a deterministic confidence with drivers, caveats; optional pharma `tags`). `POST /sessions/{id}/report/generate` first guarantees a **headline record** — the population's answer to the session question from a new hidden `verdict` instrument (position / confidence / one-line verdict, reused while the roster is unchanged; the one-liners feed the Opinions sidebar) — then numbers every record `R1…Rn` for the report model, forbids typed population figures (`FIGURE_RULES`), and rewrites `[[R1]]` into `[[record:<id>]]`. The frontend drops the KEY METRICS section: an **Outcome records** grid sits under the direct answer, citations render as figure chips, and both open a record card. `GET /sessions/{id}/records`. Tests: `records_test.py` (9, including an HTTP round trip that runs the verdict once and reuses it).
- **2026-09-24** — **Archetype matching is by job words only (§7.11).** Found in the first end-to-end test on prod: "GP partner in a coastal practice" was cast onto every *coastal* segment and "Community pharmacist" onto *community* voices and equity advocates. Place and setting words are now dropped from the role, only the job head counts, and a description-only hit never casts. Test: `archetypes_test.py::test_place_words_in_the_role_do_not_cast_patient_segments`.
- **2026-09-24** — **Dissent, refusal and consensus drift (§7.13, §11a.2, §12.2, §13.3; brief L3-06).** Twins can now say "I don't know" without becoming shruggers: a *what you actually know* block in every persona prompt with a bounded licence to refuse outside their own life and job; a `can_answer` / `cannot_answer_why` pair on every Lab instrument, reported as its own share with reasons and names and kept out of every other denominator (experiments too); a mechanical **unanimity check** that flags a lopsided headline whose structural splits show no spread, shown above the numbers; and **independent voices** — a fixed, hash-chosen 30% of twins who never read the thread — plus a dissent rule for everyone who has. New `measurement/unanimity.py`, setting `independent_voice_share`; tests `dissent_test.py` (19).
- **2026-09-24** — **Dynamic dials (§7.2a, §6.1, §12.2, §13.3; brief L3-04).** The brief's typed HCP / patient fields, generalised on Drishan's direction: instead of a fixed clinical schema, the model **chooses 6–12 dials for the session's question** — what blocks these people, what pushes them, what they must fit it around — and every twin carries a 0–10 value on each, tuned exactly like the sentiment dials. New `services/agents/dynamic_dials.py`; `analysis_sessions.dynamic_dials` (Alembic `0012_dynamic_dials`); chosen and logged by the Studio at plan time and carried on `plan.dynamic_dials`; stated in the persona prompt (plan, quick-spawn and archetype-cast paths) and stored in `dials["dynamic"]`; clamped to an archetype's priors where it has them; injected into the persona's system prompt at debate, chat and probe time; shown as a **Dynamic** group directly under Sentiment in the Agent Builder (hand-settable, filled by *Build sentiment profile*), on every roster card and in the population dial dashboard. Tests: `dynamic_dials_test.py` (16). **In the Lab** each dial is also a low/mid/high split and a *Who answers* filter: `segments_for` namespaces them (`dyn:<dial>`), every instrument and the A/B heat-map read their split list from `split_keys(rows)`, and a split heading shows the dial's authored label. Also: the Studio flips a plan to *awaiting review* only after its whole log is written, so the review never opens on a half-written log.
- **2026-09-24** — **The report cites twins, not names (§11, §12.2; brief L3-03).** The surname drift in the Blackpool report was the report call paraphrasing names, not agents re-sampling. Fixed at the source: new `services/simulation/citations.py` hands the model short handles for the roster and the transcript and forbids typing names; the answer comes back as `[[twin:<id>]]` / `[[twin:<id>|post:<id>]]` citations, any typed name (including a drifted surname) is repaired into one, and the frontend renders each citation as the name read from the agent row. Clicking it opens a trace card — who the twin is, **where they came from** (archetype / hand-authored / segment), and the verbatim line being cited, with *Talk to this twin*. Tests: `citations_test.py` (15).
- **2026-09-24** — **Archetypes: the Studio casts segments from authored twins (§7.11, §6.5a, §12.2, §13.3; brief L3-02).** Build your own agent gets a *use as an archetype* tick (on by default; lineups unchanged) that also stores the agent as an `Archetype` (`0011_archetypes`, `/archetypes`). At plan time each segment is matched to an archetype by job (synonym-aware), shown as a *cast from …* chip with a picker on the segment card (`archetype_id` edit; manual choices survive re-plans) and logged. At build time a cast segment's personas get their facts **drawn before the model runs** (age, gender, place, income, education, and a cell per frame dimension from the published shares), a per-place slice of the graph as local context, and a texture-only prompt; the mould's character is copied verbatim, dials clamp to ±2 of its priors, Expert↔Reactive to ±10, and `character.archetype` records the source (roster chip *cast from …*). Cast personas skip the register band, the voice pass and the role-at-same-age clone rule. New service `agents/archetypes.py`; tests `archetypes_test.py` (11).
- **2026-09-24** — **Build your own agent (§7.10, §4, §6.2, §6.5, §12.2, §13.3).** Brief L3 opened with L3-01 explained; Drishan's direction: instead of a template library first, let the analyst author a twin by hand. New page `/session/{id}/agents/builder` from a **Build your own agent** button on the Agents tab (Studio empty state and roster): the person in words — identity, description, relationship to the topic, personality, debate style, stance, Expert ↔ Reactive, place, and five **Character** fields (how they decide / behave / talk, information diet, failure modes) — plus all 112 dials, each either set by hand or left to the system; **Build sentiment profile** reads the text and tunes the system's dials relative to the session query, keeping the fixed ones. An agent is never saved loose: it joins a new or existing **lineup** (`POST /presets/custom`, `POST /presets/{id}/agents`; `GET /presets/{id}`), and saving builds the profile first when any dial is still the system's. New `spawned_agents.character` (Alembic `0010_character`), carried through presets → `apply-preset` and injected into every persona prompt by `agent_runner._character_block`. New service `agents/agent_builder.py`; tests `agent_builder_test.py` (9).
- **2026-09-17** — **Expert ↔ Reactive shapes voice only (§7.8).** Per Drishan: the dial must never change who is in the population — composition always aims at the most realistic mix for the demographic from the statistics. Off-centre it now acts on the personas' sentiment dials, credibility, humanity and descriptions through a deterministic `apply_voice` pass plus a prompt directive; the planner instruction no longer pulls the expert share. Test: `population_test.py::test_voice_dial_shapes_voice_not_composition`.
- **2026-09-17** — **Expert ↔ Reactive dial in the Studio (§7.8).** Replaces the humanity note: 0 = experts, 50 = exactly the real mix for this demographic, 100 = ordinary people reacting from their own experience; proportional pull either side; *let the system decide* asks the planner to choose from the question and record why (`plan.voice`). The persona writer gets a matching voice directive at build. Test: `population_test.py::test_voice_dial_instruction_and_summary`.
- **2026-09-17** — **Population map laid out as one panel per facet (§7.8).** The single ring overlapped badly with dozens of place and occupation cells; now each facet is its own panel of size-scaled bubbles, biggest first, with the tail folded into an expandable "others" bubble.
- **2026-09-16** — **Rebuild in Studio is available after a run (§4, §13.3).** The roster's completed-simulation card gained the button (the Studio reopens with the existing plan); while a simulation runs it is shown disabled with the reason.
- **2026-09-16** — **The sampling frame graph became the population map (§7.8).** Per Drishan: show the final cells and the personas in them, with cell types that matter to a reader, dynamic per question, ranked, 5–15 shown. New `services/population/facets.py`: `pick_facets` chooses and ranks the facets at plan time (attribute facets from what personas carry; persona facets the writer fills from a short label list, stored in `demographics.facets`), saved as `plan.facets`; the persona prompt lists them. The graph draws the facets as arcs of cells sized by persona count with a top-N control, click-to-focus and per-facet cell summaries; target rings are gone from it (the frame card keeps the matching). Tests: `facets_test.py` (4).
- **2026-09-16** — **Frame cells are dynamic (§7.9).** Segments carry `frame_values` and personas carry `demographics.frame` — one category per frame dimension, assigned by the planner and the persona writer against the frame's categories — and the frame's readers (achieved shares, weights, planned shares, the sampling frame graph) take those first, falling back to demographic attributes. The graph's cell types are now the frame's dimensions; its legend counts cells and personas per type and focuses on a type by click. Tests: `frame_test.py` (16), `population_test.py` (+2).
- **2026-09-16** — **The frame now drives the statistics search, with a TAM / SAM / SOM sizing trio (§7.9).** Dimensions are picked straight after detect; each becomes the first fact target of the gather stage (publisher-phrased, routed, ahead of the planner's gap targets; fallback phrasing per attribute), plus three sizing targets. After gather the sizing figures are read from the material and shown on the frame card. Fixed: the frame read `excerpt` off evidence rows, which store it as `text`. Tests: `frame_test.py` (13).
- **2026-09-16** — **The sampling frame: the Studio picks the dimensions that matter, finds the real distributions, matches to them, and asks the analyst when it can't (§7.9, §6.9, §12.2).** Brief L2-01 to L2-05 in one mechanism, as agreed in the L2 discussion. `services/population/frame.py`: ranked dimensions after detect (`population_frame_dims`), targets from the statistics on file after gather (`population_frame_target`: found / proxy / missing, never estimated), and the ladder for every gap — labelled model estimate (refuses attitudinal dimensions), uploaded CSV, proxy, skip — surfaced in the Studio's new *Sampling frame* card and paused at *clarifying*. After the plan: target vs planned per cell with a level; after the build: raking weights on every agent (`spawned_agents.weight`), achieved shares, thin cells, effective sample size. Weights reach the Lab as a weighted primary metric beside the unweighted one; the final report's prompt carries the frame summary. The sampling frame graph's rings now come from the targets. Alembic `0009_frame`. Tests: `frame_test.py` (10).
- **2026-09-16** — **Sampling frame graph in the Population Studio (§7.8).** A *Sampling frame* button opens a live graph of the cells the population is drawn from — segments and the places, age bands, genders, income bands, education levels, occupations, stances and moods they fix — where a node's dashed ring is the plan's intended count and its filled disc is the personas written so far, growing in real time through a build. Only personas move a node. Also fixed the Studio's stacked layout below the `xl` breakpoint (the three panes shared the container height and overlapped; now they size to content and the page scrolls) — the same component serves the Agents tab and the `/population` route.
- **2026-09-16** — **The Population Studio is the Agents screen; the old quick-spawn form is gone (§4, §7.7, §7.8, §13.3).** The Studio page body moved into `components/population/PopulationStudio.tsx` (props `embedded`, `onViewAgents`, `presets`/`onApplyPreset`/`onDeletePreset`); the `/population` route is a thin wrapper for deep links. `AgentDirectory` renders the Studio when the session has no agents (and keeps it open through a build until *View agents*), the live spawn dashboard while a lineup loads, and the roster afterwards with the run settings and one **Rebuild in Studio** button. Removed from the UI: audience-profile text, survey upload, stance sliders, Humanity/Coverage sliders, agent-count slider, the Fast/Pro spawn button, and the *Studio / Re-spawn* pair; saved lineups now load from the Studio's empty state. `POST /spawn-agents` and its options remain in the API. The Thread's post estimate now uses the real roster size.
- **2026-09-16** — **Scoped retrieval: each twin is given only what its profile can reach, with a route per item (§9.6, §6.10, §12.2, §13.3).** Brief L1-04, built as the generic architecture agreed the same day — nothing in it is specific to location. `services/scoping/`: a **dimension registry** (geography hierarchical along the ontology's `within` edges, role / channel / condition / stage / segment by overlap, register as an ordinal cap, time, provenance, project and licence as hard dimensions, experiment arm); a **tagger** that turns every graph chunk into a `knowledge_unit` with a provenance class, trust tier and facets drawn only from the ontology's vocabulary (one call per 12 chunks; the ontology is built first if missing); **exposure profiles** derived from the agent's region, role, segment and humanity band with a recorded basis per value, overridable via `spawned_agents.exposure`; **stacked, versioned policies** (`deny | require | allow | boost | route`, `when: own | unscoped | any | [values]`); and a pure **engine** that returns what a twin sees with a score and a route ("via Local press", "from a research paper you read") and why each hidden unit failed. Wired into the debate, probes/experiments and chat per twin (one retrieval per distinct profile and query; every serve logged to `retrievals`); untagged sessions and any load error fall back to the old shared block. Tables `knowledge_units`, `scope_policies`, `retrievals` + `spawned_agents.exposure` (Alembic `0008_scoping`). API under `/sessions/{id}/scoping`. UI: the Graph tab's third view, **Scoping** — tag, facet coverage, unit cards, and per twin the profile with its bases, Sees / Hidden / Prompt block / Policy editor. Verified in the browser against a stubbed model. Tests: `scoping_test.py` (9).
- **2026-09-16** — **Ontology: a typed layer over the session knowledge graph, with a view dropdown on the Graph tab (§9.5, §6.6, §12.2, §13.3).** First item built from Matthew Hunt's *11 Populations Build Brief* (L1-03). New `services/knowledge_graph/ontology.py`: a fixed schema of nine classes (geography with `nation → region → icb → local_authority → lsoa` levels, HCP role & specialty, condition, journey stage, intervention, channel, attitude segment, organisation, other) and twelve predicates with domain/range; `build` classifies every entity (80 per structured call) and types every relation (100 per call) on `model_fast`, then `assemble` adds the geography containment the sources implied as inferred nodes/edges. Stored in the new `kg_graphs.ontology` column (Alembic `0007_ontology`, SQLite `_ensure_columns`), flagged stale when the graph grows. API: `GET /sessions/{id}/kg/ontology`, `POST …/kg/ontology/build`. UI: the Graph tab gains a *Knowledge graph / Ontology* dropdown; the Ontology view shows the schema as a graph until built, then a swim-lane typed graph (lanes per class, dashed inferred edges, hollow inferred nodes, legend toggles, node detail with predicates, verbs and mentions). The debate and report do not read it yet — it is the analyst's view, and the base for cohort-scoped retrieval (L1-04). Verified in the browser against a stubbed model. Tests: `ontology_test.py` (7).
- **2026-09-16** — **Tavily wired in properly, under a 100-requests-per-minute budget.** New `backend/app/services/evidence/tavily.py`: one client for every Tavily call with a rolling 60 s limiter (`TAVILY_RPM`, default 95) that paces rather than drops — the 96th request in a minute waits until the oldest leaves the window — plus a single `Retry-After` retry on 429 before the engine is benched. Search engine rewritten: `site:` → `include_domains` (filter mode), `advanced` depth for domain-scoped statistics hunts, 3 chunks per source as the snippet, `country` from the plan's region, published dates. Page reader gains a Tavily Extract tier (advanced depth for tables, reranked to the query) between plain httpx and headless Chromium, also on 429/5xx/timeouts. Crawl, Map and the async Research endpoint are documented in the module but not wired. Tests: limiter pacing and FIFO under concurrency, payload shaping, engine wiring, 429 handling, extract fallback ordering.
- **2026-09-16** — **Web search survives datacenter IP blocking: Claude web search joins the engine chain.** A Population Studio build's statistics gathering lost every query (DuckDuckGo 202 bot-challenge, Yahoo 500, Brave 429, Bing "no results" padding dropped by the plausibility gate). New `anthropic` engine (`engines._anthropic`): Claude's server-side `web_search` tool (`web_search_20250305` on Haiku 4.5, `web_search_20260209` on the 4.6+ family), one `model_fast` call per query with `max_uses: 1` and a `user_location` from the region; hits come from the `web_search_tool_result` block, snippets from the model's JSON reply; tool errors bench the engine like any other. Ordered after Brave API/Tavily and before the keyless engines whenever `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set (`SEARCH_DISABLE=anthropic` opts out); it counts as a keyed engine, so the research loop's query gap drops to 0.5 s. Bing's `b_no` block is now recognised (empty result, not unrelated pages). `anthropic` SDK pinned 0.40.0 → 0.125.0 (server tools need it; the local suite already ran on 0.125.0). Tests in `evidence_test.py` (parser, error benching, tool type by model, chain order, engine call shape).
- **2026-09-15** — **Location is a first-class persona driver (§7.3, §7.4, §7.8, §4).** Where an agent lives now shapes both its dials and its voice, not just its card. Both LLM spawn paths (classic Pro `generate_agents` and the Studio's `_plan_prompt`) share a new `_GEO_INSTRUCTIONS` block: every persona gets a real, specific `region`; the persona writer must **re-read the query through that place and shift the dials to how the topic lands there** (price sensitivity vs local incomes, trust vs the local mood toward institutions, habit/friction vs local infrastructure and routines, identity vs local values — two similar personas in different places must diverge); and it writes a per-persona **`geo_behavior`** paragraph — the query analysed from that place: local attitudes, habits, prices and reference points, and the culture's way of voicing agreement or disagreement. At run time `agent_runner._geo_block` injects that paragraph into every persona system prompt (posts, chat, probes) under a *"HOW YOUR PLACE SHAPES YOU ({region})"* header with a stay-local directive (vocabulary, currency, units, examples); an agent with a region but no paragraph (older rosters) gets a generic version, and one with neither is unchanged. `region`/`geo_behavior` live in the existing `demographics` JSON (no migration); classic Pro spawns now persist `demographics` too. The Fast seed bank stays location-free (topic-agnostic by design). Frontend: `AgentDemographics.geo_behavior` typed; cards render only the keys they already showed.
- **2026-09-16** — **Statistics gathering rebuilt around the audience: a UK-first publisher catalogue, config-aware target planning, triage, a relevance judge and re-fire (§7.8).** Following the September 2026 source review ("Quantitative UK Data Architecture for Synthetic Population Simulation"), `QUANT_SOURCES` grows from 11 to 31 publishers, each carrying `covers` (population dimensions), `phrasing` (how it titles pages), `fit` (1–5 readability for an automated reader), an optional narrower `site:` prefix and an extraction `note`: ONS, Nomis, gov.uk statistics, Scotland's Census, NISRA, FCA Financial Lives, Ofcom, More in Common, Opinium, Focaldata, Savanta, Ipsos UK, YouGov, Edelman, NHS England, Sport England Active Lives, SMMT, BRC, London Datastore, Understanding Society, data.gov.uk, US Census, Pew, Gallup, Eurostat, OECD, World Bank, Our World in Data; **Statista becomes opt-in** (its teasers hide the number) and the UK defaults become the review's default set (ONS, Nomis, gov.uk, FCA, Ofcom, More in Common, Opinium, plus the devolved office or London Datastore when the geography names them). The **query planner** (`plan_quant_targets`, replacing `decompose_quant_query`) no longer splits the question and fires it blindly: it reads the analyst's configuration first — dials as set, dials already derived from research, the audience profile, the uploaded survey — then the detected population and its gaps, then the facts on file and the brief, then the ticked publishers' coverage and phrasing, and returns fact **targets about the audience** (dimension, fact, why, priority, 1–2 publisher-phrased queries routed to keys) plus a `skipped` list of dimensions the configuration already settles. **Gathering** (`sources.gather_target` / `gather_targets`) routes each query to the ticked publishers covering its dimension (`route_sources`, best readability first), **triages** search results before reading (statistical releases and tables in, press releases and policy pages out; `heuristic_rank` fallback), extracts facts **against the target** with two new judgements — `answers_target` and `relevance` 0–100, so a page about the wrong group or country is kept greyed (`on_topic` needs relevance ≥ 30) and `Evidence.relevance` carries the score — and **re-fires** when a round finds no answer: a refine call proposes another route (the survey's proper name, the publisher's region vocabulary, a broader geography, other publishers) for up to `QUANT_MAX_ROUNDS` (2) rounds; targets run in priority order sharing one page budget (`QUANT_MAX_PAGES` 16, `QUANT_RESULTS_PER_SOURCE` 5, `QUANT_PAGES_PER_QUERY` 3; `QUANT_PAGES_PER_SOURCE` retired). After gathering, the build **re-reads the dials against the new facts** (`DIALS_FROM_FACTS_SCHEMA` → `apply_detected_dials`) — detect ran before they existed. The ad-hoc Sources-panel search uses the same planner (against the latest build's constraints and detected population) and loop; a keyword query still runs verbatim, one round. The Sources panel groups publisher chips by region, orders them by fit with a readability dot, and dims fit ≤ 2. **Gathering reads as automatic** (same day, after the panel was seen presenting a manual step): the checkbox says "Gather automatically when you press Detect & plan", a status line follows the build (detecting → gathering with pages read so far → N pages with usable facts, or a warning when gathering is switched off), the lookup box is no longer prefilled with the session question, and the ad-hoc search is folded into a collapsed "Look something up yourself (optional)" disclosure; the panel receives `buildStatus` for this. An **untouched publisher selection now follows the geography** (`sources.quant_auto`): the panel's defaults used to be fixed at page load, before detection, so a UK question searched OECD / OWID / World Bank; the build re-derives the set from what detect found (`default_sources` now whole-word, and it recognises UK regions and cities in the question itself), saves it on the build and logs it. Tests: `population_test.py` (catalogue integrity, routing, prompt ordering, heuristic rank, planner fallback, ad-hoc planning against the build's config, a stubbed end-to-end `gather_target` that re-fires and one that stops after one round, and budget sharing by priority).
- **2026-09-15** — **Statistics gathering decomposes the question into fact targets (§7.8).** A prod build's Sources-panel search fired the raw session question ("Would London commuters switch to a £65/month e-bike subscription (theft cover and 24-hour…") verbatim at every ticked publisher with `site:` and got zero results everywhere — publishers index dataset titles, not questions. Now: the ad-hoc Search decomposes a question-shaped query into 2–5 per-publisher fact-target queries (`decompose_quant_query`, logged as "Fact target: …", page budget shared across them; terse keyword queries still run verbatim); the build's gather stage logs its fact targets and its failure fallback is a mechanical `keyword_squeeze` (content words only — no prices, parentheticals or question scaffolding) instead of `question[:80]`; the analyst's own `quant_query` is squeezed too when it reads like a question; the query-writing prompt now teaches per-publisher phrasing (dataset noun phrases for statistics offices, topic+survey for polling houses, market phrases for Statista) and per-target source routing; the build runs up to 4 targets (was 3). Tests: `population_test.py` (squeeze, question detection, ad-hoc decomposition + source-routing fallback, verbatim keyword pass-through).
- **2026-09-15** — **Structured LLM calls retry on truncation instead of failing (§7.8, §11a.2).** A prod Studio build repeatedly died at "Planning failed — LlmTruncated: population_plan: output hit max_tokens=6000": a rich detect (long groups-implied analysis + a survey panel) makes the plan JSON verbose, and `analyze()` treated a `max_tokens` overrun as fatal on the first try. `services/evidence/llm.analyze` now retries once with a doubled budget (capped at 16,000) before raising `LlmTruncated`, which heals every structured call in one place — the Studio's detect/questions/plan/segment, the evidence brief, the judges, theme coding and probe answers. The plan call's base budget also rose 6,000 → 9,000 so the first attempt normally lands. Same failure class as the 2026-09 verdict-sidebar truncation fix.
- **2026-09-15** — **Research runs can no longer run away or ignore Stop (§10a).** A prod run gathered for ~70 minutes against its 420 s budget (a step hung where no budget check runs) and Stop was a silent no-op once the status said "stopping" — `stop_research` only matched `queued|running`, and after a restart the in-memory task/flag maps are empty so nothing could ever land the row. Now every external await in the loop is time-bounded with graceful fallbacks (search/judges/frame/plan/brief/recommendations), the gathering loops sit under a hard wall-clock cap (`RESEARCH_HARD_CAP_SECONDS`), a second Stop press hard-cancels the task, Stop on a run whose task died finalises the row directly, a new start finalises (not "stopping") an old run whose task is gone, and a run whose row was deleted mid-flight stands down at its next save. The stopping toast now says "Press Stop again to force-stop". Tests: `evidence_test.py` (orphan-row stop, double-press force-cancel; 149 passing).
- **2026-09-15** — **Realistic debate voices: the segment's register IS the humanity dial, and posts stop reading like fifty analyst essays (§7.7, §7.8).** The first Studio prod run (50 agents, Bristol e-bikes) produced uniformly "expert" output: every post a 742–1618-char cost-benefit mini-essay, four agents citing "the knowledge graph" verbatim, and the whole population stuck at humanity 0 or 50 because the global dial capped intensity while the per-segment `humanity_hint` could only nudge coverage. Now, in the Studio path, **each segment's register sets its agents' humanity band directly** (`HINT_HUMANITY_BANDS`: expert 0–15, tempered 25–45, balanced 50–58, defensive 60–68, reactive 72–90; the persona writer varies the value per person, `finalise_segment_dicts` clamps it into the band), so a "reject-subscriptions-entirely" segment can actually be defensive/reactive; the planner is instructed to set the register from who the group honestly is (ordinary consumers are rarely "expert"); the analyst reviews it as a chip on every segment card and can change it in Edit (`humanity_hint` now merges through `decide_segment`); the Studio's global Humanity/Coverage sliders are gone (legacy constraint keys ignored; the classic Agents-page spawn keeps its own sliders). Post generation: KG context is framed as the agent's **own knowledge** with band-scaled usage (no more "the knowledge graph says"), each post draws a random band-appropriate length directive with band-scaled `max_tokens` (reactive 150 → expert 600) so threads mix one-liners with paragraphs, the `direct` stance gloss no longer calls every direct agent "a domain expert", and the balanced band may commit to a side instead of every agent being identically torn. Also: **startup recovery for background jobs** (`core/recovery.py`) — a redeploy kills in-process research runs and Studio builds; on startup, rows still claiming to run are marked `interrupted`/`stopped` with an honest log line (a stopped build with a plan stays approvable), ending the eternal "Research is finishing" spinner; and cross-segment duplicate repairs now run concurrently with a Studio log note. Tests: `population_test.py` (register bands + clamping, plan prompt pins the register, startup recovery; 147 passing).
- **2026-09-15** — **Studio, after the first production run (§7.8).** Stop now means "plan with what we have" (and keeps the agents already written when stopping a build); Approve / Re-plan / segment decisions work on a stopped build. Detect proposes the demographic and sentiment dials from the research and marks them *from research* (an analyst's move always wins). Segments are written concurrently (a 50-agent, 8-segment build ran ~1.5 min per segment sequentially). Quant evidence no longer borrows `run_id` for the build id (Postgres FK violation on the first prod page); valueless facts (`<redacted>`) are dropped. The page's three columns scroll independently and stack below wide screens; the research status on the page follows stop/finish events and polls while research runs.
- **2026-09-15** — **The Population Studio: build the population from evidence and statistics, with the analyst in the loop (§7.8, §6.9, §12.2, §12.3, §13.2, §13.3).** A new page reached only from the Agents tab. It **detects** what the session's inputs establish about who the population is (with sources and a confidence figure), **gathers base rates** from statistics publishers — Statista, ONS, gov.uk, YouGov, US Census, Pew, Gallup, Eurostat, OECD, World Bank, Our World in Data — as typed, quoted facts (`quant` evidence, `trust_tier=high`, chunked into the graph with provenance), **asks clarifying questions** it cannot infer (answer or skip), and **proposes the population as segments** with share, count, stance, age range, gender split, regions, income, education, occupations, mood, temperature, arguments, evidence and the rationale. The analyst **accepts, edits or rejects each segment with a reason** (a rejection regenerates that slot from the reason), moves the **dials** — demographics (age range/skew, gender, regions, settlement, income, education), sentiment (follow the evidence or a for/against target, temperature, institutional trust, price sensitivity, tech comfort, openness), stance mix, humanity — and **re-plans**; only an explicit **Approve** writes agents, segment by segment on Haiku (Fast) or Sonnet (Pro) via `agent_factory.generate_agents_from_plan`. Every step streams to a build log (`population_log` / `population_build`), and a reload restores it. Agents gain `segment` + `demographics` (new columns; Alembic `0006_population`, SQLite `_ensure_columns`), which reach the persona prompt (`_demographics_line`), the Lab's split keys (`segments_for`), the agent cards and presets. New model `PopulationBuild`; new router `population.py`; new services `population/sources.py`, `population/builder.py`. Tests: `population_test.py` (share/count normalisation, prompt assembly, quant chunking, and an HTTP round trip start → questions → answer → plan → reject/edit → approve with the model stubbed).
- **2026-09-15** — **Lab picker tidied; past runs fold away and can be deleted (§13.3, §12.2).** Tool cards now carry an icon, their KPI chips and how many times they have been run, in a three-column grid on wide screens with an "Open" cue on hover. Past runs sit under a collapsible header with a count (remembered per browser) instead of an ever-growing list; each row shows its tool, one-line result, answered/agents (or the variants), how long ago, and a status pill when it did not complete. Rows delete from a hover trash icon with an inline confirm. New `DELETE /sessions/{id}/probes/{probe_id}` and `DELETE /sessions/{id}/experiments/{experiment_id}` remove the run and every answer under it (an experiment takes its arms); both refuse with 409 while the run is in flight, and a probe that is an arm of an A/B test refuses too, so a comparison is never left pointing at a missing arm. `api.ts` `request()` now returns `undefined` on a 204 instead of trying to parse an empty body. Test: `measurement_test.py::test_http_delete_probe_removes_its_answers_and_refuses_what_it_should`.
- **2026-09-14** — **The Survey tool: build a form, every persona fills it in, results like a form tool but with intervals and click-through (§11a.3).** Replaces Ask in the picker (Ask lives on as the *Reaction* template and as an A/B base). A form is any number of questions — single/multiple choice, scale, yes/no, number, open text, grid — with templates (Reaction, Email test, Concept test, NPS + why, Consequences), a primary question, reordering and a respondent preview.
