One call per persona for the whole form; the schema is generated from the questions; open text is theme-coded per question. Results: **Summary** (one card per question, drawn for its type — donut, option bars, histogram with the mean, Likert stacked rows, theme chips — plus the primary question's headline and segment splits), **Question** (one question, every response), **Individual** (one persona's completed form). Clicking any bar, slice, segment or theme lists the personas behind it and what they wrote. New declaration hooks: `Instrument.form` (a bespoke builder in the frontend form registry), `Instrument.validate`, theme coding by arbitrary field. Tests: `survey_test.py` (8); 138 passing overall.
- **2026-09-14** — **Fixed a request storm from the Lab tab (§13.3).** Production HTTP logs showed ~900 requests a minute from one browser — `GET …/probes`, `…/experiments` and `…/probes/{id}` over and over — which is what the user saw as "server error". Cause: the session page passed the Lab an inline `onClearLive` callback (a new function every render) and the Lab's completion effects listed it as a dependency *and called it*; the call re-rendered the page, which minted a new callback, which re-ran the effect, which fetched again. Present since the Lab shipped, triggered once any probe completed. Fix: the page's callback is a `useCallback` that is a no-op when there is nothing to clear, and both Lab panels read it through a ref so their effects run once per completion tick. Verified locally: 10 requests in the 30 s after a probe completed (the page's own 8 s poll), against thousands before.
- **2026-09-14** — **The generic tool: Ask, coded themes, and a choose-between-them design (§11a.3, §11a.7).** Adding an instrument per question type does not scale, and purchase intent was the wrong base for anything without a price (a walk-away price on a tariff measured nothing). Now one generic instrument, **Ask**, covers "which email lands", "does this poster work", "what would happen if…": fixed schema (verdict yes/no/mixed, strength, feeling, a free-text key factor), free-text **question** written by the analyst with suggested phrasings as chips, and the reasons **coded into ≤7 population-level themes** by one cheap call after the run (`themes.py`), written back onto the answers so the driver mix, flips-by-reason and CSV work as they do for an enum. Ask declares metrics (`verdict` primary, `open_to` = yes+mixed, strength, feeling), so the A/B test works over it unchanged, coding both arms in one pass. A third comparison design, **Choose between**: every agent sees all options at once and picks a winner and a runner-up (one call per agent; hidden `choice` instrument with an enum built from the option keys) — preference race with intervals, settled-or-not, rankings, why each option won, by segment. Instruments may now declare `stimulus_key`, `question_from`, `postprocess`, `hidden` and input `suggestions`; the prompt carries the question as `THE QUESTION:` and the fingerprint includes it. Tests: new `ask_test.py` (10); 130 passing overall.
- **2026-09-14** — **A/B test redesigned to read as a comparison (§11a.7).** The first cut opened with two purchase-intent headline cards side by side, so an A/B result looked like two probes. Now: while running, one pill per agent split per arm with an amber ring on anyone who has already changed their mind, plus "answered both" and "changed their answer" counters; results lead with the verdict and a single big delta (`DeltaHero`), then every metric's lift, then a **flow diagram** of how the same agents moved between answers (new `flips.matrix` in the results, stayers included), the movers' words, the segment heat-map — and the arms demoted to one compact line each at the bottom. Verdict caveats now name the segment ("direct and neutral stance", not "direct, neutral"). Verified in the UI against the stubbed model.
- **2026-09-14** — **Behaviour Lab, instrument #2: the A/B test (§11a.7).** Built as the plan describes it — *the same probe run against two stimuli on the same agents* — as a layer over the probe primitive rather than a special instrument, so it works for every tool that declares what to compare. An `Instrument` now declares `metrics` (per-answer numbers, one primary), a `decision_key` and a `driver_key`; purchase intent declares would-buy (primary), likelihood, walk-away price and feeling. New `experiments` table (Alembic **`0005_experiments`**, RLS via session) and `services/measurement/experiment.py`: **within-subjects** (every agent answers every variant; the runner now hides sibling arms from the agent's prior-answer history so B is answered with no memory of A; paired lift) and **between-subjects** (seeded disjoint split; new `stats.unpaired_lift`). Arms run concurrently sharing one probe's concurrency budget. Results per variant vs control: lift on every metric with a bootstrap interval, **who flipped** with both reasonings and coded reasons (driver mix), a **segment heat-map** of the primary lift (thin buckets flagged), and a plain-language **verdict** that names the winner, the margin, the interval, the driver and where the effect does not hold — or says plainly that the interval includes zero. A/B/n (2–6 variants) uses the same machinery. API: `POST/GET /sessions/{id}/experiments[/estimate|/{id}|/{id}/stop|/{id}/export.csv]`; WS `experiment_started` / `experiment_complete`; `GET /lab/instruments` now carries `metrics`, `supports_experiments`, `decision_key`. UI: an **A/B test** card in the Lab picker (shown whenever an instrument is testable), `ExperimentPanel` (base tool, design, a variant editor rendering the base tool's own form per variant, estimate as `agents × variants = calls`), one live dot grid per arm, `ExperimentPage` (verdict, arms side by side, `LiftBar` per metric, flips with a toggle to see what they told the control, `HeatCell` map), CSV with every arm side by side plus `flipped`; past runs list experiments and hide arm probes. Verified end to end in the UI against a stubbed model (12 agents, £34.99 vs £8-first-month: verdict, arms, lifts, 7 flips, heat-map, past runs). Tests: new `experiment_test.py` (14 — declarations, unpaired lift, seeded split, paired analysis/flips/segments/verdict, A/B/n, empty arm, two end-to-end runs, HTTP round trip incl. CSV, validation); 119 passing overall (the 4 pre-existing bare-async failures are unrelated).
- **2026-09-11** — **Survey curation measured before/after on a real 25-agent Pro spawn.** Same query, same survey, same evidence brief. **Panel fidelity: 5 of 24 respondents represented → 19 of 24**, and invented non-panel personas fell from 16 of 25 to 4 of 25. Nineteen agents now match a specific respondent's occupation *and* age exactly (R003 hospitality supervisor 29, R016 semi-retired GP 61, R024 logistics coordinator 40, …). **Stance stopped collapsing**: the before-run returned 22 direct / 3 indirect / 0 neutral while claiming to honour a 33/33/34 quota; mirroring derives stance per person and returned 9 / 6 / 10. **Duplicates: 8 redundant personas → 2.** Staged batching is visible in the token counts — batch 1 sends 5,037 input tokens, batches 2 and 3 send 5,310 each, the difference being the "already taken" roster. Two further fixes came out of the run: `role_head` now splits on `/` (Pro writes roles as "Job / Descriptor", so two retired police sergeants *both aged 49* had read as different people), and the remaining pair — two print shop owners fifteen years apart — is deliberately kept, since the age window is what separates a clone from a coincidence. Also observed, not yet fixed: a backend restart mid-spawn silently loses the in-flight population (`BackgroundTasks` are in-process) and the UI sits on "Finishing…" indefinitely.
- **2026-09-11** — **Lab architecture: every tool owns its own inputs, KPIs and page; plus survey-curation fixes (§11a.3, §11a.4a).** Restructured before instrument #2 rather than after #5. An `Instrument` now declares `inputs` (typed `InputField`s, with `default_from: "session_query"` so the analyst edits rather than retypes), `kpis` and a `page`; `GET /lab/instruments` publishes all of it, and required inputs are validated from the declaration so the API has no opinion about stimuli or prices. The frontend `LabPanel` became a **shell** — tool picker, run/stop, streaming, cost, CSV, provenance — with `InstrumentForm` rendering a tool's controls from its declaration and `lab/pages/` mapping each tool to its own results page (`PurchaseIntentPage`), falling back to `GenericPage` so a backend-only instrument is usable before its UI exists. Nothing instrument-specific remains in the shell. The shared "stimulus + price" form and the hardcoded purchase-intent results component are gone. **Persona curation:** concurrent persona batches share one prompt and cannot see each other, so a 22-agent spawn produced two identical 38-year-old urban mobility economists — duplicates are now detected by name *and* by role-at-the-same-age, the collided slots alone are regenerated in one extra call listing who is taken, with deterministic renaming as a fallback. **Survey fidelity:** a "Mirror the panel" option (default on for a Pro spawn with a doc attached) makes an uploaded survey authoritative — one agent per respondent, stance derived from each person rather than the sliders — because previously the stance quota won and a 24-respondent consumer panel was diluted with invented analysts. Survey truncation is now one shared 8,000-character limit that reports how many rows survived, instead of trimming at 12,000 client-side and 8,000 server-side in silence. Spawn task arguments are passed by keyword (a 12-argument positional call silently mis-binds the moment a parameter is inserted anywhere but the end). Tests: `measurement_test.py` 35, new `agent_factory_test.py` 8; 99 passing overall.
- **2026-09-11** — **Behaviour Lab verified end to end in production, and three defects found and fixed.** Ran a real purchase-intent probe against a 10-agent population (HomeChef Weekly, $34.99/month meal kit): 10 answers in ~13 s, 10 LLM calls for 10 agents (confirmed in Pulse), live dot grid, aggregates, demand curve, segment splits, verbatims and CSV export all correct. **Answer quality is the headline result** — agents stayed consistent with the positions they argued in the debate and reasoned from their own circumstances, not from their dials: the anti-subscription influencer refused partly because buying would make him "a walking contradiction" against his own public advocacy; the former Head of Growth refused because she "watched it deteriorate from the inside"; the churned UX researcher cited her own documented reasons for quitting; and the 14-month subscriber said yes **while setting her walk-away price at $31.99 against a $34.99 ask** — which the contradiction detector flagged, and which her reasoning explains as "probably paying a loyalty tax". Fixes: **(1)** the cost estimate priced the *mode label* (Fast = Haiku) rather than the resolved model — production overrides `MODEL_AGENTS` to Sonnet, so a "Fast" probe was quoted ~12x under; it now prices the real model id, prices an unknown model at the top tier, and the UI shows the model under the estimate with the tier buttons relabelled plain Fast/Pro (§11a.5). **(2)** the answer field `max_price_gbp` hardcoded GBP while the instrument already priced in USD/EUR — renamed to `max_price` (schema **v2**), with v1 answers still aggregating through a fallback read. **(3)** the "balanced" humanity directive handed the model the literal example sentence *"part of me feels…, but rationally…"*, and **every** balanced-band agent opened with it — in probe answers *and* in debate posts; the stock phrase is gone and the instruction now asks for the same tension in the agent's own words. Regression tests added for all three (`measurement_test.py`, now 30).
- **2026-09-11** — **Fixed Alembic migrations silently failing on Postgres (§15.4).** The Behaviour Lab's `0004_measurement` never applied in production: the app runs Alembic over **asyncpg**, which executes through prepared statements and accepts **exactly one command**, so `op.execute(UPGRADE_SQL)` on a multi-statement script died with *"cannot insert multiple commands into a prepared statement"* — leaving `probes` / `probe_answers` missing while the deploy looked healthy (the API routes answered, `/health` said `database_ok: true`, and the only trace was a printed startup warning). This had been latent since `0001`: migrations `0001`–`0003` were applied to the Supabase project **by hand** and the project was stamped, so `0004` was the first to actually run through Alembic. Added `app/core/migrations.run_script()` — a quote-, dollar-quote- and comment-aware splitter (a `;` inside a string literal or a `do $$ … $$` body does not split) that executes each command separately — and moved **all four** migrations onto it, so a fresh database now provisions correctly too. `GET /health` gained **`schema_ok` / `schema_error`** and reports `degraded` when the startup migration failed, so this class of failure can never look healthy again. New `backend/tests/migrations_test.py` (14 tests): splitter edge cases, every shipped script splits into single commands (idempotence, not a naive semicolon check), any multi-statement script must use `run_script`, and `/health` degrades on a failed migration.
- **2026-09-11** — **Behaviour Lab, phase 1: the probe primitive and the first instrument (§11a).** The population is now a measurement instrument, not just a debate. A **probe** asks every agent one structured question once (Claude tool-use, so the answer is always valid typed JSON), stores it, and computes everything else in pure Python. Personas stay themselves while answering: the probe reuses the agent's own system prompt via the new `_build_system_prompt(agent, task="probe")` — which swaps the Reddit-post instruction for a **humanity-band-specific answering directive** (reactive agents answer from the gut, experts deliberate) — and shows each agent the topic + ranked KG/brief context, **its own last posts**, **its own earlier probe answers** (so decisions stay consistent across instruments), and its **commercial dials explicitly labelled priors, not the answer**. Every schema puts `reasoning` first (reason before number), answers are framed as private (nothing to perform), and an agent whose call fails is **dropped from the denominator rather than defaulted**. First instrument: **purchase intent** — decision, likelihood, the agent's own walk-away price, key driver and sentiment; aggregated into a share with a **Wilson interval**, a **demand curve and revenue-optimal price** derived from the walk-away prices (which is what will make the price slider instant later), the share whose own price clears the asking price plus a **contradiction count** where that disagrees with what they said, driver mix, segment splits and verbatims — with one plain-language sentence the report generator can quote. New: `services/measurement/{probe,stats}.py` + `instruments/` (registry — a new instrument is one file plus one line), tables `probes` / `probe_answers` (Alembic **`0004_measurement`**, RLS via session owner), API `GET /lab/instruments`, `POST /sessions/{id}/probes[/estimate]`, `GET /sessions/{id}/probes[/{id}][/export.csv]`, `POST …/stop`, WS events `probe_started` / `probe_answer` / `probe_complete`, and a new **Lab tab** (instrument picker, stimulus + price editor, segment filter, cost estimate before running, a live dot grid of the population answering, then charts as inline SVG with error bars everywhere). Seed, model, schema id and prompt hash are stored on every probe, and the agent sample is seeded, so a re-run is reproducible. Tests: `backend/tests/measurement_test.py` (24 — statistics, persona context assembly, filters, a full run with a stubbed model, and the HTTP round trip including CSV).
- **2026-09-10** — Magic-link / reset emails were landing on `localhost:3000` from production: the frontend sends the right `emailRedirectTo`, but the Supabase project's Site URL was still localhost and the production origin was not in the Redirect URLs allow-list, so Supabase silently fell back (visible in auth logs as `referer: http://localhost:3000` for requests from other networks). Documented the real frontend origin: **`https://populations.11-minds.com`** (Railway service `amiable-courage`; `11-population.up.railway.app` is its Railway alias). No code change.
- **2026-09-09** — **Model-output hardening after a production crash.** A research brief came back with `groups` as pseudo-XML text and `key_facts` as a string (the model hit `max_tokens` mid-structure); the panel called `.map` on it and the whole session page died with "Application error". Backend: `evidence/llm.analyze` now raises `LlmTruncated` when `stop_reason == max_tokens` (the brief retries once on the top half of the evidence, budget raised to 8k tokens) and every structured answer passes through `llm.coerce(schema, value)`, which forces the schema's shapes (recovers `<item><parameter …>` blocks into objects, strings → lists, `"35%"` → 35, unknown enum → first value, missing keys → empty). Stored briefs are normalised on the way out too (`normalise_brief`), so old rows render. Frontend: `ErrorBoundary` around every session tab and the research panel (a bad payload shows an inline notice, never a blank app) and the panel treats every model field defensively (`asList`/`asStrList`/`asNum`).
- **2026-09-09** — Research Stop is immediate and visible: the loop checks the stop flag between page reads and comment fetches (not only between queries), the API emits `research_status` (`stopping`, then `finalising` while the brief is built) so the panel shows progress and disables the button, and a stopped run still produces the brief and recommendations from everything gathered (test: `test_stop_mid_run_still_builds_brief`). The tool recommender no longer crashes on non-object entries in the model's list.
- **2026-09-09** — Prod DB fix + honest health: the documented pooler host was wrong (`aws-0-eu-west-1` → **`aws-1-eu-west-1`**); the backend had been starting with a failed schema step and every DB request 500'd, which the browser showed as "Couldn't reach the server" because unhandled 500s carried no CORS headers. `/health` now runs `SELECT 1` and reports `database_ok` / `database_error`; unhandled exceptions return a JSON 500 through the middleware stack (CORS headers attached).
- **2026-09-09** — Login: added forgot-password / set-new-password flow and a clearer wrong-password message. Documented that Supabase's Site URL + Redirect URLs allow-list must include the production origin or emailed links land on localhost.
- **2026-09-09** — **Auto-research: web search + Reddit listening ported from SuperMind (§10a).** New session → frame → plan → web engine chain with benching and a plausibility gate → page reading (trafilatura, PDF skim, Chromium fallback) → coverage judge with refinement (≤3 rounds) ‖ Reddit JSON reader (browser fallback when Reddit 403s plain HTTP) → social judge → comments (≤6 attempts / 25 on-topic) → evidence brief (stakeholder groups, stances, quotes, gaps) → tool recommendations with pre-filled specs. Tables `research_runs`, `research_queries`, `evidence` (Alembic `0003_evidence`, applied). Everything streams live (`research_*` WS events) into the new **Research** tab in Ingest (checklist, queries, verdicts, budget, Stop, add sub-question, evidence feed with exclude, brief, recommendations). Only on-topic evidence enters the graph, every chunk now carries a `[SOURCE …]` provenance header, LLM Search papers are labelled synthetic, graph context is relevance-ranked instead of insertion-ordered, and the brief is prepended to every agent's context; Pro spawning takes the brief with grounding rules (`ground_in_evidence`). Backend image switched to the Playwright Python image (Python 3.12 + Chromium). Keyless engine order measured and set to DuckDuckGo → Bing → Yahoo → Brave. Tests: `backend/tests/evidence_test.py` (parsers, gate/benching, Reddit filters, judges, ranking, full loop on SQLite with mocked network/LLM).
- **2026-09-09** — **Runtime auth config + roles.** The frontend fetches the Supabase project from the backend's new public `GET /config` at runtime (build-time `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` still take precedence), so a deployment built without those variables now shows the sign-in page instead of "not configured". Added `profiles.role` (Alembic `0002_profile_roles`, applied to the project): **the first account to register is the admin**; admins bypass ownership, can list everyone's sessions (`?scope=all`) and manage roles on the new `/admin` page (`GET /admin/users`, `PATCH /admin/users/{id}/role`, last-admin protection). `GET /me` returns the caller's role. Fixed the first Postgres deploy: `alembic/` was excluded by `.dockerignore`, and the Supabase URL env value is now sanitised.
- **2026-09-09** — **Proper database + user accounts.** The app now persists to the **"11 Minds Population" Supabase project** (Postgres, eu-west-1): the full schema (sessions, agents, posts, reports, presets, `kg_graphs`, `profiles` + trigger, enums, indexes, RLS owner policies) was applied there and versioned as Alembic `0001_initial`, which the backend runs at startup. **Knowledge graphs moved from disk files into `kg_graphs`** (legacy `kg.json` files are imported once if found), so a redeploy no longer destroys anything. **Supabase Auth** added end to end: `/login` page (password, sign-up, magic link), `AuthProvider`/`RequireAuth` gate, bearer token on every API call and on the WebSocket; backend `app/core/auth.py` verifies ES256 tokens against the project JWKS (HS256 fallback via the Auth server), every route is owner-scoped (`user_id` on sessions and presets; children via the session), foreign resources 404, sockets close 4401/4404. Auth and Postgres switch on via env vars (`DATABASE_URL`, `APP_SUPABASE_URL`, `APP_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*`); without them the app runs as before (SQLite, no login), so the deploy is safe before the variables are set. The landing page's "no database" warning is gone. New tests: `backend/tests/auth_test.py` (token verification, per-user isolation, WebSocket rejection, DB-backed KG round-trip).
- **2026-09-09** — **Fixed the Agent Opinions sidebar stuck on "summarising…" forever.** Root cause (proved from Pulse `execution_logs`): `POST /sessions/{id}/opinions` asked Haiku for one JSON object keyed by 36-char UUIDs for *every* agent in a single call capped at `max_tokens=1200`; all 9 production calls ended at exactly 1200 completion tokens, so the JSON was always truncated, `json.loads` always failed, and the endpoint returned `{"opinions": {}}` with HTTP 200 — which the frontend treated as "still summarising". Rebuilt as `services/simulation/opinions.py`: agents are **batched (25/call, ≤6 concurrent)** with short **integer keys**, `max_tokens` scales with the batch, **truncated output is salvaged** pair-by-pair, LLM errors return a friendly `error` string (never a 500 or a silent empty map), and verdicts are **persisted** on the new `spawned_agents.verdict` column (exposed on `GET .../agents`). Frontend: verdicts seed from the roster on load, generation retries 3× with backoff, the sidebar shows "summarising…" only while a request is in flight, falls back to a first-post excerpt otherwise, and has a **Refresh/Retry** button with the error text. Added `backend/tests/opinions_test.py` (pytest, mocked Claude + SQLite).
- **2026-06-10** — **Fixed the knowledge graph staying sparse on Pro/small runs.** The mid-simulation KG enrichment used a flat 15% post sample (tuned for 1000-agent Fast runs), which on a small Pro run (5–50 agents) rounded to ~0 posts — so the debate never reached the graph. Made it **adaptive**: `min(1.0, KG_SIM_MAX_UPDATES / agent_count)`, so any run ≤120 agents now feeds **every** post into the KG and only large runs throttle. Replaces `KG_SIM_SAMPLE` with `KG_SIM_MAX_UPDATES` (default 120).
- **2026-06-10** — **Fixed the "Generate Report" button doing nothing on failure.** `handleMakeReport` swallowed thrown errors (only `console.error`), so a 404 (e.g. a wiped session) left the button spinning then blank. It now surfaces the reason in the report panel — and a "Session not found" 404 shows a clear "this session was reset (no persistent storage) — start a new one" message. (LLM errors already returned a friendly HTTP 200 that rendered; only the thrown-error path was silent.)
- **2026-06-10** — **Added a "no database" notice to the landing page.** The deployment runs without persistent storage (ephemeral SQLite, no volume), so sessions vanish on restart/redeploy and stale ones 404 with "Session not found". The landing page now warns users up-front to treat each session as temporary. (Durable fix remains: add a Railway Postgres plugin + a volume for `LIGHTRAG_DATA_DIR` — see §15.4.)
- **2026-06-10** — **Onboarded the project to AI Agent Pulse monitoring (direct-to-Supabase, no n8n).** Added `app/core/monitoring.py`: every Anthropic call now logs its **exact** token usage (`response.usage`, not an estimate) straight into the Pulse `execution_logs` Supabase table — `tracked_messages_create` wraps the 9 async calls, `record_usage_sync` covers the 2 Vision paths. Cost is computed by the table's trigger from `llm_pricing`. Fully no-op unless `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`/`PULSE_PROJECT_ID` are set; fire-and-forget so it never blocks or breaks a request. See §12.4.
- **2026-06-10** — **Stopped the live thread from hijacking navigation.** `post_created` no longer force-switches the user to the Thread tab on every post (you can now browse Agents/Graph/Report while the debate streams). ThreadView auto-scroll now **sticks to the bottom only when you're already there** — scrolling up to read is no longer yanked back down — with a floating **"Jump to latest"** button when scrolled away.
- **2026-06-10** — **Made the Humanity dial graduated & far more impactful.** Replaced the old binary humanity thresholds (`≥40` human, `≥66` intense) with a **5-band sentiment-vs-logic scale** (`agent_runner._humanity_band`): expert `[0,20)`, **tempered** `[20,50)` (logic leads, feeling colors), **balanced** `[50,60)` (50/50), **defensive** `[60,70)` (feelings decide, logic defends them at any cost), **reactive** `[70,100]` (pure gut — ignores logic, just judges/reacts, 1–2 lines). Each band has its own hard directive, so the dial now visibly changes behavior as it moves. The credibility/citation rule is gated to `<50`; Fast-bank `_humanize` grades the spawned dials by band; the Agents page shows a live band chip under the slider.
- **2026-06-10** — **Scaled to 1000 agents + added Fast/Pro modes + replaced rounds with an intensity ladder.**
  - **Fast vs Pro modes** everywhere. **Fast** (default) samples a new **pre-built, procedurally-generated agent bank** (`seed_bank.py` → committed `app/data/agent_bank.json`, ~500 diverse personas with full 112 dials) — spawning is **instant, no LLM, no API key** (verified: **1000 agents in ~0.4 s**). **Pro** curates personas *and* runs the debate on **Claude Sonnet** (`MODEL_PRO_*`) for far richer output; the UI warns about the time/cost, especially past 150 agents.
  - **Agent count raised from 50 → 1000** (UI slider + number input + quick-pick chips; backend clamps to 1000). Spawn now **bulk-inserts** the population in one transaction and streams it in **batched `agents_spawned_batch` events** (~40/event), replacing the old per-agent commit + 0.3 s sleep + per-agent event (which made big spawns take minutes and overflowed the WS queue). Pro batch generation is bounded by `SPAWN_CONCURRENCY`.
  - **Simulation rewritten as a coverage-guaranteed phase engine.** "Max rounds" → **activity intensity**: L1 = one post per agent (guaranteed), L2 +1 reaction, L3 +1 debate, L4 +1 reply, then more. Each phase runs every agent concurrently bounded by `SIM_CONCURRENCY_FAST` (48) / `SIM_CONCURRENCY_PRO` (12). An in-memory **status watcher** replaces thousands of per-agent DB status reads; KG-from-post enrichment is **sampled (15%) + concurrency-bounded (6)** so it never saturates the API mid-run.
  - WS subscriber queue raised to 4000; thread/roster/opinion rendering capped for 1000-scale (latest 400 posts, 60 cards/stance, top 150 opinions). New config: `MODEL_PRO_ORCHESTRATION`, `MODEL_PRO_AGENTS`, `SPAWN_CONCURRENCY`, `SIM_CONCURRENCY_FAST`, `SIM_CONCURRENCY_PRO`, `KG_SIM_CONCURRENCY`, `KG_SIM_SAMPLE`. *(Note: live debate posts still require a valid `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; the configured key currently returns 401, so the post-generation path could not be verified end-to-end — Fast spawn and the engine's control flow were.)*
- **2026-06-07** — Fixed **large-population spawning**: agent generation is now **batched** (~10 personas/call, run concurrently) so big counts no longer overflow `max_tokens` and truncate the JSON (which caused "Unterminated string" spawn errors). Added a salvage parser (`_parse_agents_json`) that recovers complete agents from partial output, and a clear error if generation fully fails.
- **2026-06-07** — Hardened **report & agent chat**: LLM failures now return a friendly message (HTTP 200) via `friendly_llm_error` instead of a 500; the frontend API client got a 120 s timeout with clear network/timeout errors (no more silent "Failed to fetch"/endless spinner); assistant chat replies now render markdown instead of raw text.
- **2026-06-07** — Fixed **YouTube transcript extraction**: forced the `android` yt-dlp player client (`_with_client`) on all calls because YouTube's default client returned zero captions/formats (transcripts were silently empty); bumped `yt-dlp` to `2025.10.14`.
- **2026-06-07** — Added **Humanity & Coverage** spawn-time controls (§7.7). A coverage% subset of the population now reasons emotionally (gut-driven, low-citation) instead of like experts; sentiment is the primary driver of agent voice; dial generation is pushed for greater diversity; agent cards surface a "% human" badge + dominant-emotion chips. Adds `SpawnedAgent.humanity` (with a non-destructive startup migration) and `humanity`/`humanity_coverage` params on `POST /spawn-agents`.
- **2026-06-07** — Removed the landing hero tagline; hardened KG ingestion (extraction failures now logged, not swallowed) and added `backend/tests/kg_ingestion_test.py`.
- **2026-06-07** — Initial blueprint authored.

---

*End of blueprint. For the precise behavior of any subsystem, the files above are authoritative; this document summarizes them as of 2026-10-08.*
